# The people below the programme map

None of the 137 nodes on the programme map is a person, so every one of the board's 311 people sits below it: witnesses, officials, legislators, contractors, scientists and journalists. This file takes the 120 with the most connections, most connected first, and gives what the board records about each of them and up to eight of their claims.

## David Charles Grusch

David Charles Grusch is a person on the board; board tier A, named in sworn testimony or primary documents as directly inside the programme; dates 2023-07-26; axes C2 A3 D3; 67 connections.

In one line: Former NGA/NRO officer; ICIG complainant; the proximate cause of the UAPDA.

### The longer account

Grusch is the hinge of the modern disclosure record: the first person holding a current TS//SCI clearance with CI and lifestyle polygraph and a UAP Task Force billet to state under oath that the United States runs a multi-decade UAP crash-retrieval and reverse-engineering effort concealed from Congress. His resume is documentary, not anecdotal. The House Oversight Committee published his biography: intelligence officer 2009-2023; 21st Operations Group Space Control Intelligence Cell; 3d Space Experimentation Squadron; Chief, Intelligence Integration Division, Space Security and Defense Program 2013-2016; Senior Intelligence Officer at the National Reconnaissance Office 2016-2021 on the Director's briefing staff coordinating the President's Daily Brief; then GS-15 Senior GEOINT Capabilities Requirements Officer at the National Geospatial-Intelligence Agency from November 2021 to April 2023. He was NRO's representative to the UAP Task Force 2019-2021 and NGA's co-lead for UAP and trans-medium object analysis from late 2021 into 2022. He testified that in 2019 the UAP Task Force Director tasked him to identify every special access program and controlled access program needed to satisfy the Task Force's congressionally mandated mission; that he was then informed in the course of official duties of a multi-decade crash-retrieval and reverse-engineering program; and that he was denied the read-ons when he requested them. That denial - a cleared officer legally tasked to inventory SAPs being refused - is the structural fact this board turns on. He disclosed to the DoD Inspector General in July 2021 and filed a PPD-19 urgent-concern complaint with the Intelligence Community Inspector General on 25 May 2022 through counsel Charles McCullough III, the IC's first Inspector General; ICIG found it credible and urgent in July 2022. DoD's own Prepublication and Security Review cleared his statements for open publication on 4 and 6 April 2023 - the Pentagon's security apparatus signed off on the words the Pentagon's press office later called unsubstantiated. He went public through Leslie Kean and Ralph Blumenthal in The Debrief on 5 June 2023, on NewsNation with Ross Coulthart days later, and under oath before House Oversight on 26 July 2023, where he stated he had interviewed over 40 witnesses over four years, that biologics came with some recoveries and were nonhuman, that he has personal knowledge of people harmed in the concealment effort, and that the programs run on misappropriation of funds and contractor IR&D self-funding. He has since suffered documented reprisal - clearance revocation, denied promotion, loss of medical retirement per Rep. Eli Crane's 24 July 2025 letter to DoD - and the FOIA leak of his 2014 and 2018 emergency-custody police records to The Intercept in August 2023, which he litigated and lost. Since 27 March 2025 he has been Special Advisor to Rep. Eric Burlison and a fixture of the House Task Force on the Declassification of Federal Secrets, appearing at the 9 June 2026 Capitol press conference where he alleged slush funds of billions of dollars per annum and said the government is aware of several kinds of non-human intelligence.

### What is left unexplained

A GS-15 with PDB-level access was formally tasked to enumerate all SAPs and CAPs bearing on a congressionally mandated mission - and was refused. No official account has ever explained what he was refused access to, or under whose authority the refusal was issued.

### Where the record conflicts

- AARO's Historical Record Report Volume I (8 March 2024) states alleged reverse-engineering programs do not exist, are misidentified authentic national security programs, or were disestablished, and that none of AARO's interviewees had firsthand knowledge - directly contradicting Grusch's sworn account of over 40 witnesses, some with firsthand knowledge who filed their own protected disclosures.
- AARO's memorandum for record documents nine contacts between June 2023 and January 2024 seeking to interview Grusch, including a 14 November 2023 Arlington appointment he did not attend. Grusch cites legal and security exposure; AARO uses the non-appearance as its central impeachment.
- The Intercept's 9 August 2023 publication of his 2014 and 2018 emergency-custody records is treated by Grusch and by members of Congress as a targeted smear; the underlying records are authentic county documents, and a Virginia court held their release lawful.
- AARO says Grusch refused to cooperate; Grusch's position is that he had already lawfully routed his concerns through the ICIG and that AARO was not an independent venue.

### Recorded as unverified

- The name of the multi-decade program he was denied read-on to has never been placed on the public record.
- The cooperative and hostile witness list of specific individuals he offered Rep. Mace after the 26 July 2023 hearing has never been made public.
- His statement that he has personal knowledge of people harmed, and his deflection on murder, remain untested outside classified channels.
- Whether any of the roughly 40 witnesses have been deposed by any committee, in open or closed session.
- The specific contractors Grusch identified to the Inspectors General and in closed session.
- The dollar scale of the alleged IR&D diversion.
- Whether AARO ever offered a venue with ICIG or congressional counsel present.
- The direct NewsNation URL for the 9 June 2026 report (cited here via the Wikipedia footnote).
- Whether Grusch has himself used the NDA-waiver channel established by ODNI ES 2026-00818.

Affiliations recorded: National Geospatial-Intelligence Agency, National Reconnaissance Office, UAP Task Force (NGA representative), US Air Force / Air National Guard / Reserve, Major (2009-2023), 21st Operations Group, Space Control Intelligence Cell (2009-2011), 3d Space Experimentation Squadron (2011-2013), Space Security & Defense Program (2013-2016), National Reconnaissance Office (2016-2021), UAP Task Force, NRO representative (2019-2021), National Geospatial-Intelligence Agency (2021-2023), The Sol Foundation, COO (2023- ), Office of Rep. Eric Burlison, Special Advisor (2025- ), US Air Force (2009-2019), National Reconnaissance Office, Senior Intelligence Officer (2016-2021), National Geospatial-Intelligence Agency, GS-15 (2021-2023), Office of Rep. Eric Burlison, formerly National Geospatial-Intelligence Agency, formerly National Reconnaissance Office.

## Luis D. Elizondo

Luis D. Elizondo is a person on the board; board tier A, named in sworn testimony or primary documents as directly inside the programme; dates 2017-10-04; axes C2 A3 D3; 38 connections.

In one line: Former OUSD(I) counterintelligence officer; states he ran AATIP 2010-2017.

### The longer account

Elizondo is the witness who put the contractor divestment attempt on the congressional record under oath. On 13 November 2024, before the House Oversight Committee, Rep. Eric Burlison asked him whether defense contractors possess technology. Elizondo answered that his predecessor program AAWSAP had tried to collect 'material of unknown origin,' that 'there was an aerospace contract company that requested to divest itself of the material... that was collected in the 1950s,' and that the transfer did not occur. Burlison then read to him the reporting of journalist Christopher Sharp describing a transfer between Lockheed Martin, Bigelow Aerospace and a CIA block; Elizondo replied 'What I can say is that it was blocked,' said he had later been told by contract personnel that the account was accurate, and explained the bureaucratic mechanism: the transfer required a memorandum from the Secretary of the Air Force that never came, after which he attempted to obtain one from Secretary of Defense James Mattis instead. Asked who possesses the material today, he refused to answer in open session. In the same hearing, Rep. Nancy Mace asked whether material is allocated to contractors by domain expertise - undersea propulsion to a General Dynamics, aerospace to Lockheed - and Elizondo replied 'Yes, ma'am, absolutely correct. Different contractors have different levels of expertise.' That exchange is the clearest description in any public record of how a distributed contractor custody architecture would function. Elizondo's statements are pre-publication-reviewed by DoD's Office of Prepublication and Security Review, a point he made in the hearing itself.

### What is left unexplained

If Elizondo truly had no assigned AATIP duties, no one at OUSD(I) has explained why he was the officer who submitted the DD-1910 for the AATIP-associated video files, or why his supervisor's evaluation described a classified portfolio of overstated-to-understate national-security importance.

### Where the record conflicts

- DoD says Elizondo never led AATIP; Elizondo says he did. DoD has never produced the AATIP charter or an org chart that would settle it.
- The New York Times reported his resignation letter was addressed to Defense Secretary Jim Mattis and dated 4 October; FOIA 18-F-0324 shows two letters, a 3 October letter through his chain and a 4 October memorandum for record saluted 'Mr. Secretary'. Garry Reid's internal memo separately questioned the second letter's 'uncertain provenance'.
- Pentagon spokespeople have said since June 2019 that he had no assigned AATIP responsibilities; Elizondo, TTSA, Senator Harry Reid and his own 2016 evaluation language point the other way. The Department has never reconciled the two positions.
- Elizondo was an officer of To The Stars Academy, a private company that itself acquired and held alleged exotic material and contracted with the US Army.
- The Department has issued inconsistent characterisations of Elizondo's AATIP role over time while simultaneously clearing his book for open publication.
- Elizondo's May 2025 disc image was publicly identified by analysts as an irrigation circle; he has not published a retraction in the sources reviewed.

### Recorded as unverified

- Any document naming Elizondo in an AATIP role has not been released; DIA FOIA searches for Elizondo correspondence pertaining to AATIP returned no records.
- That Pentagon spokeswoman Dana White confirmed in December 2017 that Elizondo led AATIP. This is widely repeated but no primary transcript or release has been located; the documented 2017 confirmation is that DoD Public Affairs verified he was an OUSD(I) staff member.
- The identity of the 'Defense Department official' who handed the three video files to Christopher Mellon in the Pentagon parking lot.
- The dates and nature of the Elizondo-Grusch working relationship at US Space Force.
- Which specific Lockheed facility in California is alleged to have held the material.
- Whether the CIA officer said to have blocked the transfer is the individual named separately by George Knapp.
- Whether 'Reckoning' cleared DOPSR review and on what terms.
- Whether Elizondo has used or been offered the ODNI NDA-waiver channel.

Affiliations recorded: US Army counterintelligence, Office of the Under Secretary of Defense for Intelligence (2008-2017), To The Stars Academy (2017-2020), Author, 'Imminent' (2024), U.S. Army (1990s), U.S. Government civilian intelligence officer / Special Agent (1998-2017), ONCIX (2000s), ODNI (2000s), OUSD(I) (2008-2017), AATIP (c.2010-2017, disputed), To The Stars Academy of Arts & Science (2017-2020), Author, Imminent (2024) and Reckoning (2026), Office of the Under Secretary of Defense for Intelligence, AATIP, program lead (to 2017), US Army counterintelligence (1996-2008), Office of the Under Secretary of Defense for Intelligence, AATIP (2010-2017), To The Stars Academy of Arts and Science (2017-2020), OUSD(I), counterintelligence (to 2017), AATIP (per his account), formerly Advanced Aerospace Threat Identification Program, HarperCollins/Mudlark.

### Why it holds its tier

Re-graded A on a global pass (rank 2 of 1057, score 196). 43 connections, 20 of them custody/witness/funding/programmatic, 7 official denials recorded against it. Axes C2/A3/D3.

Source: DD Form 1910 released via FOIA, published by Blue Blurry Lines, 17 August 2019

## Eric Burlison

Eric Burlison is a person on the board; board tier A, named in sworn testimony or primary documents as directly inside the programme; dates 2026-05-22; axes C1 A2 D3; 27 connections.

In one line: U.S. Rep. (R-MO-7); Oversight subcommittee chair; runs the FFRDC/contractor records investigation.

### The longer account

Burlison is the member who took the disclosure fight off the witness table and onto the contractors. He arrived at the July 2023 hearing an announced skeptic - 'I have been here for 6 months and I am pretty skeptical... I am from Missouri. You have got to show me' - and used his round to test whether the recovered material could be domestic, which Grusch denied. He then radicalised on the process rather than the phenomenon. In March 2024 he led a bipartisan call for a House select committee on UAP focused first on the money, and said flatly that DoD lied to members in a classified setting: 'They weren't transparent to us in a SCIF setting. So they could have brought forward evidence. They could have explained what these Special Access Programs actually do. But they are not willing to do that with Congress.' In March 2025 he hired David Grusch as a senior adviser on UAP matters for a four-month term - the whistleblower moving inside the legislative branch. At the 9 September 2025 hearing he publicly unveiled, for the first time, footage he says shows an MQ-9 firing a Hellfire missile at an orb off Yemen on 30 October 2024, and put two further stonewalls on the record: that his UAP disclosure amendment to the NDAA had been ruled non-germane 'mostly deemed by staff, not even an elected official,' and that in 2024 'we were blocked by someone in House administration from being able to receive a full briefing from AARO.' In 2026 he turned the investigation onto the FFRDC layer, writing MIT Lincoln Laboratory in May 2026 for a classified 1952 'flying saucer talk' briefing recording (their attorneys agreed to comply within 30 days) and MITRE on 22 May 2026 with a ten-page demand covering over forty production categories and records back to 1930. His stated theory: 'If you really want to hide something from Congress, you don't put it in a government file cabinet. You hand it to a private contractor. That's why my investigation is following the trail into RAND, MITRE, Aerospace Corp, MIT Lincoln Labs, and the Northrop Grummans of the world.' ## Anna Paulina Luna

Anna Paulina Luna is a person on the board; board tier A, named in sworn testimony or primary documents as directly inside the programme; dates 2025-09-09; axes C0 A2 D3; 26 connections.

In one line: U.S. Rep. (R-FL-13); Chairwoman, Task Force on the Declassification of Federal Secrets.

### The longer account

Luna is the member who converted House UAP interest into a standing institutional instrument. She was waived onto the July 2023 subcommittee and used her rounds to establish two things on the record that the committee has pursued ever since: that Grusch had received DoD Prepublication and Security Review approval for what he was saying, and that he feared for his life. On 11 February 2025 Oversight Chairman James Comer created the Task Force on the Declassification of Federal Secrets and put her in the chair; she and Comer sent letters to Secretary of State Marco Rubio, Secretary of Defense Pete Hegseth and CIA Director John Ratcliffe demanding a briefing on all UAP records in their possession. On 9 September 2025 she chaired the third House UAP hearing and used her opening statement to document the concealment architecture in plain language - that DoD told committee staff that under special access program rules only members of the House Armed Services Committee and the Defense Subcommittee of Appropriations could be read in, and that any other member needed the approval of the chairman and ranking member of both. That is the mechanism: an Oversight committee with subpoena power told it lacks the clearance jurisdiction to see what it is investigating. In the same statement she called former AARO director Sean Kirkpatrick 'a documented liar' and read Christopher Mellon's assessment of AARO's historical report into the record. On 1 April 2026 she wrote Secretary Hegseth demanding AARO's unreleased UAP video files after whistleblowers told the Task Force they exist; the Department blew the deadline in mid-April 2026 and she publicly raised the subpoena. On 9 June 2026 she called on the White House to grant immunity to whistleblowers willing to name locations of recovered craft and technology.

### Where the record conflicts

- Luna spent over a year demanding AARO be abolished and then endorsed its director within weeks of receiving requested video; the reversal is documented but its terms are not.

### Recorded as unverified

- Whether the Task Force has obtained the witness list Grusch offered Rep. Mace in July 2023.
- Whether any written record of Kosloski's apology exists.
- The text and addressee of Luna's April 2026 letter that Release 02 is said to answer; it has not been published.

## Eric W. Davis

Eric W. Davis is a person on the board; board tier A, named in sworn testimony or primary documents as directly inside the programme; dates 2010-01-01; axes C2 A3 D3; 20 connections.

In one line: Astrophysicist, EarthTech International; five-time AAWSAP DIRD author.

### The longer account

Davis is the single most important technical witness on this board because his key act is documented rather than merely asserted. He is an astrophysicist who worked under Puthoff at EarthTech International and, as the cover page of DIA-08-1004-004 states in plain type, authored 'Traversable Wormholes, Stargates, and Negative Energy' for the DIA Defense Warning Office's Advanced Aerospace Weapon System Applications programme - dated 6 April 2010, produced in FY2009, with James T. Lacatski, D.Eng., named as AAWSA programme manager and the return address given as Bldg 6000, Washington DC 20340-5100. He co-authored a second DIRD, 'Warp Drive, Dark Energy, and the Manipulation of Extra Dimensions', with Richard Obousy. He was a subcontractor and then consultant to the Pentagon UFO programme from 2007. On 23 July 2020 The New York Times reported, on the record, that Davis - by then at The Aerospace Corporation - had given a classified briefing to a Defense Department agency as recently as March 2020 about retrievals from 'off-world vehicles not made on this earth', and that he had given classified briefings on retrievals of unexplained objects to staff members of the Senate Armed Services Committee on 21 October 2019 and to members of the Senate Intelligence Committee two days later. Of the materials he said: 'We couldn't make it ourselves.' Those are dated events with named committees, and they are the reason the phrase 'off-world vehicles' entered the congressional record's ambient vocabulary. Davis is also the author of record of the notes of an alleged October 2002 meeting with Vice Adm. Thomas R. Wilson, which surfaced in 2019 among the papers of the late Edgar Mitchell and describe a special access programme concealed from a sitting DIA director.

### What is left unexplained

Neither DoD nor AARO has ever addressed the Wilson-Davis notes in a published report, including in AARO's 2024 Historical Record Report Volume I.

### Where the record conflicts

- Thomas Wilson denies the meeting took place and denies the substance attributed to him.
- Davis confirmed on the record on 8 March 2026 that he wrote the notes and that they are '100% accurate', while declining to confirm or deny that the meeting took place; Adm. Wilson has denied that the meeting occurred. The author endorsing his own notes is not authentication, and the other named party still disputes them. No government body has authenticated the memo.
- Sceptical commentators characterise the DIRD corpus as speculative literature reviews of little intelligence value; Steven Aftergood of FAS said the AAWSAP contract 'sounds like it was a good deal for the contractor' but that it would be hard to argue the military or the public got their money's worth.

### Recorded as unverified

- Whether AAWSAP funds paid for any Davis work beyond the five named DIRDs.
- The chain of custody by which the notes left Davis's possession and entered public circulation in 2019 has not been established on the record.
- The provenance chain of the Wilson-Davis memo pages between 2002 and 2019.
- Whether Davis was a party to the 2009-2010 negotiation AARO describes.
- NewsNation reported in August 2026 (Katie Pavlich Tonight) that Davis said he has 'seen the evidence' and 'seen the records' of recovered non-human bodies of 'a minimum of four' species and asked the White House for immunity before a fuller account. The NewsNation pages could not be opened from this environment (bot wall), so the statement is recorded here as unverified rather than carried as a claim.
- The identity of the Defense Department agency Davis briefed in March 2020.
- Whether any transcript, memorandum for record, or read-ahead of the 21 October 2019 SASC staff briefing exists in committee files.
- Davis's own account of the provenance of the materials he says 'we couldn't make ourselves'.

Affiliations recorded: National Institute for Discovery Science (1996-2004), EarthTech International (1996-present), Aerospace Corporation (2010s), AAWSAP/AATIP DIRD author (2008-2010), National Institute for Discovery Science (staff scientist, late 1990s-2004), EarthTech International / Institute for Advanced Studies at Austin, Bigelow Aerospace Advanced Space Studies (2008-2012), The Aerospace Corporation (2010s), Defense Intelligence Agency AAWSAP, DIRD author (2008-2010), The Aerospace Corporation (2019-2022), National Institute for Discovery Science (late 1990s-2000s), EarthTech International Inc. / Institute for Advanced Studies at Austin (c.1999- ), DIA AAWSAP/AATIP DIRD author (2008-2010), The Aerospace Corporation (by 2020).

## Harry M. Reid

Harry M. Reid is a person on the board; board tier A, named in sworn testimony or primary documents as directly inside the programme; dates 2009-06-24; axes C1 A3 D3; 17 connections.

In one line: US Senator (D-NV); Senate Majority Leader 2007-2015.

### The longer account

Reid is the appropriator who created the funded lineage and then tried to seal it. As Majority Leader he worked with Appropriations chairman Daniel Inouye and Ted Stevens in 2007 to direct roughly $22 million into the defense black budget for what became AAWSAP at DIA, AARO's own 2024 report states flatly that 'at the direction of Senate Majority Leader Harry Reid (D-NV), the Defense Appropriations Acts of Fiscal Years 2008 and 2010 appropriated $22 million for the DIA.' Reid took repeated DIA briefings on contract status: talking papers survive from January 2009, May 2009 and November 2009. On 24 June 2009 he wrote to Deputy Secretary of Defense William Lynn III requesting that portions of what he called the 'Advanced Aerospace Threat and Identification Program' be placed in a Restricted Special Access Program with a bigoted access list, protecting methodologies for identifying and engineering advanced technologies, personnel and oversight details, and the involvement of private contractors and industry partners. That letter is the most important single document in this domain because it establishes that the Senate Majority Leader believed the program was producing something warranting SAP protection with a compartmented access list. DoD refused. Reid later said publicly that $22 million was not enough and that failing to study the subject would be malpractice, and called the program one of the good things he did in his congressional service. He died 28 December 2021.

### Where the record conflicts

- Reid's letter names the program 'Advanced Aerospace Threat and Identification Program (AAITP)', DIA's contract names it AAWSAP, and DoD's later public position uses AATIP, the naming divergence originates here.

Affiliations recorded: US Senate (1987-2017), Senate Majority Leader (2007-2015), U.S. Senate, Nevada (1987-2017).

### Why it holds its tier

Re-graded A on a global pass (rank 37 of 1057, score 90). 17 connections, 6 of them custody/witness/funding/programmatic, 1 official denial recorded against it. Axes C1/A3/D3.

## Sean M. Kirkpatrick

Sean M. Kirkpatrick is a person on the board; board tier A, named in sworn testimony or primary documents as directly inside the programme; dates 2024-03-08; axes C2 A3 D1; 17 connections.

In one line: Founding Director, All-domain Anomaly Resolution Office, 2022-2023.

### The longer account

Kirkpatrick is the single most consequential figure in the containment architecture. A laser and materials physicist (BS 1991, PhD 1995, University of Georgia), he came to AARO through the exact channels that would matter: the National Reconnaissance Office and CIA Directorate of Science and Technology 2003-2007, then the Defense Intelligence Agency 2007-2016 as Chief Technology Officer, Division Chief and Defense Intelligence Officer for Scientific and Technical Intelligence, then deputy director of intelligence roles at US Strategic Command and US Space Command, with a stint on the National Security Council 2017-2018 as Director of National Security Strategy. He was named the first director of the All-domain Anomaly Resolution Office when Deputy Secretary of Defense Kathleen Hicks established it by memorandum on 15 July 2022. He testified before the Senate Armed Services Emerging Threats and Capabilities Subcommittee on 19 April 2023 that AARO held over 650 cases and that spherical objects made up nearly half of them. He then became the public face of the Department's response to David Grusch: within days of the 26 July 2023 House Oversight hearing he posted on LinkedIn that the hearing was 'insulting' to the workforce, prompting the White House to publicly distance itself from him. He announced his departure on 7 November 2023 and left on 1 December 2023, and immediately took the job of Chief Technology Officer for Defense and Intelligence Programs at Oak Ridge National Laboratory - a laboratory operated by UT-Battelle that had supported AARO during his tenure, and which in 2024 was given custody of the alleged UAP material sample AARO analysed. On 19 January 2024 he published an op-ed in Scientific American arguing the whole disclosure record was 'a textbook example of circular reporting' traceable to 'a small group of interconnected believers' seeded by Robert Bigelow and AATIP. He shaped, then supervised the rollout of, the Historical Record Report Volume I, released 8 March 2024 after he had already left. In September 2025 he told the New York Post that Rep. Anna Paulina Luna's oversight was 'playhouse politics' and that the congressional UAP hearings were 'a disgrace' and 'reality TV'; Luna's office called him 'a known documented liar' who 'blocked the release of information' and 'tried to discredit witnesses.' By May 2026 his own successor, Jon Kosloski, had - per Luna - apologised to members of Congress for the conduct of the prior AARO leadership.

### What is left unexplained

Kirkpatrick has never publicly denied that the April 2022 meeting occurred, nor explained why the concerns raised in it did not generate an AARO line of inquiry when he took the office three months later.

### Where the record conflicts

- AARO says the program was terminated for lack of merit; Lacatski, Kelleher and Knapp say it produced more than 100 detailed reports and was ended over institutional discomfort.
- Kirkpatrick's public position that AARO has seen no credible proof sits against the ICIG's credible-and-urgent determination on the same allegations, and against his own undisputed April 2022 classified meeting with the complainant.
- Kirkpatrick was simultaneously the investigator of the allegations and the official whose office would be implicated if they were true; he departed before publication and became a public advocate against the claimants.
- Kirkpatrick says whistleblowers refused to bring evidence to AARO; Grusch's position is that AARO was not a lawful or safe venue for information he had already routed through the ICIG under PPD-19.

### Recorded as unverified

- The substance of the April 2022 Kirkpatrick-Grusch conversation; whether Kirkpatrick documented it.
- The identities of the 'named companies' AARO visited.
- Whether AARO had subpoena or inspection authority over contractor holdings, or relied entirely on voluntary corporate statements.
- Whether ORNL's receipt of the AARO material sample in 2024 postdated or predated any recusal arrangement covering Kirkpatrick.
- Whether Kirkpatrick has any role in ORNL's UAP-related specimen work, and whether ORNL's AARO analyses fall within his defense and intelligence portfolio.

Affiliations recorded: Defense Intelligence Agency, chief scientist for the Defense Intelligence Directorate, All-domain Anomaly Resolution Office, founding Director (July 2022 - December 2023), Defense Intelligence Agency, chief scientist for defense intelligence, All-domain Anomaly Resolution Office, Director (July 2022 - December 2023), Defense Intelligence Agency, chief scientist, All-domain Anomaly Resolution Office, Director (2022-2023), Air Force Research Laboratory (1997-2003), CIA / NRO (2003-2007), Defense Intelligence Agency (2007-2016), National Security Council (2017-2018), US Space Command / USSTRATCOM (2016-2021), AARO, Director (2022-2023), Oak Ridge National Laboratory, CTO Defense & Intelligence (2023-present), Oak Ridge National Laboratory, formerly All-domain Anomaly Resolution Office, formerly Defense Intelligence Agency.

## George Knapp

George Knapp is a person on the board; board tier A, named in sworn testimony or primary documents as directly inside the programme; dates 2021-08-01; axes C1 A1 D3; 16 connections.

In one line: Investigative journalist, KLAS-TV Las Vegas; co-author of the AAWSAP insider books.

### The longer account

Knapp converted forty years of Nevada-desert sourcing into sworn congressional testimony on 9 September 2025, and in doing so put the contractor-custody allegation into the Congressional Record under his own name. Testifying before the House Oversight Task Force on the Declassification of Federal Secrets, he told the panel that Robert Bigelow and a colleague from the AAWSAP program 'met with and negotiated with senior executives at Lockheed Martin and hammered out a deal wherein Bigelow's company, BAASS, would receive a quantity of unusual material that had been stashed away and protected at a facility in California,' adding flatly: 'That material was not made here.' Asked directly by Rep. Tim Burchett which corporations hold the material, Knapp answered 'one of them is Lockheed,' declined to treat the company as villainous - 'they are doing what they were asked to do' - and offered the committee a list. Pressed by Rep. Nick Begich to name individual gatekeepers, he named Dr. James Ryder at Lockheed and, separately, Glenn Gaffney of CIA. He also placed on the record his interviews with Alfred O'Donnell, first general manager of EG&G in Nevada, and with Senators Harry Reid and Howard Cannon. Knapp's 1989 Bob Lazar reporting, which he re-affirmed in the same testimony, is the origin point of the modern S-4 reverse-engineering narrative. He is not a cleared insider and does not claim to be; his value is that he is a named journalist who has now sworn to his sourcing before Congress, converting decades of tier-3 anecdote into tier-2 record.

### What is left unexplained

A sworn witness told a congressional task force that reprisal against Grusch was occurring in the days before the hearing. Nobody on the dais asked him what he meant, and the record contains no follow-up.

### Where the record conflicts

- Knapp worked as a consultant to BAASS, the DIA contractor at the centre of the alleged Lockheed material transfer - he is reporting on an episode in which his own employer was a party.

### Recorded as unverified

- What the 'events in recent days' against Grusch in early September 2025 were; Knapp did not specify and no reporting has identified them.
- The identity and role of Dr. James Ryder within Lockheed Martin.
- The existence and current location of the 'list' Knapp offered to provide to Congress.
- The alleged O'Donnell deathbed recording, which has never surfaced.

Affiliations recorded: KLAS-TV Las Vegas, I-Team (1980s-present), Mystery Wire (2019-present), Co-author, 'Hunt for the Skinwalker' (2005) and 'Skinwalkers at the Pentagon' (2021), KLAS-TV Las Vegas, chief investigative reporter (1980s- ), Coast to Coast AM, Co-author, Skinwalkers at the Pentagon, KLAS-TV Channel 8 (1981-1991, 1995-present), National Institute of Discovery Science (late 1990s-2000s), Weaponized podcast (with Jeremy Corbell), KLAS-TV Las Vegas, investigative reporter (1980s-present), Coast to Coast AM, contributor, WEAPONIZED podcast, co-host with Jeremy Corbell, Bigelow Aerospace Advanced Space Studies / BAASS, consultant (2008-2010).

## Timothy Floyd Burchett

Timothy Floyd Burchett is a person on the board; board tier A, named in sworn testimony or primary documents as directly inside the programme; dates 2023-07-26; axes C0 A1 D3; 16 connections.

In one line: U.S. Rep. (R-TN-2); House Oversight; founder, Congressional UAP Caucus.

### The longer account

Burchett is the member who forced the modern UAP record into the open. He and Anna Paulina Luna drove the 26 July 2023 House Oversight National Security subcommittee hearing into existence; Ranking Member Robert Garcia thanked him from the dais for the leadership 'that has brought us to this place today,' and Jared Moskowitz did the same. Burchett opened that hearing by putting the first documented stonewall on the record: 'we were even denied access to a classified briefing in a SCIF prior to this hearing due to the amount of hoops that we had to jump through to grant temporary clearance to witness Grusch.' He then used his rounds to ask Grusch the questions no one else would - whether people had been harmed or murdered in the course of concealment (Grusch: 'Yes, personally,' and on murder, 'I have to be careful answering that question. I directed people with that knowledge to the appropriate authorities'), and what Special Access Programs cover the material and how they evaded oversight (Grusch: 'I do know the names'). Every one of those answers terminated at a SCIF the committee was not allowed into. He launched the bipartisan Congressional UAP Caucus in August 2023 and led a caucus letter to the Intelligence Community Inspector General; in January 2024 he brought roughly sixteen members to a closed ICIG briefing with Thomas Monheim and rated it a four out of ten - 'more of the same... it's very compartmentalized; it's like looking down the barrel of a .22 rifle.' He led the first push for a UAP select committee under Speaker McCarthy, which died with the speakership. He is the author of every standalone House UAP bill that matters: H.R. 8424 and H.R. 1187 (UAP Transparency Act) and H.R. 10111 and H.R. 5060 (UAP Whistleblower Protection Act). None have received a markup. His structural theory of the concealment is explicit and it is the theory this board is built on: 'You have to imagine Oak Ridge National Laboratory, the Manhattan Project, thousands of people working on the atomic bomb and less than 12 knew what it was.'

### Recorded as unverified

- Who denied the SCIF for the July 2023 hearing and under what authority.
- Whether Knapp's offered list was ever transmitted to the committee.
- The subject of the briefing Burchett described on 1 April 2026.

Affiliations recorded: U.S. House of Representatives, TN-2 (2019- ), House Committee on Oversight and Accountability / Government Reform (2019- ), Congressional UAP Caucus, founder (2023- ), Task Force on the Declassification of Federal Secrets (2025- ), Subcommittee on Delivering on Government Efficiency, Chairman (2025- ), US House of Representatives, TN-02 (2019- ), House Oversight Committee, Task Force on the Declassification of Federal Secrets, US House of Representatives (2019-present), House Committee on Oversight and Accountability, US House of Representatives (TN-2).

## 'UAP Gerb' (Sam)

'UAP Gerb' (Sam) is a person on the board; board tier A, named in sworn testimony or primary documents as directly inside the programme; dates 2026-08-07; axes C0 A0 D3; 16 connections.

### The longer account

UAP Gerb is a pseudonymous open-source investigator who has produced the most granular publicly available reconstruction of how the legacy recovery and reverse-engineering portfolio is organised - not as anecdote, but as org charts, office symbols, statutory authorities and named incumbents. His method is unusual in this field and is why he belongs on this board at tier A: each investigation is a multi-hour documentary built on cited primary documents, and he publishes those citations in the video description. His Battelle investigation (18 February 2024) links directly to the CUFON Project Stork progress reports, the CIA reading room copy of Special Report No. 14, a Black Vault release of the Second Progress Report on Titanium Alloys, AEC research reports at the University of North Texas digital library, and the Defense.gov transcript of the Moultrie roundtable. He runs a corporate-by-corporate series - Battelle, Northrop Grumman, TRW, Lockheed Martin, SAIC, Sandia - and an institution-by-institution series covering the Air Force, Navy, Army, NRO and now MITRE. On 7 August 2026 he published 'The MITRE Corporation - UFO Reverse Engineering, USO Tracking, & Legacy Programs', the named priority for this board, which argues that FFRDCs are the 'missing link' in the whole question: the mechanism by which the government can technically vest programs outside the government while retaining strict control of materials and data, and which arbitrates how much defense contractors are told. His two-part 'Manhattan Project 2.0' / 'Special Access Required' series (1 May and 19 June 2026) is the legal-mechanism argument: that the 1947 effort borrowed the Manhattan Project's compartmentalization wholesale, vested custody of recovered disks in AEC national laboratories, placed the control group under the National Security Council and its 5412 Committee, shielded it statutorily under the Atomic Energy Act of 1954, and that after a 1994 restructuring of the SAP enterprise the portfolio now hides inside 'Non-Covert Action Programs' with managed need-to-know access and waived reporting and budgetary requirements. He solicits first-hand witnesses at UAPGERB@protonmail.com. Ross Coulthart has platformed him repeatedly on NewsNation's Reality Check and said on air that he thinks Gerb is 'on the money'.

### The longer account

Puthoff is the hinge on which the whole technical layer turns. He is a Stanford electrical-engineering PhD (1967, under Richard Pantell) who spent the 1970s and early 1980s running SRI International's classified psychoenergetics work with Russell Targ - the SRI half of what became the Stargate Project - which is the formative fact about him: he has been the government's outsourced handler for phenomena the government does not want on its own org chart for more than fifty years. In 1985 he founded the Institute for Advanced Studies at Austin, incorporated under EarthTech International in 1991, at 11855 Research Blvd, Austin - the address that appears on the cover of at least one Defense Intelligence Reference Document. Under the DIA Defense Warning Office's Advanced Aerospace Weapon System Applications (AAWSA) programme in FY2009 he authored 'Advanced Space Propulsion Based on Vacuum (Spacetime Metric) Engineering', one of the 38 reference products the DIA listed to the Federation of American Scientists on 16 January 2019. He was not merely an author: the cover page of Christopher 'Kit' Green's DIRD on human injuries carries the imprint 'Institute for Advanced Studies at Austin' and its preface quotes a 'November 2007 Proposal to Analyze Probable ET Generated Specific Near-field Electromagnetic Emissions: to Puthoff/Hathaway' - meaning Puthoff was being solicited to organise medical reverse-engineering of putative extraterrestrial field effects nine months before the AAWSAP contract was awarded. In 2017 he co-founded To The Stars Academy with Tom DeLonge, Jim Semivan and Steve Justice, and his laboratories became the analytical destination for the ADAM Research Project's metamaterial samples. That is the custody problem in one man: the same physicist who tested Linda Moulton Howe's bismuth-magnesium laminate privately in the 1990s and 2000s, wrote a 2012 letter reporting no 'interesting/anomalous outcome', and then in 2019 sat inside the corporate entity that bought those same samples for $35,000 and put them under a five-year Army CRADA. He appeared as an interviewee in Dan Farah's 2025 documentary 'The Age of Disclosure'.

### Where the record conflicts

- CIA's own contracted evaluation (AIR, 1995) concluded the operational value of remote viewing was not established; the programme was terminated the same year.
- Puthoff's SRI remote-viewing results were challenged by Marks and Kammann, who found sensory cueing in session transcripts; his 1989 zero-point-energy inertia paper was found by Steve Carlip to contain a computational error. Neither bears on his documented AAWSAP authorship or his custody of samples, but both are used to discredit him wholesale.

- Whether EarthTech or IASA has held any classified contract or SAP read-on beyond the unclassified//FOUO DIRD work.

## John Greenewald Jr.

John Greenewald Jr. is a person on the board; board tier A, named in sworn testimony or primary documents as directly inside the programme; dates 2022-08-17; axes C0 A0 D3; 14 connections.

In one line: Founder, The Black Vault; FOIA requester of record on AATIP and Elizondo.

### The longer account

Greenewald is the private citizen who has done more than any institution to build the documentary base this board runs on. He began filing FOIA requests as a teenager in 1996 and has since obtained and published well over two million pages. Two of his releases are load-bearing for the 1970s-2000s era: DoD FOIA case 97-F-1677, processed by the Office of the Assistant Secretary of Defense in September 1997, which produced the Halt memorandum on Rendlesham and the November 1975 Malmstrom AFB overflight records; and his 2013 Mandatory Declassification Review of the entire NSA UFO holding, which forced further release of documents NSA had previously produced with only a few words visible per page. In 2025 he became the principal test of the Pentagon's own honesty about disinformation: after the Wall Street Journal reported that the Secretary of Defense's office had issued a spring 2023 memo ordering an end to an Air Force practice of falsely telling personnel they were on a reverse-engineering programme, Greenewald filed for the memo. OSD responded in September 2025 that no such record existed; AFOSI said it had no records of any such investigation; the Pentagon spokesperson said in October 2025 she could not confirm the memo existed. In December 2025 an OSD appellate authority granted his appeal and remanded for a new search. That sequence - press claim, government no-records finding, appeal, remand - is the mechanism by which this board's evidence is actually produced.

### Recorded as unverified

- Why the Department applied password protection to released PDFs, and whether it was a deliberate processing decision or an artefact of the export pipeline.

## Christopher C. 'Kit' Green

Christopher C. 'Kit' Green is a person on the board; board tier A, named in sworn testimony or primary documents as directly inside the programme; dates 2010-01-01; axes C2 A3 D2; 13 connections.

In one line: Neuroradiologist; former CIA analyst; AAWSAP DIRD author on field effects on biological tissue.

### The longer account

Green is the medical end of the reverse-engineering programme, and his released DIRD is one of the most revealing documents in the entire corpus because of what it takes for granted. A physician-neuroscientist who ran life-sciences analysis for CIA's Office of Scientific Intelligence before moving to General Motors and then Wayne State University, Green was the analyst to whom the intelligence community's biological anomalies were routed - including, in the 1970s, the SRI remote-viewing work. His AAWSAP paper, 'Clinical Medical Acute & Subacute Field Effects on Human Dermal & Neurological Tissues', was released publicly by Popular Mechanics on 14 February 2020, and its cover carries the imprint of Puthoff's Institute for Advanced Studies at Austin. Its preface quotes verbatim from a November 2007 proposal addressed 'to Puthoff/Hathaway' which states the intention plainly: 'certain characteristics of the fields associated with exotic propulsion can with near certainty be gleaned from archival records of their effects on human physiology.' Its Hypothesis One is that 'sufficient evidence exists from human injury / effects to reverse engineer certain aspects of the Energy / Propulsion Systems'. Its executive summary concludes that 'some advanced systems are already deployed, and opaque to full US Intelligence understanding'. It contains a Schuessler catalogue appendix, a 'Green-Morris Cases' appendix, and an ICD-diagnostics appendix built on 42 peer-reviewed cases plus roughly 300 unpublished ones, and it names the Cash-Landrum event. This is a US intelligence contractor document that treats humans injured by unidentified craft as an instrumented dataset for reverse engineering exotic propulsion.

### What is left unexplained

A named CIA scientific intelligence officer says he ran the anomalous-phenomena desk during the period the Agency says it had no such desk.

### Where the record conflicts

- CIA's own 1997 published history of its UFO role does not name Green or describe any post-1970s UFO portfolio.
- Green has given differing accounts over time of how much of the Wilson-Davis material he held and how it was released.

### Recorded as unverified

- The size and contents of Green's clinical case series; no primary medical record set has been released.
- The medical files Green is said to hold on anomalous-injury cases have never been released.
- The full contents of the Green document holdings.
- Any direct Green statement affirming contractor custody of material.
- The identity of the 'Sponsor and Affiliates' referenced in the DIRD's executive summary.
- The contents of the Green-Morris case files and whether the roughly 300 unpublished cases exist in a government-held database.
- Whether the follow-on continuation effort described in the DIRD (medical evaluation of 300-500 events) was ever funded.

Affiliations recorded: Central Intelligence Agency, Office of Scientific Intelligence (1969-1985), Wayne State University School of Medicine (1990s-present), National Institute for Discovery Science science advisory board, AAWSAP/AATIP DIRD author (2008-2010), Central Intelligence Agency, Office of Scientific Intelligence / Life Sciences Division (1969-1985), General Motors (1985-2000s), Wayne State University School of Medicine, Consultant, BAASS/AAWSAP-era human-effects work, Central Intelligence Agency, Directorate of Science and Technology (1969-1985), To The Stars Academy of Arts and Science, advisor, Central Intelligence Agency, Office of Scientific Intelligence, Life Sciences Division (1969-1985), Wayne State University School of Medicine, Departments of Diagnostic Radiology and Psychiatry & Behavioral Neurosciences, Institute for Advanced Studies at Austin / AAWSAP DIRD author (2009-2010).

## Susan Gough

Susan Gough is a person on the board; board tier A, named in sworn testimony or primary documents as directly inside the programme; dates 2019-06-13; axes C0 A1 D1; 13 connections.

In one line: DoD spokesperson for UAP matters, Office of the Assistant to the Secretary of Defense for Public Affairs.

### The longer account

Gough was, for roughly six years, the single voice through which the Department of Defense spoke on UAP, and the record of what she said is unusually complete because Elizondo wrote to her directly and those emails were released under FOIA 21-F-0938. On 13 June 2019 she told The Black Vault that Harry Reid's newly authenticated 2009 SAP-request memorandum 'makes no change to previous statements. Mr. Elizondo had no assigned responsibilities for AATIP while he was in OUSD(I).' On 6 December 2019 she went further and repudiated her own colleague, stating 'Neither AATIP nor AAWSAP were UAP related' and that 'when implemented, AATIP did not pursue research and investigation into unidentified aerial phenomena', explaining that Christopher Sherwood had merely been 'repeating the information that had been provided by a previous spokesperson some two years earlier' and that the earlier spokesperson 'is no longer with my organization.' On 21 May 2021 she partially reversed again, conceding that the AATIP contract 'allowed for research drawn from a wide variety of sources, including reports of UAPs' while insisting 'the examination of UAP observations was not the purpose of AATIP.' Elizondo wrote to her on 3 June 2020: 'You released a statement claiming I had no assigned duties in the AATIP program. The truth, as everyone involved in the program knows, is that I ran the program.' In his November 2024 written testimony he told Congress that the Pentagon's use of 'a professional psychological operations officer' as its primary UAP contact was 'unacceptable', a reference to Gough's career field.

### Where the record conflicts

- Her 6 December 2019 statement directly contradicts Christopher Sherwood's 22 May 2019 statement; her 21 May 2021 statement partially contradicts her own December 2019 statement.
- The Department's Gough-issued denials are bounded by 'AARO has not discovered', which is not a statement that no such programme exists.

### Recorded as unverified

- Her exact departure date from the UAP spokesperson role.
- What internal coordination produced the 7 June 2023 language, and whether AARO cleared it.

Affiliations recorded: Office of the Assistant to the Secretary of Defense for Public Affairs (2017-2023), U.S. Army Reserve / psychological operations, Office of the Assistant to the Secretary of Defense for Public Affairs, UAP spokesperson (c.2019-2024), Office of the Secretary of Defense, public affairs, Office of the Secretary of Defense, Public Affairs.

### Why it holds its tier

Source: Susan Gough to The Black Vault, 13 June 2019

## Christopher K. Mellon

Christopher K. Mellon is a person on the board; board tier A, named in sworn testimony or primary documents as directly inside the programme; dates 2017-12-16; axes C1 A3 D3; 11 connections.

In one line: Former Deputy Assistant Secretary of Defense for Intelligence; TTSA National Security Affairs Advisor.

### The longer account

Mellon is the strategist of the current cycle. He is Board Chair of the Disclosure Foundation, and the FY2026 NDAA's three UAP provisions - the NORAD/NORTHCOM intercept briefing requirement, the accounting for UAP security classification guides, and the streamlining of agency data-sharing with AARO - reflect recommendations from him and the Foundation. That is legislative drafting influence, not commentary, and the intercept provision in particular is the sharpest instrument in the current statutory record because it presumes and demands an operational history rather than a sighting inventory. On 20 February 2026, hours after the President's post, his assessment was 'This might be a consequential moment, but the impact will depend on the follow-through... The public's trust has been eroded and must be restored.' On 14 May 2026, six days after the first tranche, he delivered the judgement that has framed everything since: 'Data alone is not disclosure. Releasing raw files without context may confuse more than clarify.' Mellon is the figure who most clearly held both positions at once through 2026 - that the release programme is real and that it is not disclosure - and both of his 2026 statements have proved load-bearing on the record.

### Recorded as unverified

- The identity of the Defense Department official who handed him the files.
- The identities of the well-placed officials who briefed Mellon.
- Whether the NORAD/NORTHCOM intercept briefing required by the FY2026 NDAA has been delivered to Congress.

## Nancy Ruth Mace

Nancy Ruth Mace is a person on the board; board tier A, named in sworn testimony or primary documents as directly inside the programme; dates 2024-11-13; axes C0 A1 D2; 11 connections.

In one line: U.S. Rep. (R-SC-1); chaired the 13 Nov 2024 UAP hearing.

### The longer account

Mace chaired the hearing at which the Immaculate Constellation document entered the Congressional Record. As Chairwoman of the Cybersecurity, Information Technology and Government Innovation subcommittee she co-chaired the 13 November 2024 joint hearing with Grothman, and opened it by saying plainly that people had tried to stop it: 'I am not going to name names, but there are certain individuals who did not want this hearing to happen because they feared what might be disclosed. But we stood firm.' She read Karl Nell's 'nonhuman intelligence exists' statement and H.R. McMaster's remarks into the record, and then personally moved the 12-page Immaculate Constellation report - delivered to Congress that morning by Michael Shellenberger - into the record, noting Luna's warning that saying the name aloud would put her 'on some list. Maybe a FISA warrant. So, come at me bro, I guess.' At the 2023 hearing she had already put the core oversight question to Grusch directly: 'Do you believe that officials at the highest levels of our national security apparatus have unlawfully withheld information from Congress and subverted our oversight authority?' and drew from him the answer that non-human biologics came with some recoveries. She closed by asking which agencies and contractors should be called next - the question that Burlison's 2026 FFRDC letters eventually answered in practice. She is a cosponsor of H.R. 10111 and sits on the Task Force.

### What is left unexplained

A sworn witness offered Congress a named list of cooperative and hostile individuals inside an alleged illegal program. Three years later there is no public evidence the list was ever acted on.

### Recorded as unverified

- Whether Grusch delivered the witness list to Mace or the committee, and where it now resides.

Affiliations recorded: U.S. House of Representatives, SC-1 (2021- ), House Oversight Subcommittee on Cybersecurity, Information Technology and Government Innovation, Chairwoman (2023-2025), Task Force on the Declassification of Federal Secrets (2025- ), US House of Representatives, SC-01 (2021- ), House Oversight Committee, US House of Representatives (2021-present), House Committee on Oversight and Accountability.

## Robert T. Bigelow

Robert T. Bigelow is a person on the board; board tier A, named in sworn testimony or primary documents as directly inside the programme; dates 2008-09-22; axes C3 A2 D2; 11 connections.

### The longer account

Bigelow privatised the problem. A Las Vegas real-estate developer who built the Budget Suites of America extended-stay chain, he founded the National Institute for Discovery Science in 1995 as a privately funded research institute studying aerial phenomena, animal mutilations and related anomalies, and staffed it with people carrying federal clearances and federal programme histories - Hal Puthoff from the CIA/DIA remote-viewing programmes, Colonel John Alexander from Army INSCOM and Los Alamos, the biochemist Colm Kelleher, and Jacques Vallee. In 1996 he bought the 480-acre Sherman ranch in Uintah County, Utah - Skinwalker Ranch - as a permanent instrumented field site, requiring the sellers to sign non-disclosure conditions as part of the purchase. He told the Deseret News in August 1998, in his first interview on the subject, that the phenomena had continued and that 'we wouldn't be there just for the weather,' while declining to discuss specifics. NIDS operated a public reporting hotline and a veterinary necropsy response capability. He shut NIDS down in 2004 but kept the ranch. Three years later a senior Defense Intelligence Agency official wrote to him about Skinwalker, and that contact became the AAWSAP contract. Bigelow is therefore the mechanism by which a private billionaire's field programme was converted into a federally funded intelligence programme without changing the personnel - which is the single most important structural fact about how this subject was handled after Blue Book closed.

### Where the record conflicts

- NIDS published almost nothing peer-reviewed from its nine years of operation; its data set has never been made available for independent examination.
- Bigelow was simultaneously a DIA contractor, a private funder of UAP research, and the intended private recipient of government-connected material.

### Recorded as unverified

- The full amount BAASS itself spent above the government obligation; Bigelow has said he spent far more of his own money than the contract paid.
- The disposition of NIDS' raw instrument and necropsy data after the 2004 shutdown is unknown.
- Whether any material was ever physically delivered to Bigelow Aerospace.
- The identity of the 'facility in California.'

Affiliations recorded: Budget Suites of America (1988-present), National Institute for Discovery Science (1995-2004), Bigelow Aerospace (1999-present), Bigelow Aerospace Advanced Space Studies (2007-2012), Bigelow Aerospace Space Operations / BAASS successor efforts (2012-present), Budget Suites of America (founder), National Institute for Discovery Science (founder, 1995-2004), Bigelow Aerospace (founder, 1999), Bigelow Aerospace Advanced Space Studies (2008-2012), Bigelow Institute for Consciousness Studies (2020-), Budget Suites of America, founder, Bigelow Aerospace Advanced Space Studies / BAASS (2008-2010s).

## Jon T. Kosloski

Jon T. Kosloski is a person on the board; board tier A, named in sworn testimony or primary documents as directly inside the programme; dates 2024-11-19; axes C2 A3 D2; 10 connections.

In one line: Director, All-domain Anomaly Resolution Office (Aug 2024- ); former NSA physicist.

### The longer account

Kosloski is the third person to run AARO and the first whose tenure produced anything the disclosure side counts as movement. He came from the National Security Agency, where he was a senior research scientist working in quantum optics, cryptanalysis and advanced research; DoD announced his selection on 26 August 2024, ending nine months in which the office had no confirmed director. His public line is materially identical to Kirkpatrick's on the central question - 'to date, AARO has discovered no verifiable evidence of extraterrestrial beings, activity, or technology,' as he told the Senate Armed Services Emerging Threats and Capabilities Subcommittee on 19 November 2024 - but his framing diverges in two ways that matter. He has repeatedly conceded that a residue of cases is 'truly anomalous' and that there are cases he does not understand, and he has committed the office to declassification and data-sharing rather than pure debunking. At his 14 November 2024 media roundtable on the FY2024 report he disclosed the GREMLIN prototype sensor system undergoing a 90-day collection at a classified national security site, described AARO as employing several dozen personnel, and promised Volume II of the Historical Record Report 'within months' - a promise that as of August 2026 remains unkept. He signed off on the FY2025 Consolidated Annual Report, which reached Congress on 21 July 2026, months past the statutory cycle in 50 U.S.C. 3373(k), and which carries the most striking single case AARO has ever published: roughly 100 airborne UAP and two likely uncrewed surface systems observed by US Navy assets off the Virginia coast, still unresolved. In May 2026 he met Reps. Anna Paulina Luna and Tim Burchett and, per Luna, apologised for the conduct of the prior AARO leadership - after which Luna, who had spent more than a year demanding the office be defunded, publicly backed him. That apology is the closest thing to an institutional admission that the Kirkpatrick-era office behaved improperly toward Congress and witnesses.

### What is left unexplained

The FY2025 Virginia coast event - approximately 100 airborne UAP plus two likely uncrewed surface systems observed by Navy assets - remains open in AARO's own report.

### Where the record conflicts

- AARO's parallax reanalysis of GO FAST directly contradicts the operational reading that produced the January 2015 Fleet Forces Command 'URGENT SAFETY OF FLIGHT ISSUE' message which, per Gallaudet, carried that same video and cited 'multiple near-midair collisions.' AARO's analysis addresses the object's speed; it does not address why a fleet safety warning about it was recalled.
- Kosloski maintains no verifiable evidence of non-human technology while simultaneously conceding a residue of cases he cannot explain and an unresolved 100-object Navy event.

- Whether AARO was granted access to any waived or unacknowledged SAP holdings when producing its case assessments.

## Leslie Kean

Leslie Kean is a person on the board; board tier B, documented channel and capability, programme role indicated but not yet nailed to a primary source; dates 2023-06-05; axes C0 A1 D3; 10 connections.

In one line: Investigative journalist; co-author of the 5 June 2023 Debrief story.

### The longer account

Leslie Kean is the person in this domain who took the government to court and won a search. On 31 January 2003 she filed a FOIA request with NASA for records referring to Kecksburg, the incident of 9 December 1965, the 'Fragology Files' for 1962-1967, Richard M. Schulherr, Project Moon Dust and Cosmos 96. NASA answered 'no records' on 11 April 2003. She appealed; NASA's Associate Deputy Administrator James Jennings granted the appeal on 18 June 2003 and promised an expedited agency-wide search; NASA then failed to act on its own letter, a failure the court later attributed to poor tracking and the relocation of the FOIA office. On 15 October 2003 she notified NASA she intended to sue, and on 9 December 2003 - thirty-eight years to the day after Kecksburg - she filed Kean v. National Aeronautics and Space Administration, Civil Action No. 03-2509 (EGS), in the United States District Court for the District of Columbia, represented by Lobel, Novins & Lamont. Judge Emmet G. Sullivan held a hearing on 20 March 2007 and on 27 March 2007 issued a memorandum opinion finding that NASA had not met its burden of showing its searches were adequate, denying the agency summary judgment on the search question and ordering the parties to propose search and documentation procedures that would create a proper record. NASA settled in part on 16 October 2007 and searched under court supervision, filing joint status reports every sixty days until the case was dismissed on 24 August 2009. Kean went on to write 'UFOs: Generals, Pilots and Government Officials Go on the Record' (2010) and, with Ralph Blumenthal and Helene Cooper, the 16 December 2017 New York Times story that revealed AATIP.

### Where the record conflicts

- Space writer James Oberg argued in 2008 that NASA was unlikely to hold such records, that the men at the Kecksburg site were probably Air Force personnel identifying themselves as NASA, and that Kean's suit was a publicity stunt for her Sci Fi Channel backers. That characterisation is recorded here as a denial artifact; the court's finding on search adequacy is a judicial holding and stands on its own.

### Recorded as unverified

- Which outlets declined the story and on what stated grounds.
- Kean's date of birth. The contents of the two emails between FOIA officer Stephen McConnell and NASA attorney Bierbower, which the court reviewed in camera and upheld as withheld under Exemption 5.

Affiliations recorded: The Debrief (2023), The New York Times (2017-2019, contributor), Author, UFOs: Generals, Pilots and Government Officials Go on the Record (2010), The New York Times (contributor, 2017), The Debrief (contributor, 2023), UFODATA, KPFA / Flashpoints (1990s), Coalition for Freedom of Information, The New York Times (contributing reporter, 2017 AATIP story), Lobel, Novins & Lamont (counsel: Martin Lobel, Lee Ellen Helfrich).

## Richard C. Doty

Richard C. Doty is a person on the board; board tier B, documented channel and capability, programme role indicated but not yet nailed to a primary source; dates 1983-04-01; axes C1 A2 D2; 10 connections.

In one line: USAF AFOSI Special Agent 1979-1988; later New Mexico State Police officer.

### The longer account

Doty is the only US counterintelligence officer to have stated on camera and on the record that he personally ran disinformation about UFOs against American civilians as part of his official duties. As an AFOSI special agent at Kirtland AFB from 1979, he authored or handled the 1980-1983 Kirtland documents concerning unexplained objects over Coyote Canyon, fed fabricated material to Paul Bennewitz, and in April 1983 showed the documentary producer Linda Moulton Howe a briefing paper purporting to describe a government programme - the 'Project Aquarius' teletype - which she was not permitted to copy. He was also a conduit in the emergence of the MJ-12 material through Bill Moore. He left AFOSI in 1988 and later served as a New Mexico State Police officer; his 321-page state police personnel file was obtained under the New Mexico Inspection of Public Records Act and published in 2021, giving the first full documentary picture of his post-Air Force career. Doty's value to this board is not that his statements are reliable - they are not, and he has repeatedly changed them - but that his existence is the proof of concept. The United States assigned a counterintelligence officer to manage the UFO information environment. He has said so. Whatever else is in dispute, that is not.

### What is left unexplained

No US government document has ever acknowledged the authority under which an AFOSI agent conducted a multi-year disinformation campaign against a US citizen.

### Where the record conflicts

- AARO's 2024 Historical Record Report Volume I makes no mention of Doty, AFOSI, Bennewitz or Kirtland - an omission in a report whose stated purpose was to address the historical record of USG involvement with UAP.

### Recorded as unverified

- Doty's varying claims about what he was shown or told inside AFOSI cannot be corroborated from any released document.

Affiliations recorded: US Air Force Office of Special Investigations (c.1979-1988), New Mexico State Police (1990s-2000s).

### Why it holds its tier

Re-graded B on a global pass (rank 318 of 1057, score 36). 10 connections, 1 of them custody/witness/funding/programmatic. Axes C1/A2/D2.

## Garry Nolan

Garry Nolan is a person on the board; board tier A, named in sworn testimony or primary documents as directly inside the programme; dates 2023-06-05; axes C2 A1 D3; 9 connections.

In one line: Professor of Pathology, Stanford; Sol Foundation co-founder.

### The longer account

Nolan is the most credentialed working scientist to put his name to UAP materials analysis, and the reason the subject now has a peer-reviewed footprint at all. He has held the Harris endowed chair in pathology at Stanford since 2011, founded Rigel, Nodality, BINA Technology (sold to Roche for $107m) and Akoya Biosciences, and co-developed CODEX and multiplexed ion beam imaging (MIBI) with Michael Angelo and Sean Bendall - the same MIBI instrument he then turned on alleged UAP debris. His entry into the field was not voluntary in the ordinary sense: he told KQED in June 2021 that he was approached by 'some people representing the government and an aerospace corporation to help them understand the medical harm that had come to some individuals, related to supposed interactions with an anomalous craft', because 'they were interested in the kinds of blood analysis that my lab can do'. That is the crux for this board - an unnamed government element and an unnamed aerospace corporation walking human casualty material into a Stanford pathology laboratory. In January 2022 he published, with Jacques Vallee, Sizun Jiang and former NASA Ames engineer Larry Lemke, the first serious peer-reviewed instrumental study of alleged UAP material in Progress in Aerospace Sciences, applying SIMS and MIBI at 200 nm lateral resolution to the 1977 Council Bluffs, Iowa molten-metal mass. The finding is genuinely awkward for both camps: no anomalous isotope ratios, but incomplete mixing of the constituent metals indicating an industrial process of unknown origin, with meteoritic, satellite, aircraft, dumping and thermite explanations each argued away. In May 2023 at the SALT iConnections conference he said governments have retrieved artefacts from extraterrestrial craft and put the probability of long-term extraterrestrial visitation at '100 percent'. He co-founded The Sol Foundation with Peter Skafish on 15 August 2023, and in June 2026 was named to the White House-requested UAP Science Advisory Council.

### What is left unexplained

The incomplete mixing of metals in the Council Bluffs mass, with all the ordinary industrial and impact explanations argued away, is the residual: a sample that is terrestrial by isotope and unexplained by process.

### Where the record conflicts

- Nolan's brain-imaging findings - reported asymmetries in the caudate/putamen of people with claimed UAP-proximity exposure - have been described by him in interviews and on stage for several years but are not, as of August 2026, traceable to a peer-reviewed publication under his name. Recorded here as a tier-2 assertion pending publication.

### Recorded as unverified

- Whether Nolan has ever been offered access to material connected to the programs Grusch describes.
- The identity of the government element and the aerospace corporation that approached him.
- Whether any of the material Nolan has examined came with documented federal chain of custody, and where such documentation resides.
- The peer-reviewed status and location of his brain-imaging dataset.
- The identity of the multi-witness event whose material was analysed in Briefing No. 3, the chain of custody of the specimen, and the journal of peer-reviewed publication.

Affiliations recorded: Stanford University School of Medicine, Professor of Pathology, The Sol Foundation, co-founder (2023- ), Stanford University School of Medicine, Department of Pathology (Harris Professor, 2011- ), Rigel Pharmaceuticals (founded 1996), Nodality (2003), BINA Technology (2010), Akoya Biosciences (2015), The Sol Foundation (co-founder, 2023- ), UAP Science Advisory Council (2026- ), Stanford University, The Sol Foundation, UAP Science Advisory Council.

### Why it holds its tier

## Jacques F. Vallee

Jacques F. Vallee is a person on the board; board tier B, documented channel and capability, programme role indicated but not yet nailed to a primary source; dates 1995-01-01; axes C2 A1 D2; 9 connections.

In one line: Computer scientist and venture capitalist; ARPANET engineer; NIDS advisory board; materials-analysis researcher.

### The longer account

Vallee is the provenance chain. Where Nolan brought the instruments, Vallee brought the samples and, crucially, the fifty-year paper trail attached to them. Trained in mathematics at Paris (1959) and astrophysics at Lille (1961), with a Northwestern PhD (1967), he joined Douglas Engelbart's Augmentation Research Center at SRI International in 1971 - the same institution and period as Puthoff's psychoenergetics work - and succeeded Paul Baran as principal investigator on the NSF computer-networking project, building PLANET, one of the first ARPANET conferencing systems. He then spent 1987-2010 as a Silicon Valley general partner. Across all of it he assembled a physical collection of alleged UAP-associated materials with documented case files: the 1957 Ubatuba magnesium fragments, the 1977 Council Bluffs molten mass, and the 1945 San Antonio, New Mexico 'Trinity' case material he wrote up with Paola Harris in 2021. He was a consultant to Robert Bigelow's National Institute for Discovery Science and a member of the Bigelow Aerospace scientific advisory board, which puts him inside the same Bigelow ecosystem that produced BAASS and the AAWSAP contract. His 2022 collaboration with Nolan is the moment the collection met modern instrumentation. The custody problem here is honest and stated: Vallee's samples come with newspaper reporting, police photographs, fire-department accounts and witness interviews - a civil chain, not a federal one - and that is exactly why the government-held material remains the missing term. His lifetime archive went to the Archives of the Impossible at Rice University, with embargoes running to 2028-2031: a dark segment with a published end date.

### Where the record conflicts

- Barry Greenwood and other critics have argued that decades of Bigelow-funded field research produced no documentary evidence fit for publication. The 2022 paper is the counter-example that its critics have to address on instrumentation grounds rather than on rhetoric.

### Recorded as unverified

- The full inventory of Vallee's materials collection and which items have documented custody back to the incident.
- The contents of the Rice University Archives of the Impossible material under embargo to 2028-2031.

Affiliations recorded: Stanford Research Institute, Augmentation Research Center (1970s), SRI International, National Institute for Discovery Science (advisory board, 1995-2004), Documatica Research / venture capital, SRI International, Augmentation Research Center (1971-1972), Institute for the Future (1972-1976), InfoMedia Corporation, Sofinnova / Euro-America Ventures (1987-2010), National Institute for Discovery Science - consultant, Bigelow Aerospace scientific advisory board, Documatica Research LLC.

- **Tier 1 documented.** Vallee is second author of the Progress in Aerospace Sciences 128 (2022) 100788 instrumental analysis of the Council Bluffs material. Source: Progress in Aerospace Sciences, DOI 10.1016/j.paerosci.2021.100788

## Colm A. Kelleher

Colm A. Kelleher is a person on the board; board tier A, named in sworn testimony or primary documents as directly inside the programme; dates 2009-07-01; axes C2 A2 D3; 8 connections.

In one line: Biochemist; Deputy Administrator, BAASS; former NIDS project manager.

### The longer account

Kelleher is the working scientist who ran the field side of the government's own anomalous-phenomena programme, and he is the reason the biological and materials strands are joined. A biochemist by training, he was a project manager at Robert Bigelow's National Institute for Discovery Science through its Skinwalker Ranch and animal-mutilation investigations, and in 2005 co-wrote 'Hunt for the Skinwalker' with George Knapp - the book that DIA official James Lacatski read, which prompted Lacatski to contact Bigelow, visit the ranch, and set in motion the sequence that produced Sen. Harry Reid's $22 million appropriation and the AAWSAP contract to BAASS. When that contract landed, Kelleher became deputy administrator of BAASS, which is to say the deputy director of the operational arm of a Defense Intelligence Agency programme. In 2021 he co-authored 'Skinwalkers at the Pentagon' with Lacatski and Knapp, an account written by the programme's own manager and its own deputy administrator. That is a very unusual thing: the two people who ran the contract wrote the memoir. What they describe - biological effects on personnel, physical trace evidence, sample collection - is the same subject matter as Green's DIRD and Nolan's later laboratory work, and it is the connective tissue between the paranormal-adjacent field programme and the materials-analysis pipeline.

### Where the record conflicts

- Barry Greenwood, writing in the Journal of Scientific Exploration, criticised the Skinwalker corpus for the absence of documentary evidence after decades of investigation.

### Recorded as unverified

- Total BAASS headcount under Kelleher; figures in the 40-50 range are commonly cited but not in a released primary document.
- NIDS' raw field data has never been published or independently reviewed.
- Whether BAASS transferred physical samples to DIA, and if so under what receipt.
- The disposition of the BAASS 'Ten Month Report' and the monthly reports sent to the Pentagon.

Affiliations recorded: National Institute for Discovery Science, project manager (1996-2004), Bigelow Aerospace Advanced Space Studies, Deputy Administrator (2008-2012), National Institute for Discovery Science (Deputy Administrator, 1996-2004), Bigelow Aerospace Advanced Space Studies (project manager, 2008-2012), National Institute for Discovery Science (1996-2004), Bigelow Aerospace Advanced Space Studies - deputy administrator (2008-2010), Bigelow Aerospace, Author, 'Hunt for the Skinwalker' (2005) and 'Skinwalkers at the Pentagon' (2021).
