## Ryan Graves

Ryan Graves is a person on the board; board tier A, named in sworn testimony or primary documents as directly inside the programme; dates 2023-07-26; axes C0 A1 D3; 8 connections.

In one line: Lt, USN (Ret.), VFA-11 Red Rippers; Executive Director, Americans for Safe Aerospace.

### The longer account

Graves converted the 2014-2015 USS Theodore Roosevelt workup encounters from an anecdote into an institutional safety problem, which is why he is the aviator the disclosure movement could not be talked out of. In his written statement to House Oversight on 26 July 2023 he testified: 'In 2014, I was near Virginia Beach as part of VFA-11, a Navy Fighter/Attack Squadron made up of F/A-18F Super Hornets,' and that his squadron 'began witnessing advanced UAP demonstrating complex maneuvers on a regular basis' after the fleet upgraded to the APG-79 AESA radar. The pivotal event he described occurred in Warning Area W-72, 'an exclusive block of airspace ten miles east of Virginia Beach,' where 'one of the pilots saw a dark gray cube inside of a clear sphere - motionless against the wind, fixed directly at the entry point,' and two jets 'only 100 feet apart, were forced to take evasive action.' The institutional failure is the part that indicts: 'Our squadron submitted a safety report, but there was no official acknowledgement of the incident and no further mechanism to report the sightings.' Two of the three videos the Department of Defense formally released on 27 April 2020 - GIMBAL and GO FAST, both filmed in January 2015 - came out of this operating area. Graves founded Americans for Safe Aerospace to build the reporting channel the Navy did not provide, telling Congress in July 2023 that it had 'nearly 5,000 members' and was 'actively working with more than 30 UAP witnesses.'

### What is left unexplained

A stationary dark cube inside a clear sphere, holding position against the wind at a fixed airspace entry point, has no assessed explanation in any released AARO product.

### Where the record conflicts

- AARO's 19 November 2024 SASC case slides assess GO FAST as showing an object at roughly 13,000 feet moving at about 45 mph with 60-knot winds, i.e. 'did not demonstrate any anomalous speeds or flight characteristics.' Graves's testimony does not rest on GO FAST's speed but on the near-midair in W-72 and the absence of a reporting mechanism, neither of which AARO's parallax analysis addresses.

### Recorded as unverified

- The squadron safety report Graves says VFA-11 submitted after the W-72 near-miss has never been released; its Navy Safety Center or hazard-report control number is unknown.
- Whether Americans for Safe Aerospace's aircrew reports have been ingested into AARO's case system, and under what arrangement.

Affiliations recorded: US Navy (2009-2019), VFA-11 Red Rippers, USS Theodore Roosevelt (2014-2015), Americans for Safe Aerospace, founder and Executive Director (2022-), Scientific Coalition for UAP Studies / AIAA UAP community engagement, US Navy F/A-18F pilot, VFA-11 (2009-2019), Americans for Safe Aerospace, founder (2022-), Scientific Coalition for UAP Studies, Americans for Safe Aerospace, formerly US Navy VFA-11.

Source: Ryan Graves, written testimony, House Oversight subcommittee, 26 July 2023
- **Tier 1 documented.** 'Our squadron submitted a safety report, but there was no official acknowledgement of the incident and no further mechanism to report the sightings.' Source: Ryan Graves, written testimony, House Oversight subcommittee, 26 July 2023

## William L. Moore

William L. Moore is a person on the board; board tier B, documented channel and capability, programme role indicated but not yet nailed to a primary source; dates 1989-07-01; axes C0 A1 D2; 8 connections.

In one line: Co-author, 'The Roswell Incident' (1980); first recipient of the MJ-12 film; self-confessed conduit for AFOSI disinformation.

### The longer account

William L. Moore wrote the book that made Roswell a public case and then, nine years later, told a room full of ufologists that he had been working with Air Force counterintelligence. With Charles Berlitz he published 'The Roswell Incident' (Grosset & Dunlap, 1980), built on Stanton Friedman's 1978 contact with Jesse Marcel Sr. and on the second-hand Barney Barnett material, and it established the modern case. In December 1984 he and Jaime Shandera received in the post a roll of undeveloped 35mm film containing the Eisenhower Briefing Document and the Truman-Forrestal memorandum - the origin of the entire MJ-12 corpus that Friedman then spent a decade defending and Randle a decade attacking. At the 1989 MUFON symposium in Las Vegas, Moore stated publicly that from about 1980 he had cooperated with personnel of the Air Force Office of Special Investigations, and that he had knowingly passed disinformation to the Albuquerque physicist and businessman Paul Bennewitz, whose mental health collapsed under it. That admission is the single most consequential statement ever made by a researcher in this field, and this board treats it structurally rather than moralistically: it establishes, on the admission of the participant, that a US counterintelligence element used a civilian UFO researcher as a delivery channel for fabricated material into the UFO community. Any document, witness or claim entering the record through that channel between roughly 1980 and 1989 - and the MJ-12 papers arrived in the middle of it - carries a provenance problem that cannot be argued away.

### Where the record conflicts

- Friedman continued to regard the MJ-12 core documents as authentic after Moore's admission; Randle regarded the admission as conclusive against them.

### Recorded as unverified

- The names of the AFOSI personnel Moore says he dealt with, and whether any AFOSI record of the relationship has ever been requested or released. Nothing responsive has surfaced.

Affiliations recorded: Fair-Witness Project, Co-author with Charles Berlitz, 'The Roswell Incident' (1980).

### Why it holds its tier

Re-graded B on a global pass (rank 288 of 1057, score 38). 8 connections, 2 of them custody/witness/funding/programmatic, 1 official denial recorded against it. Axes C0/A1/D2.

### The claims on the record

- **Tier 1 documented.** Moore and Shandera published 'The MJ-12 Documents: An Analytical Report' as Fair-Witness Project Publication Number 1500 in 1990, and Moore and Friedman published 'Philip Klass and MJ-12: What are the Facts' as Publication Number 1290 in 1988 - both cited in CIA's own 1997 history of the Agency's UFO role. Source: Gerald K. Haines, 'CIA's Role in the Study of UFOs, 1947-90', endnote 93
- **Tier 1 documented.** The US Air Force's 1995 Roswell report names 'The Roswell Incident (1980) by William Moore and Charles Berlitz' and 'Crashed Saucers: Evidence in Search of Proof (1985) by Moore' among the major works it reviewed to set the parameters of its records search, and describes Moore and Berlitz as having 'engaged in research which led them to publish' the 1980 book. Source: Headquarters USAF, 'The Roswell Report: Fact versus Fiction in the New Mexico Desert' (1995) https://archive.org/details/DTIC_ADA326148
- **Tier 2 named person on the record.** 'The Roswell Incident' by Charles Berlitz and William Moore was published in New York by Grosset & Dunlap in 1980, ISBN 978-0448211992. Source: Publisher's imprint and library catalogue records; DEMOTED from Tier 1 (the same assertion is Tier 2 on the-roswell-incident-1980); needs primary replacement
- **Tier 2 named person on the record.** Moore publicly admitted at the 1989 MUFON symposium that he had cooperated with US Air Force counterintelligence and had knowingly passed false information to Paul Bennewitz. Source: William L. Moore, address to the MUFON Symposium, Las Vegas, July 1989
- **Tier 2 named person on the record.** Moore stated at the 1989 MUFON symposium that he had cooperated with US Air Force Office of Special Investigations personnel from about 1980 and had knowingly passed disinformation to Paul Bennewitz. - **Tier 3 stated inference.** The 1984 arrival of the MJ-12 film at Moore's address falls inside the window he later admitted was a period of cooperation with AFOSI, which is sufficient on its own to bar the MJ-12 corpus from tier 1 or tier 2 status regardless of any forensic analysis. ## Harry S. Truman

Harry S. Truman is a person on the board; board tier B, documented channel and capability, programme role indicated but not yet nailed to a primary source; dates 1946-01-22; axes C2 A3 D1; 7 connections.

In one line: President of the United States from 12 April 1945; signatory of the 22 January 1946 intelligence directive and of the National Security Act of 1947.

### The longer account

The president who signed the founding instruments of the structure this board maps has had no node on it, because the board's prose says 'Truman' and never 'Harry'. Two records settle the office and the instruments. The directive of 22 January 1946 creates the National Intelligence Authority and the Central Intelligence Group and the post of Director of Central Intelligence, and carries his signature. The National Security Act of 1947, approved 26 July 1947, creates the National Security Council, the Central Intelligence Agency, the National Military Establishment and a Research and Development Board, and requires the Secretary of Defense to come from civilian life. He is here for the instruments, not for anything said about him on a podcast.

### Why it holds its tier

Re-graded B on a global pass (rank 237 of 1057, score 41). 7 connections, 0 of them custody/witness/funding/programmatic. Axes C2/A3/D1.

### The claims on the record

- **Tier 1 documented.** Section 102(d)(5) of the same Act is the residual authority every later covert-action directive is written under: the Agency is 'to perform such other functions and duties related to intelligence affecting the national security as the National Security Council may from time to time direct.' Source: National Security Act of 1947, Public Law 80-253, 61 Stat. 495, sec. 102(d)(5), https://www.govinfo.gov/content/pkg/STATUTE-61/pdf/STATUTE-61-Pg495.pdf
- **Tier 1 documented.** The 22 January 1946 directive is signed by him and creates the office by fiat: 'It is my desire, and I hereby direct, that all Federal foreign intelligence activities be planned, developed and coordinated so as to assure the most effective accomplishment of the intelligence mission related to the national security.' The Director of Central Intelligence 'shall be designated by me'. Source: Presidential Directive on Coordination of Foreign Intelligence Activities, 22 January 1946, FRUS 1945-1950, Emergence of the Intelligence Establishment, Document 71, https://history.state.gov/historicaldocuments/frus1945-50Intel/d71
- **Tier 1 documented.** The Department of State's own list of persons gives the office and its start: 'Truman, Harry S., President of the United States from April 12, 1945'. Source: FRUS 1945-1950, Emergence of the Intelligence Establishment, List of Persons, https://history.state.gov/historicaldocuments/frus1945-50Intel/persons
- **Tier 1 documented.** The same directive draws the domestic line the board's later argument keeps testing: 'No police, law enforcement or internal security functions shall be exercised under this directive', and it makes the Authority and the Director 'responsible for fully protecting intelligence sources and methods'. Source: Presidential Directive on Coordination of Foreign Intelligence Activities, 22 January 1946, FRUS 1945-1950, Emergence of the Intelligence Establishment, Document 71, https://history.state.gov/historicaldocuments/frus1945-50Intel/d71
- **Tier 1 documented.** The statute approved on 26 July 1947 creates, in one instrument, the Council, the Agency and the military establishment: 'There is hereby established a council to be known as the National Security Council'; 'There is hereby established under the National Security Council a Central Intelligence Agency with a Director of Central Intelligence, who shall be the head thereof'; 'There is hereby established the National Military Establishment, and the Secretary of Defense shall be the head thereof.' Source: National Security Act of 1947, Public Law 80-253, ch. 343, 61 Stat. 495, secs. 101(a), 102(a), 201(a), approved 26 July 1947, https://www.govinfo.gov/content/pkg/STATUTE-61/pdf/STATUTE-61-Pg495.pdf

Section 201(a) establishes the National Military Establishment under a Secretary of Defense who, by section 202(a), must be appointed from civilian life. Sources: Presidential Directive on Coordination of Foreign Intelligence Activities, 22 January 1946 (FRUS 1945-50 Intel, Doc. 71), https://history.state.gov/historicaldocuments/frus1945-50Intel/d71, dated 1946-01-22.
- Harry S. Truman runs out to **Central Intelligence Agency** (institution, board tier A), a institutional link at Tier 1 documented. The link reads: Signed the statute that created the Agency. Section 102(a) of the National Security Act of 1947, approved 26 July 1947, establishes the Central Intelligence Agency under the National Security Council. Sources: Presidential Directive on Coordination of Foreign Intelligence Activities, 22 January 1946 (FRUS 1945-50 Intel, Doc. 71), https://history.state.gov/historicaldocuments/frus1945-50Intel/d71, dated 1946-01-22.

## Jesse A. Marcel Sr.

Jesse A. Marcel Sr. is a person on the board; board tier A, named in sworn testimony or primary documents as directly inside the programme; dates 1978-02-20; axes C3 A2 D3; 7 connections.

In one line: Intelligence officer, 509th Bomb Group.

### The longer account

Major Jesse Marcel was the staff intelligence officer of the 509th Bomb Group and the officer who physically handled the Foster ranch debris in July 1947, going to the site with Counter Intelligence Corps Capt. Sheridan Cavitt and carrying material back to the base and onward to Fort Worth. He is the highest-custody witness in the entire 1940s record: not a distant observer, but the man whose job was to identify recovered materiel and who was then photographed in Gen. Ramey's office beside weather balloon debris he later said had been substituted for what he brought in. Marcel said nothing publicly for thirty-one years. In February 1978 he told researcher Stanton Friedman that the balloon explanation was a cover story and that the material he recovered was not from any device he could identify, the interview that restarted the entire Roswell inquiry and, downstream, produced the 1994-1997 Air Force reports and the 1995 GAO records search. His son, Dr. Jesse Marcel Jr., separately described handling the debris as a child. The Air Force's counter-position, that Marcel was mistaken about Project MOGUL balloon materials, is recorded here as the official rebuttal it is.

### What is left unexplained

No disposition authority has ever been produced for the destruction of the RAAF outgoing message traffic covering the exact period of the recovery.

### Where the record conflicts

- Marcel overstated parts of his military biography in later interviews, which the Air Force uses to impeach him; the overstatements do not bear on what he handled in July 1947

### Recorded as unverified

- Whether material was substituted between Roswell and Fort Worth on 8 July 1947

Affiliations recorded: 509th Bomb Group, Roswell Army Air Field, S-2 Intelligence (1946-1948), US Air Force, Special Weapons Program (1948-1950).

### Why it holds its tier

Re-graded A on a global pass (rank 94 of 1057, score 65). 8 connections, 5 of them custody/witness/funding/programmatic, 1 official denial recorded against it. Axes C3/A2/D3.

### The claims on the record

- **Tier 1 documented.** Marcel was the 509th Bomb Group intelligence officer who collected debris at the Foster ranch with CIC Capt. Sheridan Cavitt, and was photographed with debris at Fort Worth on 8 July 1947. Source: USAF, 'The Roswell Report: Fact vs. Fiction in the New Mexico Desert' (1995), which reproduces the Ramey office photographs and the Cavitt interview
- **Tier 1 documented.** The Air Force's own 1995 and 1997 reports concluded the recovered material was from Project MOGUL balloon trains, directly contradicting Marcel. Source: USAF, 'The Roswell Report: Fact vs. Fiction' (1995) and 'The Roswell Report: Case Closed' (1997)
- **Tier 2 named person on the record.** Marcel stated in 1978 that the weather balloon explanation was a cover story and the recovered material was not identifiable as any known device. Source: Jesse Marcel Sr., interview with Stanton T. Friedman, February 1978
- **Tier 2 named person on the record.** Marcel told Stanton Friedman in February 1978 that the material he recovered was not from a weather balloon and was not of terrestrial manufacture, and that the material displayed at Fort Worth was substituted. Source: Friedman's account of the 1978 interview; repeated in Berlitz and Moore (1980) and in Marcel's own filmed interviews
- **Tier 2 named person on the record.** Marcel was named in the 8 July 1947 Roswell Army Air Field press release as the intelligence officer responsible for the recovery, and was photographed at Fort Worth AAF with debris identified there as a weather balloon and radar target. Source: RAAF press release and Fort Worth AAF press photographs, July 1947; reproduced in the 1995 Air Force report as Fig. 4, 'Maj. Jesse Marcel With Flying Disc Debris'
- **Tier 3 stated inference.** Marcel's silence from 1947 to 1978 is more probative than his 1978 statement: a career intelligence officer who believed he had been publicly humiliated by a substitution had thirty-one years and a reserve pension's worth of reasons to say nothing, and did. Reasoning: the timing of his disclosure tracks his retirement and the death of the officers senior to him, not the arrival of a book contract. Source: Inference from his service chronology
- **Tier 3 stated inference.** The GAO's finding that the relevant RAAF messages and administrative records were destroyed without disposition authority means Marcel's account cannot be checked against the primary record, which is itself the point. Source: Inference from GAO/NSIAD-95-187, p.3

## Kirsten E. Gillibrand

Kirsten E. Gillibrand is a person on the board; board tier B, documented channel and capability, programme role indicated but not yet nailed to a primary source; dates 2021-12-27; axes C0 A2 D3; 7 connections.

In one line: D-NY; author of Sec. 1683, FY2022 NDAA (50 U.S.C. 3373).

### The longer account

Gillibrand built the statutory machine that everything else in this domain hangs off. Section 1683 of the FY2022 NDAA, P.L. 117-81, signed 27 December 2021 and codified at 50 U.S.C. 3373, ordered the Secretary of Defense and the DNI to stand up a permanent office to absorb the UAP Task Force, to force reporting into a centralized repository, and to report to Congress. It was restated a year later by Section 6802 of the Intelligence Authorization Act for FY2023, enacted inside P.L. 117-263 on 23 December 2022, which named the office the All-domain Anomaly Resolution Office, put its Director reporting to the Deputy Secretary of Defense and the Principal Deputy DNI, and at subsection (j) demanded a written historical record report on the United States Government's dealings with UAP built from intelligence community records, oral history interviews and interviews of current and former officials. In February 2023 Gillibrand and Rubio led sixteen senators demanding full funding for AARO, arguing it 'provides the opportunity to integrate and resolve threats and hazards to the U.S., while also offering increased transparency.' She was an original cosponsor of S.Amdt. 797 in July 2023. Her posture is different from Schumer's: she legislates the plumbing, not the seizure. It is her plumbing, Sections 1841-1843 of the FY2024 NDAA, that survived when Schumer's Review Board did not, and it is that surviving language she invoked on 8 May 2026 when the administration began releasing files, saying the government must 'finally meet its legal obligation to the American people.'

Affiliations recorded: U.S. Senate, New York (2009- ), Senate Armed Services Committee, Chair, SASC Subcommittee on Personnel, US Senate (NY), SASC Subcommittee on Emerging Threats and Capabilities, Chair.

### Why it holds its tier

## Salvatore Cezar Pais

Salvatore Cezar Pais is a person on the board; board tier A, named in sworn testimony or primary documents as directly inside the programme; dates 2015-07-24; axes C2 A1 D1; 7 connections.

In one line: Aerospace engineer, NAWCAD, NAS Patuxent River (to 2019); named inventor on five Navy-assigned patent filings 2015-2019; later US Navy Strategic Systems Programs, then US Air Force.

### The longer account

Pais is the only person in the open record who has put a description of a craft with the reported UAP flight characteristics into the United States patent system, with the United States Navy as assignee and his own name on the front. That is the entire reason he belongs on this board, and it is a documentary fact independent of whether anything he described works.

Between July 2015 and March 2018 he filed five applications from NAWCAD at NAS Patuxent River, every one of them assigned to 'The United States of America as represented by the Secretary of the Navy': an electromagnetic field generator, a craft using an inertial mass reduction device, a high frequency gravitational wave generator, a piezoelectricity-induced room temperature superconductor, and a plasma compression fusion device. Three were granted. Two published as applications.

The Navy's own invention-disclosure briefing for the inertial mass reduction device, Navy Case PAX 205, was released under FOIA and is the most useful document in the set because Pais wrote it for an internal audience rather than for an examiner. It describes a 'hybrid aerospace / undersea craft (HAUC)' that would 'move with great ease through the air / space / water mediums' enclosed in a 'Vacuum plasma bubble/sheath', achieving 'extreme aerial and underwater speeds (lack of air/water-skin friction) and enhanced stealth capabilities (non-linear scattering of RF and sonar signals)'. Set that description beside the flight characteristics the Navy's own aviators reported in the same decade and the overlap is not subtle: transmedium travel, extreme acceleration, and low observability, itemised in a Navy invention disclosure.

The same briefing is equally explicit about what did not exist. Pais states, twice, that this 'is a theoretical concept - no prototype in existence, as yet', gives the invention original date as 6 March 2015, and proposes Section 219 NISE funding of 250,000 dollars in labour and 250,000 dollars in NAWCAD facility cost to investigate it experimentally. A slide header reads 'EXPERIMENT TRUMPS THEORY EVERYTIME!'. Whatever else the documents are, on their face they are a proposal to test an idea, written by someone asking for money to test it.

That is where the record turns against the strong reading. NAWCAD did fund the work, and reporting based on documents released under FOIA says it spent roughly half a million dollars between 2016 and 2019 and concluded that the effect could not be demonstrated, with no further research conducted. The figure matches the two 250,000 dollar lines Pais proposed. A board that leaves that out is not making the case stronger, it is making it dismissible.

What remains genuinely unusual, and is not disposed of by the negative test result, is the institutional behaviour around the filings. A patent examiner's job is to reject a claim that is not enabled, and the record as reported is that one was rejected on exactly that ground and then granted after the Naval Aviation Enterprise's chief technology officer wrote to the USPTO asserting operability and warning that China was pursuing the same capability. Agencies do not usually spend senior technical credibility forcing through a patent they believe describes nothing. Either the Navy believed the physics, or it wanted the priority date on the public record for reasons it has not stated. Both readings are live, and neither is established.

### What is left unexplained

Why the Navy pressed for grant of a filing an examiner had rejected for lack of enablement, and then funded a test that it concluded had failed, without withdrawing or disclaiming the granted patents.

### Where the record conflicts

- The strong reading of these filings, that they describe technology already understood from a recovered object, is in direct tension with the author's own written statement that no prototype existed and with NAWCAD's reported conclusion that the effect could not be demonstrated. Both the statement and the test result are part of the record and are recorded here.
- Two different questions are routinely collapsed into one when this record is discussed: whether a working article existed, and where the concept came from. The PAX 205 briefing speaks directly to the first and is silent on the second. Treating its answer to the first as an answer to the second is the most common error made about this cluster in both directions.
- {'source': 'USPTO file wrapper document image JCTFAFUKPXXIFW1.pdf against secondary summaries of the episode', 'text': "The widely repeated account that Sheehy wrote to the patent office 'asserting the invention was operable' is contradicted by the letter itself, which opens by agreeing with the examiner that the concept is beyond present possibility. The board carried that version as Tier 2 until the primary document was read on 28 August 2026. Anyone citing the secondary version to a sceptical reader is handing them the rebuttal."}

### Recorded as unverified

- Whether Pais had any access to, or knowledge of, material from any recovery programme. Nothing in the released record connects him to AATIP, AAWSAP, KONA BLUE, any DIRD, or any material-custody chain on this board.
- His current office and title. Secondary reporting places him at US Navy Strategic Systems Programs from 2019, the US Air Force from 2021 and the Space Force more recently; no primary personnel record has been obtained.
- Whether Pais has stated in public interviews that the filings were speculative concepts, and whether any such statement is constrained by an active clearance. No transcript has been obtained for this board; long-form interview material exists and is unindexed here.
- Whether patents assigned to a United States government agency owe maintenance fees on the ordinary 37 CFR 1.362 schedule, or are treated differently. Until that is settled, the lapse of US 10,144,532 cannot be read confidently as a decision rather than a bookkeeping outcome. This is the first thing to check before the expiry is used as an argument.
- The Reasons for Allowance dated 24 October 2018 is still unread, and it is now known WHY: the file wrapper uploaded to this project stops at the Appeal Brief of 20-21 August 2018. Nothing after that date is present for application 15/141,270. It remains the most informative unobtained document on this node, because it is the examiner's own account of what reversed a rejection he had sustained twice. See research/patents/wrapper/MISSING.md.
- No demonstration of the effect appears anywhere in the file wrapper as uploaded, yet the examiner had made one an explicit condition in July 2018 and allowed the case in October 2018. Whether something was filed in between, or whether the examiner simply yielded on the burden-of-proof argument, cannot be determined without the papers listed in MISSING.md. Do not assert either.

Affiliations recorded: Naval Air Warfare Center Aircraft Division (NAWCAD), NAS Patuxent River, Maryland, Naval Air Systems Command (NAVAIR), US Navy Strategic Systems Programs (from 2019), United States Air Force (from 2021).

### Why it holds its tier

Re-graded A on a global pass (rank 46 of 1057, score 83). 7 connections, 6 of them custody/witness/funding/programmatic. Axes C2/A1/D1.

### The claims on the record

- **Tier 1 documented.** A patent assigned to a federal agency owes maintenance fees on the ordinary schedule, which closes the question the expiry finding rested on. 37 CFR 1.362(a) requires them 'in all patents based on applications filed on or after December 12, 1980, except as noted in paragraph (b) of this section, to maintain a patent in force beyond 4, 8 and 12 years after the date of grant', and paragraph (b) gives the exemptions in full: 'Maintenance fees are not required for any plant patents or for any design patents.' Both Pais grants are utility patents from applications filed long after 1980. Neither was in an exempt class, and both owed the fee. Source: 37 CFR 1.362, Time for payment of maintenance fees (CFR 2024, title 37, vol. 1) || https://www.govinfo.gov/content/pkg/CFR-2024-title37-vol1/xml/CFR-2024-title37-vol1-sec1-362.xml
- **Tier 1 documented.** Both granted Pais patents lapsed at the first maintenance fee, five weeks apart. US 10,135,366, granted 20 November 2018, is recorded expired for non-payment on 26 December 2022; US 10,144,532, granted 4 December 2018, is recorded expired for non-payment on 9 January 2023. USPTO mailed a maintenance fee reminder for each in the preceding July. Source: USPTO application data for 14/807943 and 15/141270 (product PTGRXML, data.uspto.gov), retrieved from the file wrappers 28 August 2026
- **Tier 1 documented.** Paragraph [0020] of the application as filed credits the mechanism to a named figure already on this board: 'it is feasible to remove energy-mass from the system by enabling vacuum polarization, as discussed by Harold Puthoff; in that diminution of inertial (and thus gravitational) mass can be achieved via manipulation of quantum field fluctuations in the vacuum.' Source: Application 15/141,270 as filed, 'A Craft Using an Inertial Mass Reduction Device', Navy Case PAX 205
- **Tier 1 documented.** Paragraph [0037] of the application as filed states that the preferred electromagnetic generator for the craft is the one described in US application 14/807,943, 'incorporated by reference, and has the same inventor'. The inertial mass reduction craft and the electromagnetic field generator are therefore linked as components of one design by the filings themselves rather than by inference. Source: Application 15/141,270 as filed, 'A Craft Using an Inertial Mass Reduction Device', Navy Case PAX 205
- **Tier 1 documented.** Sheehy's declaration of 11 May 2018 states his credentials: a PhD in physiological optics from Pennsylvania State University, a master's in Human Factors Engineering from Rensselaer, a bachelor's from Kean University; a civilian Navy employee since 1985; formerly Chief Scientist of the Naval Air Systems Command; Chief Technology Officer of the Naval Aviation Enterprise for the preceding eleven years; promoted to the Senior Executive Service in November 2001; awarded the Presidential Rank Award in 2007. The summary records that no exhibit was shown and no demonstration conducted, and that 'The Examiner pointed out that he still felt that there were enablement issues. The Applicant to submit arguments to the contrary. No agreement was reached.' Source: USPTO file wrapper, application 15/141,270 (PAX 205), retrieved from USPTO Patent Center 28 August 2026; document image JCKKXW5GRXEAPX4.pdf, Applicant-Initiated Interview Summary mailed 22 January 2018; transcript at research/patents/wrapper/interview-summary-2018-01-17.txt
- **Tier 1 documented.** The Appeal Brief states the ownership position without qualification: 'The real party in interested is the United States Government represented by the Secretary of the Navy (The Department of the Navy). The inventor is a U.S.government employee who invented the invention during the course of his employment. As a result, Federal law provides all rights to this invention belong to the United States of America under 37 C.F.R. 501.6.' This is the Navy's own characterisation, in a filing signed by its Office of Counsel. Source: USPTO file wrapper, application 15/141,270 (PAX 205), retrieved from USPTO Patent Center 28 August 2026; document images JLZJWBOMRXEAPX0.pdf and JLZJWBOORXEAPX0.pdf, Appeal Brief under 37 C.F.R. 41.37 faxed 20 August 2018; notes at research/patents/wrapper/appeal-brief-2018-08-20.txt
- **Tier 1 documented.** The December 2017 letter places the NAWCAD experimental programme on the record and dates it. Sheehy wrote that 'Dr. Pais is currently funded by NAWCAD to design a test article and instrumentation to demonstrate the experimental feasibility of achieving high electromagnetic (EM) field-energy and flux values', and that 'He is currently one year into the project and has already begun a series of experiments'. That places the start of funded experimental work in about December 2016 and corroborates, from a primary document, the secondary reporting that NAWCAD funded testing from 2016. ## Allen W. Dulles

Allen W. Dulles is a person on the board; board tier B, documented channel and capability, programme role indicated but not yet nailed to a primary source; dates 1953-03-13; axes C1 A3 D1; 6 connections.

In one line: Director of Central Intelligence; signed the CIA's distancing letters on the Robertson Panel report.

### The longer account

Allen W. Dulles signed the three letters that closed the Agency's 1952 flying-saucer file. On 13 March 1953, over his name and on Office of the Director paper classified SECRET, the CIA sent the Robertson Panel report to the Secretary of Defense, to the Federal Civil Defense Administration and to the National Security Resources Board, told each that the conclusions pointed to 'certain potential dangers to national security', and then said in the same letter that the Agency did not consider the subject primarily its concern. Those three letters do two things the board needs: they attribute the panel to Bedell Smith in the Agency's own words, and they are a dated, signed instance of the Agency stepping back from a subject it had itself convened a panel on ten weeks earlier. Dulles also has an OSS personnel file, named by the National Archives among the notable files opened in 2008; the board carries no Office of Strategic Services node, so no edge is drawn for it.

### Recorded as unverified

- That Dulles's published diary shows a meeting with the president on the date of an Eisenhower MJ-12 briefing. Not opened; the Dulles papers at Princeton were not reachable and no date or page is given by the speaker.

Affiliations recorded: Central Intelligence Agency, Director of Central Intelligence, Office of Strategic Services (personnel file, Record Group 226).

### Why it holds its tier

Re-graded B on a global pass (rank 255 of 1057, score 40). 6 connections, 0 of them custody/witness/funding/programmatic, 1 official denial recorded against it. Axes C1/A3/D1.

### The claims on the record

- **Tier 1 documented.** Each of the three letters is a rewrite of an earlier version that was withdrawn. A note to the Executive Registry filed with two of them reads: 'Please note that the attached letter ... was not sent out, but was rewritten as of 13 March 1953 (copy attached).' The letters themselves carry the stamp 'REWRITTEN 13 March 1953'. Source: Executive Registry notes filed with CIA-RDP80B01676R001000160044-8 and -045-7, https://archive.org/details/cia-readingroom-document-cia-rdp80b01676r001000160045-7
- **Tier 1 documented.** On 13 March 1953 Dulles forwarded the Robertson Panel report to three agencies over his own signature, describing it as pointing out 'certain potential dangers to national security which are related to the subject and suggest ways of their elimination.' The three addressees are the Secretary of Defense, the Acting Administrator of the Federal Civil Defense Administration and the Vice Chairman of the National Security Resources Board, which are exactly the three IAC-D-67 had proposed on 18 February 1953. Source: Allen W. Dulles, three letters of 13 March 1953, CIA-RDP80B01676R001000160043-9, -044-8 and -045-7, https://archive.org/details/cia-readingroom-document-cia-rdp80b01676r001000160043-9
- **Tier 1 documented.** The National Archives names Dulles among the OSS personnel files opened on 14 August 2008: the press office distributed CDs including 'selected files of some of the notable people who served in the OSS, including former CIA directors Allen Dulles and William Casey'. Source: National Archives media alert 08-130, 'NARA to Open Official Personnel Files of the OSS', 7 August 2008, https://www.archives.gov/press/press-releases/2008/nr08-130
- **Tier 1 documented.** The letters attribute the Robertson Panel to his predecessor by name: 'This panel was convened at the direction of General Smith, following the recommendation of the Intelligence Advisory Committee.' Source: Allen W. Dulles to Jack Gorrie, National Security Resources Board, 13 March 1953, CIA-RDP80B01676R001000160044-8, https://archive.org/details/cia-readingroom-document-cia-rdp80b01676r001000160044-8
- **Tier 1 documented.** The same letters distance the Agency from the subject in the same breath: 'Although this Agency does not consider problems arising from sightings of "flying saucers" primarily its concern, we shall be pleased to assist in any appropriate action that you deem advisable.' Source: Allen W. Dulles to the Secretary of Defense, 13 March 1953, SECRET / Security Information, CIA-RDP80B01676R001000160043-9, https://archive.org/details/cia-readingroom-document-cia-rdp80b01676r001000160043-9

## Abraham 'Avi' Loeb

Abraham 'Avi' Loeb is a person on the board; board tier B, documented channel and capability, programme role indicated but not yet nailed to a primary source; dates 2026-06-15; axes C1 A1 D3; 6 connections.

In one line: Frank B. Baird Jr. Professor of Science, Harvard University; Director, Institute for Theory and Computation, Harvard-Smithsonian CfA; founder, The Galileo Project; Chair, UAP Science Advisory Council (2026).

### The longer account

Loeb is what happens when the technical layer refuses to wait for the classified system. Harvard astronomy faculty since 1993, tenured three years later, chair of the astronomy department 2011-2020, Baird Professor since 2012, and director of the Institute for Theory and Computation at the Harvard-Smithsonian Center for Astrophysics since 2007, he founded The Galileo Project on 26 July 2021 - four weeks after the ODNI preliminary assessment - on the explicit premise that the way out of a classified impasse is to build unclassified instruments and publish. The project raised roughly $1.8 million in its first year from donors including Frank Laukien of Bruker and William Linton of Promega, installed its first observatory at Harvard College Observatory in 2022, and published its instrumentation architecture in the Journal of Astronomical Instrumentation. In June-July 2023 he led a $1.6 million Pacific Ocean expedition off Papua New Guinea to recover fragments of CNEOS 2014-01-08 (IM1), returning with sub-millimetre spherules that his team classified as a distinct 'BeLaU' composition in Research Notes of the AAS and Chemical Geology. The scientific counter-attack was heavy and specific: Fernando et al. in Geophysical Journal International (March 2024) showed the guiding seismic signal was probably truck traffic; Gallardo argued coal-ash contamination in Research Notes of the AAS; Rudraswami et al. re-evaluated the spherules' origin in Chemical Geology in March 2025. Loeb conceded ground on 3I/ATLAS in 2025-2026 after speculating about its artificiality. Then on 15 June 2026 the position changed entirely: Loeb was named chair of a UAP Science Advisory Council formed at the request of the White House in coordination with AARO, ODNI, the FBI and other agencies - a council with, by contemporaneous reporting, no budget and no access to classified material.

### Where the record conflicts

- The Guardian reported that scientific observers regarded Loeb's council picks as 'apparently similar minds', and the Associated Press characterised some members as 'UFO activists'. The Galileo Project's IM1 claims are contested in three separate peer-reviewed rebuttals.
- DefenseScoop reports Loeb formed and leads the council; the Sol Foundation states the council was announced by the Office of the Director of National Intelligence with the support of the FBI. The two accounts differ on whether the council is a private body advising the government or a government-chartered one.

### Recorded as unverified

- The charter document, if any, establishing the UAP Science Advisory Council, and which office within the White House requested it.
- Whether the council reports to the 'UAP Governance Board' described by the Sol Foundation, and what that board is.
- The full membership roster of the 12-member council has not been published in a government document.
- Whether the council has a Federal Advisory Committee Act charter, a sponsoring agency of record, or appointed rather than invited membership.

Affiliations recorded: Harvard University Department of Astronomy (1993- ; chair 2011-2020), Harvard-Smithsonian Center for Astrophysics, Institute for Theory and Computation (director, 2007- ), The Galileo Project (founder, 2021- ), UAP Science Advisory Council (chair, 2026- ), Harvard University, Department of Astronomy, Galileo Project, UAP Science Advisory Council, chair (2026-), Harvard University, UAP Science Advisory Council.

## Jeremy Kenyon Lockyer Corbell

Jeremy Kenyon Lockyer Corbell is a person on the board; board tier C, contextual, real and relevant, no direct programme linkage on the record yet; dates 2021-05-14; axes C0 A1 D2; 6 connections.

In one line: Filmmaker; co-host, Weaponized.

### The longer account

Jeremy Corbell is the movement's primary conduit for leaked military imagery, and that role - not his filmmaking - is what makes him structurally significant. Beginning in 2019 he released a sequence of US Navy materials including the USS Omaha 'transmedium' footage and the pyramid-shaped object video, several of which the Pentagon subsequently confirmed as genuine Navy recordings. The confirmation pattern is the interesting datum: material leaks to Corbell, Corbell publishes, the Department authenticates the provenance while contesting the interpretation. He directed Bob Lazar: Area 51 & Flying Saucers, which premiered on Netflix in 2019 and returned Lazar to public attention, and Hunt for the Skinwalker, based on the George Knapp and Colm Kelleher book. He co-hosts Weaponized with Knapp. He has been criticised within the field for showmanship and for imagery later explained by lens artefacts, and both things are true at once: he is an unreliable narrator and a reliable pipe.

## Marco Rubio

Marco Rubio is a person on the board; board tier B, documented channel and capability, programme role indicated but not yet nailed to a primary source; dates 2023-07-13; axes C0 A3 D2; 6 connections.

### The longer account

Rubio matters here because of what he had access to. As Vice Chairman of the Senate Select Committee on Intelligence from February 2021 until he resigned his seat in January 2025 to become Secretary of State, he was one of eight people in the country entitled to be briefed on everything, and he used that position to say repeatedly and on the record that the accounts were coming from serious people. In March 2021, then already SSCI Vice Chair, he told Fox Business that objects were flying over military installations and that 'It isn't ours. It isn't anything that's registered to the FAA, and in many cases exhibits attributes of things we've never seen.' He was an original cosponsor of S.Amdt. 797 on 13 July 2023, the only member of the Gang of Eight other than Schumer to put his name on the eminent domain text. In February 2023 he co-led the sixteen-senator push with Gillibrand to fully fund AARO. His departure from the Senate in January 2025 removed the disclosure coalition's most senior intelligence-committee Republican, and the collapse of UAP provisions in the FY2026 and FY2027 authorization cycles begins immediately after it.

## Paul Frederick Bennewitz

Paul Frederick Bennewitz is a person on the board; board tier B, documented channel and capability, programme role indicated but not yet nailed to a primary source; dates 1980-11-10; axes C1 A0 D3; 6 connections.

In one line: Physicist; president, Thunder Scientific Corp.; target of AFOSI disinformation 1980-1988.

### The longer account

Bennewitz is the clearest documented casualty of a US counterintelligence operation run against an American civilian over UFO information. He was a working physicist who ran Thunder Scientific Corporation, an instrumentation firm whose customers included the Air Force, from a property whose sightlines took in the Manzano Weapons Storage Area and Coyote Canyon on the eastern edge of Kirtland AFB. From 1979 he recorded lights and electronic signals over the Manzano nuclear-weapons storage complex and reported them - to the Air Force, in writing, repeatedly. The Air Force's documented response was an AFOSI file: a formal AFOSI complaint-form record dated 10 November 1980 confirming that Kirtland security police had observed unexplained aerial objects over Coyote Canyon and that Bennewitz's photography and reporting were under official notice. What followed, on the accounts of the AFOSI personnel involved, was not an investigation but a feed: Bennewitz was supplied with fabricated material about underground alien bases, treaties and biological experiments, calibrated to his existing beliefs, in order to discredit him and to mask whatever it was he had actually recorded near a nuclear storage site. He deteriorated, was hospitalised for psychiatric care in 1988, and died in 2003. The Bennewitz affair is the template: the state does not need to seize a witness's evidence if it can destroy the witness's credibility, and it can do that using its own personnel, on the record, with no legal consequence.

### What is left unexplained

AFOSI's own 1980 document concedes unexplained objects over a nuclear weapons storage area. No follow-up investigation of that concession has ever been released.

### Where the record conflicts

- No official US government report has ever acknowledged the Bennewitz disinformation operation. AARO's 2024 Historical Record Report Volume I does not mention Bennewitz, Doty, AFOSI or Kirtland at all.

### Recorded as unverified

- The content of the material Bennewitz recorded on his equipment has never been independently analysed; his original data is not publicly held.

Affiliations recorded: Thunder Scientific Corporation, Albuquerque (founder/president), Aerial Phenomena Research Organization (associate).

### Why it holds its tier

Re-graded B on a global pass (rank 317 of 1057, score 36). 6 connections, 2 of them custody/witness/funding/programmatic, 1 official denial recorded against it. Axes C1/A0/D3.

### The claims on the record

Source: AFOSI complaint-form record, Kirtland AFB, 10 Nov 1980, released under FOIA
- **Tier 2 named person on the record.** At 0:34:30 in the same episode Bishop said that in his own reading Bennewitz 'was his own worst enemy' and that the specific thing he knows Bill Moore did was to be told to give Bennewitz the Aquarius document, in which two of Bennewitz's own films are mentioned. The author of the standard account is on the record limiting what he says was actually done to the man. Source: American Alchemy with Jesse Michels, 'Fake Alien Signals Drove Him Insane... (ft. ## Roscoe H. Hillenkoetter

Roscoe H. Hillenkoetter is a person on the board; board tier B, documented channel and capability, programme role indicated but not yet nailed to a primary source; dates 1960-06-01; axes C3 A2 D3; 6 connections.

In one line: First Director of Central Intelligence, May 1947 - October 1950; NICAP board member 1960.

### The longer account

The first Director of Central Intelligence went on the public record in 1960 saying that the objects were 'operating under intelligent control' and asking Congress to investigate, and the documents that establish it are held by the Central Intelligence Agency itself. A Worcester Gazette clipping of 1 June 1960, filed in CIA records and released in 2014, reports him declaring: 'The unknown objects are operating under intelligent control. It is imperative that we learn where the UFO's (unidentified flying objects) come from and what their purpose is.' The same clipping gives his tenure as DCI, May 1947 to October 1950, and reports that he wanted a congressional investigation. A second CIA-held clipping, an Enquirer editorial of 4 March 1960, independently records him as a board member of NICAP who 'has referred his conclusions to the Senate Space Committee, requesting an investigation' -- while itself disagreeing with him.

None of that requires MJ-12, and this board does not route him through it. The purported 1948 Hillenkoetter-to-Truman memo shown to be an emulation of a Marshall-to-Roosevelt letter is a claim about a document, not about the man, and nothing above depends on it. A negative finding points the same way as the Corso file: the National Security Archive's mirror of the FBI files on Hillenkoetter contains, across the sections searched, zero occurrences of NICAP, flying saucer, unidentified flying, Keyhoe, or the year 1960. His FBI file is not a UFO file.

The structural point is the ordering. A former DCI publicly contradicting the Air Force's public position, while sitting on the board of the organisation pressing for hearings, is a fact about the disclosure apparatus that the board should reach directly rather than through somebody else's narrative about a forgery.

### Why it holds its tier

Re-graded B on a global pass (rank 161 of 1057, score 49). 6 connections, 0 of them custody/witness/funding/programmatic. Axes C3/A2/D3.

### The claims on the record

- **Tier 1 documented.** A Worcester (Mass.) Gazette clipping of 1 June 1960, held in CIA records, reports Hillenkoetter declaring of the so-called flying saucers: 'The unknown objects are operating under intelligent control. It is imperative that we learn where the UFO's (unidentified flying objects) come from and what their purpose is.' Source: CIA-RDP68-00046R000200090025-2, Worcester Gazette, 1 June 1960, 'Congressional Action: Ex-C.I.A. Chief Wants UFO Probe' by Bulkley Griffin; declassified in part 2014-06-12
- **Tier 1 documented.** An Enquirer editorial of 4 March 1960, also held in CIA records, states that 'Admiral Hillenkoetter, former director of the Central Intelligence Agency, is a board member of the privately financed NICAP ... As such he has referred his conclusions to the Senate Space Committee, requesting an investigation.' Source: CIA-RDP68-00046R000200090028-9, Enquirer, 4 March 1960, editorial 'Flying Saucers Again'; declassified in part 2014-06-12
- **Tier 1 documented.** The founding proposal for Project BLUEBIRD went to him as Director of Central Intelligence. The memorandum of 5 April 1950 from the Chief of the CIA's Inspection and Security Staff is addressed to the Director of Central Intelligence and says on its face: 'In view of the extreme sensitivity of this project and its covert nature, it is deemed advisable to submit this project directly to you, rather than through the channel of the Projects Review Committee. Knowledge of this project should be restricted to the absolute minimum number of persons.' It asks him for approval and for an allocation of $65,515. Source: Chief, Inspection and Security Staff, CIA, to the Director of Central Intelligence, 'Project Bluebird', Top Secret, 5 April 1950, CIA-RDP83-01042R000800010003-1, National Security Archive John Marks Collection Box 9, https://nsarchive.gwu.edu/sites/default/files/documents/1950-04-05%20JM%20Box%209%20F5%20Replacement%20CIA%20FOIA-ocr.pdf
- **Tier 1 documented.** The same document records that Hillenkoetter 'headed the Central Intelligence Agency from May 1947 to October 1950' and that he 'wants a Congressional investigation of the UFOs'. On the wartime and immediate post-war years he is quoted: 'I know that neither Russia nor this country had anything even approaching such high speeds and maneuvers.' Source: CIA-RDP68-00046R000200090025-2, Worcester Gazette, 1 June 1960
- **Tier 1 documented.** The same editorial records its own disagreement with him: 'WE CANNOT wholly agree with the respected Vice Adm. R. H. Hillenkoetter (ret.) that the Air Force is deceiving the public' -- which preserves his position as well as the paper's. Source: CIA-RDP68-00046R000200090028-9, Enquirer, 4 March 1960
- **Tier 2 named person on the record.** The Director on that date was Hillenkoetter. The memorandum itself names only the office; the National Security Archive's catalogue record for it names him as the recipient, and this node already dates his tenure as DCI from May 1947 to October 1950 on a CIA-held clipping. The identification is Tier 2 because the document does not make it. Source: National Security Archive catalogue record for the 5 April 1950 Project Bluebird memorandum, https://nsarchive.gwu.edu/document/32717-document-02-chief-inspection-and-security-staff-us-central-intelligence-agency
- **Tier 2 named person on the record.** The National Security Archive's mirror of the FBI files on Hillenkoetter -- eighteen serials plus a main file -- contains, across the sections text-searched, zero occurrences of NICAP, 'flying saucer', 'unidentified flying', Keyhoe, or the year 1960. OCR quality on these scans is uneven, so this is a strong indication rather than a proof. Source: FBI files on Roscoe H. Hillenkoetter, National Security Archive mirror; full-text search of the sections available

## Timothy Gallaudet

Timothy Gallaudet is a person on the board; board tier A, named in sworn testimony or primary documents as directly inside the programme; dates 2024-11-13; axes C1 A2 D3; 6 connections.

In one line: Rear Admiral, USN (Ret.); former Oceanographer of the Navy; former acting NOAA Administrator.

### The longer account

Gallaudet is the flag officer who put the safety-of-flight coverup into the congressional record in the first person. Testifying before the joint House Oversight subcommittees on 13 November 2024, he described a January 2015 email from the operations officer of US Fleet Forces Command to all subordinate commanders with the subject line 'URGENT SAFETY OF FLIGHT ISSUE.' The email carried the GO FAST video and, in Gallaudet's account, asked: 'If any of you know what these are, tell me ASAP. We are having multiple near-midair collisions, and if we do not resolve it soon, we will have to shut down the exercise.' The next day, he testified, the email disappeared from his account and from other recipients' accounts without explanation. His conclusion in sworn written testimony is the sharpest available statement of the mechanism: the information 'must have been classified within a special access program managed by an intelligence agency.' This is the single most useful witness account on the board for the question of authority, because it describes not a leak or a rumour but an administrative action taken against an official Navy safety communication - a recall of a fleet-wide flight-safety warning. Gallaudet was Commander, Naval Meteorology and Oceanography Command in 2015, later Oceanographer of the Navy, then Assistant Secretary of Commerce and acting NOAA Administrator.

### What is left unexplained

A US Fleet Forces Command flight-safety message reporting 'multiple near-midair collisions' was withdrawn from the accounts of every recipient within 24 hours, and no agency has ever acknowledged doing so.

### Recorded as unverified

- The identity of the Fleet Forces Command operations officer who sent the January 2015 email.
- The exact date and message identifier of the email, and the authority under which it was recalled.
- Whether the recall was executed by Navy IT administrators, a Fleet Forces Command action, or an outside agency request.
- Whether council members are compensated, cleared, or bound by any agreement, given that all data shared with them is to be unclassified.

Affiliations recorded: US Navy (1989-2017), Rear Admiral, Commander, Naval Meteorology and Oceanography Command (2015), Oceanographer of the Navy, NOAA Deputy Administrator / acting Administrator (2017-2019), Ocean STL Consulting, CEO, US Navy, Rear Admiral (retired 2017), NOAA, Assistant Secretary and Acting Administrator (2017-2019), Ocean STL Consulting, UAP Science Advisory Council, formerly NOAA, formerly US Navy.

## Bob Fish

Bob Fish is a person on the board; board tier B, documented channel and capability, programme role indicated but not yet nailed to a primary source; dates 2015-03-06; axes C0 A1 D2; 5 connections.

In one line: Private correspondent of John Podesta in the WikiLeaks Podesta Emails, March 2015; USS Hornet Museum; self-described 1986 contractor to the White House Communications Agency.

### The longer account

A private correspondent of John Podesta's in the WikiLeaks Podesta Emails, writing from robertbfish@earthlink.net over three days in March 2015 in six emails, of which three have been opened here in full with headers. He identifies himself publicly by his work with the USS Hornet Museum, says he was a contractor to the White House Communications Agency in 1986, and offers Podesta programme names for instrument-collected UFO data. His central claim is that the USAF Defense Support Program satellite constellation collects such data. He states in the same email that he was never fully briefed into DSP and relays a single second-hand lunchroom account of a Fastwalker detection during the run-up to Desert Shield and Desert Storm.

### Why it holds its tier

Re-graded B on a global pass (rank 346 of 1057, score 35). 5 connections, 2 of them custody/witness/funding/programmatic. Axes C0/A1/D2.

### The claims on the record

- **Tier 1 documented.** In a separate email of 6 March 2015 Fish relays a claim that Project Blue Book continued after 1970, concluding 'if true, Blue Book was not disbanded - only the outer layer of the onion (the "public information layer) was stripped away in 1970.' Source: WikiLeaks, The Podesta Emails, email id 31721, 'Leslie Kean book - Blue Book', https://wikileaks.org/podesta-emails/emailid/31721
- **Tier 1 documented.** In an email forwarded by Podesta to Leslie Kean on 6 March 2015 under the subject 'Leslie Kean book - DSP program', Bob Fish wrote: 'One of the government programs that collects hard data on unidentified flying objects is the USAF DSP satellite program.' Source: WikiLeaks, The Podesta Emails, email id 30433, 'Fwd: Leslie Kean book - DSP program', https://wikileaks.org/podesta-emails/emailid/30433
- **Tier 1 documented.** In the same email Fish qualifies the claim: 'While I was never fully briefed into the DSP operation directly, I was introduced to them as the US prepared for Operation Desert Shield and Desert Storm', and the Fastwalker detection he relays is a remark made to him at lunch by a member of that group. Source: WikiLeaks, The Podesta Emails, email id 30433, https://wikileaks.org/podesta-emails/emailid/30433
- **Tier 1 documented.** Podesta's own reply to Fish the previous day was declining and non-committal in full: 'Thx for the note. Hard for me follow up at this moment but I will keep your contact information.' Source: WikiLeaks, The Podesta Emails, email id 18724, 'Re: Leslie Kean book comment', https://wikileaks.org/podesta-emails/emailid/18724
- **Tier 1 documented.** The exchange runs to six emails between 5 and 7 March 2015, ids 54211, 47433, 36957, 31721, 30433 and 18724, and no speed figure and no date in the 1980s appears in any of the three opened in full. Source: WikiLeaks, The Podesta Emails, corpus search for 'Bob Fish', https://wikileaks.org/podesta-emails/?q=%22Bob+Fish%22
- **Tier 2 named person on the record.** On the Danny Jones Podcast, episode 320, 4 August 2025, at 0:52:23, the guest who identifies himself only as UAP Gerb cited the Fish and Podesta emails for the claim that the Defense Support Program tracked UFOs in the 1980s at 22,000 mph. The three emails opened in full carry no speed figure and no date in the 1980s: Fish dates the episode to the run-up to Desert Shield and Desert Storm and writes in March 2015 that it is now 24 years later, which puts it in 1990 or 1991, and he says of himself that he was never fully briefed into the DSP operation directly. ## Gen. Charles P. Cabell

Gen. Charles P. Cabell is a person on the board; board tier B, documented channel and capability, programme role indicated but not yet nailed to a primary source; dates 1953-01-01; axes C2 A2 D2; 5 connections.

In one line: Director of Intelligence, USAF, to 1953; Deputy Director of Central Intelligence, 1953 to 1962.

### The longer account

The board already holds a document over Cabell's signature, cited as a source on the Keyhoe node, and has never had a node for the man who signed it. The two FRUS persons lists give the offices: Director of Intelligence in the Office of the Deputy Chief of Staff, U.S. Air Force, through 1953, then Deputy Director of Central Intelligence from 1953 to 1962. Those dates are the finding. He was the Air Force's intelligence chief through the reorganisation of the UFO project and only afterwards a CIA officer, which is the reverse of how the podcast corpus places him. The CIA's own historical chronology closes the second office: he resigned as DDCI on 27 December 1961, effective 31 January 1962, and was succeeded by Marshall S. Carter in March 1962.

### Why it holds its tier

Re-graded B on a global pass (rank 238 of 1057, score 41). 5 connections, 0 of them custody/witness/funding/programmatic, 1 official denial recorded against it. Axes C2/A2/D2.

### The claims on the record

- **Tier 1 documented.** He held the Air Force post in the earlier period too. The 1945-1950 volume's list of persons carries him without dates as 'Director of Intelligence, Office of the Deputy Chief of Staff, U.S. Air Force; member, Intelligence Advisory Committee'. Source: FRUS, 1945-1950, Emergence of the Intelligence Establishment, List of Persons, https://history.state.gov/historicaldocuments/frus1945-50Intel/persons
- **Tier 1 documented.** The CIA's own declassified chronology closes the CIA post: '27 DEC. Lt. Gen. C.P. Cabell resigns as DDCI (effective 31 Jan 62)', and '9 MAR. Maj. Gen. Marshall S. Carter appointed DDCI, succeeding Cabell'. The same volume records that in December 1961 'DDCI Cabell named CIA member' when USIB membership was expanded that month. Source: CIA Historical Staff Chronology 1946-65, Volume II 1956-65, June 1970, CIA-RDP85B00803R000200050003-5, declassified in part 8 July 2013, https://archive.org/details/cia-readingroom-document-cia-rdp85b00803r000200050003-5
- **Tier 1 documented.** The Department of State's list of persons for the intelligence volume gives both offices: 'Cabell, General Charles P., USAF, Director of Intelligence, Office of the Deputy Chief of Staff from 1951 to 1953; Intelligence Advisory Committee, Deputy Director of Central Intelligence from 1953 to 1962.' Source: FRUS, 1950-1955, The Intelligence Community, List of Persons, https://history.state.gov/historicaldocuments/frus1950-55Intel/persons
- **Tier 1 documented.** The board already cites a letter he sent to Donald Keyhoe. The reading room's own catalogue entry for it reads 'UNTITLED (TO DONALD KEYHOE FROM C P CABELL ACKNOWLEDGING HIS LETTER OF MARCH 1', reading-room date 5 February 1958. The scan's OCR is close to unusable: the signature block resolves only far enough to read C. P. Cabell over the title Deputy Director, and no sentence of the body is legible. Nothing is quoted from it here. Source: CIA reading room document 0000015305, https://archive.org/details/cia-readingroom-document-0000015305
- **Tier 2 named person on the record.** On the Danny Jones Podcast #406, 19 June 2026, at 1:24:32, Matthew Ehret said Project Blue Book was launched under 'CIA Deputy Director Charles Cabell' at the urging of Sir Henry Tizard's UK flying saucer working party, and that Cabell was fired by John F. Kennedy in 1961. The opened records contradict the office: at Blue Book's launch in March 1952 he was the Air Force's Director of Intelligence, and he joined CIA as Deputy Director only in 1953. The CIA's own chronology records a resignation, not a dismissal, on 27 December 1961 effective 31 January 1962. Source: Danny Jones Podcast #406, 'They Want to Replace God', 19 June 2026, at 1:24:32 to 1:25:21; contradicted against FRUS, 1950-1955, The Intelligence Community, List of Persons, https://history.state.gov/historicaldocuments/frus1950-55Intel/persons

## Charles E. Schumer

Charles E. Schumer is a person on the board; board tier B, documented channel and capability, programme role indicated but not yet nailed to a primary source; dates 2023-07-13; axes C0 A2 D3; 5 connections.

In one line: Senate Majority Leader (2021-2025); sponsor, UAP Disclosure Act.

### The longer account

Schumer is the author of the only piece of United States legislation ever drafted on the explicit premise that the Federal Government holds recovered non-human technology and has hidden it. On 13 July 2023, as Senate Majority Leader, he submitted S.Amdt. 797 to S. 2226, the FY2024 NDAA, for himself and Senators Rounds, Rubio, Gillibrand, Young and Heinrich; its text was printed in the Congressional Record at Vol. 169 No. 120, pages S2953-S2959. The amendment carried a legal definition of 'non-human intelligence,' a legal definition of 'legacy program' reaching private contractors and academic institutions, a nine-member Records Review Board appointed by the President with the advice and consent of the Senate, and, at Section __10(a), later Section 9010(a) of Division G of the engrossed bill, a command that 'The Federal Government shall exercise eminent domain over any and all recovered technologies of unknown origin and biological evidence of non-human intelligence that may be controlled by private persons or entities in the interests of the public good.' The Senate passed S. 2226 carrying that division on 27 July 2023. By the time the conference product was signed on 22 December 2023 the Review Board, the eminent domain clause, the Controlled Disclosure Campaign Plan and the $20,000,000 authorization were gone; only Sections 1841-1843 survived. Schumer framed the effort as inheriting Harry Reid's project, telling reporters he was 'honored to carry on the legacy of my mentor and dear friend Harry Reid,' and after the gutting said it was 'really an outrage the House didn't work with us on adopting our proposal for a review board.' He re-filed the full text through Rounds as S.Amdt. 2610 in July 2024. It never got a vote.

### Recorded as unverified

- Schumer has never given a floor speech on the UAP Disclosure Act on the day he filed it: a full-text search of the Congressional Record for 13 July 2023 returns the word 'anomalous' only inside printed amendment texts (SA 542, SA 666, SA 797), not in any transcribed Senate remarks.

Affiliations recorded: U.S. Senate, New York (1999- ), Senate Majority Leader (2021-2025), Senate Minority Leader (2025- ).

### Why it holds its tier

Re-graded B on a global pass (rank 253 of 1057, score 40). 5 connections, 1 of them custody/witness/funding/programmatic, 1 official denial recorded against it. Axes C0/A2/D3.

- **Tier 1 documented.** Sponsor of record of S.Amdt. 797 to S. 2226: congress.gov lists 'Sponsor: Sen. Schumer, Charles E. [D-NY] (Submitted 07/13/2023)'. ## Daniel P. Sheehan

Daniel P. Sheehan is a person on the board; board tier C, contextual, real and relevant, no direct programme linkage on the record yet; dates 2023-11-27; axes C1 A0 D3; 5 connections.

In one line: Constitutional attorney; lobbied the FY2024 NDAA conference.

### The longer account

Daniel Sheehan is the legal architecture of the disclosure movement. A Harvard College and Harvard Law graduate who worked the Pentagon Papers case, Watergate, Silkwood, Greensboro and the La Penca bombing suit, he founded the Christic Institute and the Romero Institute and now runs the New Paradigm Institute, which lobbies directly for the UAP Disclosure Act. He was counsel to Harvard psychiatrist John Mack and to Steven Greer's Disclosure Project, and he represents Luis Elizondo in litigation against the Department of Defense. His significance for this board is that he is the person converting testimony into legal instruments - eminent domain provisions, records-review boards, whistleblower protection - and the New Paradigm Institute is running the campaign to get the Senate to pass the UAPDA after House action. Sheehan's record also carries a real liability: the Avirgan v. Hull suit was dismissed in 1988 with Rule 11 sanctions of over a million dollars against the Christic Institute, and the presiding judge criticised his reliance on witnesses who were dead, unwilling or one person removed from the facts. That is a hostile-audit vulnerability the board should carry openly, because the same evidentiary pattern - second-hand witnesses, unavailable primaries - recurs in the UAP context.

### Where the record conflicts

- Judicially sanctioned in 1988 for reliance on unavailable and second-hand witnesses - a pattern critics apply to his UAP advocacy.

Affiliations recorded: New Paradigm Institute (founder), Christic Institute (co-founder), Romero Institute (Chief Counsel), Christic Institute (founder), Counsel to Luis Elizondo v. DoD.

### Why it holds its tier

Re-graded C on a global pass (rank 579 of 1057, score 27). 5 connections, 1 of them custody/witness/funding/programmatic. Axes C1/A0/D3.

### The claims on the record

- **Tier 2 named person on the record.** In Avirgan v. Hull, Judge James Lawrence King dismissed the suit on 23 June 1988 and ordered the Christic Institute to pay $955,000 in attorney's fees and $79,500 in costs under Rule 11. Source: Wikipedia, 'Daniel Sheehan (attorney)'; US District Court, Southern District of Florida, DEMOTED from Tier 1 (cited to a secondary/encyclopedic source); needs primary replacement

## David Fravor

David Fravor is a person on the board; board tier B, documented channel and capability, programme role indicated but not yet nailed to a primary source; dates 2023-07-26; axes C0 A1 D3; 5 connections.

In one line: CDR, USN (Ret.); Commanding Officer, VFA-41 Black Aces; 14 Nov 2004 Nimitz intercept.

### The longer account

Fravor is the aviator whose single intercept broke the observational record open. In November 2004 he was Commanding Officer of Strike Fighter Squadron Forty-One, the Black Aces, attached to Carrier Air Wing Eleven aboard USS Nimitz. In his sworn written statement to the House Oversight Subcommittee on National Security, the Border and Foreign Affairs on 26 July 2023 he described being vectored off a training intercept to investigate a contact the cruiser USS Princeton had been tracking, and finding 'a small white Tic Tac shaped object with the longitudinal axis pointing N/S and moving very abruptly over the white water,' with 'no Rotors, No Rotor wash, or any visible flight control surfaces.' He testified the Princeton had been watching objects descend from above 80,000 feet to 20,000 feet 'for the previous 2 weeks.' The single most load-bearing line in his statement is a negative one: 'the incident was never investigated, none of my crew were ever questioned, tapes were never taken.' That sentence is not a denial of the coverup - it is evidence of the specific shape of it. The Navy's own range fouler report on the incident, released via FOIA on 13 January 2023, records that 'Reporting terminated at 3rd Fleet, there was no process to share the reporting.' The event stayed dormant until Jay Stratton contacted Fravor in 2009 to investigate it for a DoD program, and until the New York Times published the FLIR1 video in December 2017. Fravor told 60 Minutes on 16 May 2021 the object 'was aware we were there' and that 'it just disappears. Like, gone.'

### What is left unexplained

A 46-foot object with no visible propulsion or control surfaces that departed the intercept faster than an F/A-18F could track, then reappeared at the squadron's undisclosed CAP point, remains unexplained in the Navy's own 2023 range fouler release.

### Where the record conflicts

- Fravor's sworn statement that 'tapes were never taken' is in direct tension with the accounts attributed to USS Princeton crewmen Kevin Day and Gary Voorhis that recording media were removed by unidentified personnel who arrived by helicopter. Both can be true if the collection was aimed at Princeton's Aegis and CEC data and at Nimitz's ATFLIR tape library rather than at VFA-41 aircrew, whom Fravor confirms were never even debriefed.

Affiliations recorded: US Navy (1989-2006), VFA-41 Black Aces, Commanding Officer (2004), Carrier Air Wing Eleven / USS Nimitz (2004).

### Why it holds its tier

Re-graded B on a global pass (rank 199 of 1057, score 44). 6 connections, 5 of them custody/witness/funding/programmatic. Axes C0/A1/D3.

### The claims on the record

- **Tier 1 documented.** 'In Nov 2004, I was the Commanding Officer of Strike Fighter Squadron Forty-One' and encountered 'a small white Tic Tac shaped object ... There were no Rotors, No Rotor wash, or any visible flight control surfaces.' Source: David Fravor, written statement for the House Oversight Subcommittee on National Security, the Border and Foreign Affairs, 26 July 2023
- **Tier 1 documented.** 'the incident was never investigated, none of my crew were ever questioned, tapes were never taken.' Source: David Fravor, written statement to House Oversight, 26 July 2023
- **Tier 1 documented.** USS Princeton had been tracking the objects on Aegis for two weeks, descending from above 80,000 ft to 20,000 ft. Source: David Fravor, written statement to House Oversight, 26 July 2023
- **Tier 1 documented.** Witness of record at the 26 July 2023 House Oversight hearing: 'Commander David Fravor (Ret.), Former Commanding Officer, United States Navy'. Written statement, Truth in Testimony form and bio filed in the House Committee Repository, event 116282. Source: CHRG-118hhrg53022 CONTENTS; docs.house.gov EventID 116282
- **Tier 2 named person on the record.** 'It was aware we were there' and 'It just disappears. Like, gone.' Source: David Fravor, CBS 60 Minutes, 16 May 2021
