## Maj. Donald E. Keyhoe, USMC (Ret.)

Maj. Donald E. Keyhoe, USMC (Ret.) is a person on the board; board tier B, documented channel and capability, programme role indicated but not yet nailed to a primary source; dates 1957-09-20; axes C1 A1 D2; 5 connections.

In one line: Director, National Investigations Committee on Aerial Phenomena, by September 1957 and still in post May 1964.

### The longer account

Keyhoe ran the civilian organisation the board already carries as nicap, and he is the hinge between it and the former Director of Central Intelligence who sat on its board. A US Air Force headquarters letter of 20 September 1957 is addressed to him as Director NICAP at the organisation's Connecticut Avenue address, and NICAP's 1964 volume The UFO Evidence, in the copy the CIA filed, carries him on its masthead as director. He was a US Naval Academy graduate of 1920 and a former Marine Corps aircraft and balloon pilot, and the volume says plainly that his Navy connections are why several of its military cases are in it. In 1958 the CIA recorded a decision to forward his UFO inquiries to the Air Force's public information office to be answered there, and to treat that as a precedent for all such inquiries. No record opened here gives an end date for his directorship.

### Recorded as unverified

- The 1969 end of his NICAP directorship. No opened record gives an end date; the last opened record of him in post is the May 1964 volume.
- The 22 January 1958 CBS broadcast in which his audio is said to have been cut mid-sentence. No broadcast record, network transcript or contemporaneous government document reached from here mentions it, and the 1964 volume, searched for 'CBS', 'Armstrong Circle' and 'censored', carries nothing on it.

### Why it holds its tier

Re-graded B on a global pass (rank 208 of 1057, score 43). 5 connections, 0 of them custody/witness/funding/programmatic, 3 official denials recorded against it. Axes C1/A1/D2.

### The claims on the record

- **Tier 1 documented.** He held the post by September 1957 on a government correspondent's own address line. A letter on the letterhead 'Hq Sq Sec, First Air Force, Mitchel Air Force Base, NY', dated 20 September 1957, is addressed to 'Mr. Donald E. Keyhoe, Director NICAP, 1536 Connecticut Ave, N. W., Washington 6, D. C.' and transmits a quoted Air Force report of eight round objects sighted at Fort Devens, Massachusetts on 17 September 1957. Source: First Air Force letter of 20 September 1957, reproduced in The UFO Evidence, 1964, in CIA-RDP81R00560R000100010001-0, https://archive.org/details/cia-readingroom-document-cia-rdp81r00560r000100010001-0
- **Tier 1 documented.** He put the question to the Director of Central Intelligence directly. An internal CIA memorandum of January 1959 quotes his memorandum of 13 March 1958 to the DCI asking whether CIA representatives had told UFO witnesses not to make their sightings public, and on what authority. The scan is partly illegible and no wording is quoted from it here. Source: CIA reading room document 0000015416, 'Inquiry by Major Donald E. Keyhoe (deleted)', reading-room date 4 January 1959, https://archive.org/details/cia-readingroom-document-0000015416
- **Tier 1 documented.** NICAP's own 1964 volume names him as its head on the masthead: 'MAJ. DONALD E. KEYHOE, USMC (RET.), DIRECTOR', printed under the organisation's name and its 1536 Connecticut Avenue NW address, above the Board of Governors list. Source: The UFO Evidence, National Investigations Committee on Aerial Phenomena, Washington DC, May 1964, Richard H. Hall ed., in the CIA's filed copy CIA-RDP81R00560R000100010001-0, https://archive.org/details/cia-readingroom-document-cia-rdp81r00560r000100010001-0
- **Tier 1 documented.** The CIA recorded a standing rule for handling him. A memorandum for record on 'Comments on Letters Dealing with Unidentified Flying Objects' notes that the Air Force was 'most insistent' on answering his letters itself, that 'the Air Force is endeavoring to impress upon Major Keyhoe and others in a similar position, that his office is the proper place to come for such information', and concludes: 'I think we can use this procedure as a precedent henceforth in all inquiries regarding UFO's.' The scan's OCR is imperfect and renders his surname inconsistently; the quoted sentences were legible. Source: CIA memorandum for record, 'Comments on Letters Dealing with Unidentified Flying Objects', CIA reading room document 0005516043, reading-room date 4 April 1958, https://archive.org/details/cia-readingroom-document-0005516043
- **Tier 1 documented.** The volume states the Hillenkoetter connection as a personal one: 'Vice Admiral R. H. Hillenkoetter, USN (Ret.), a long-time acquaintance of Major Keyhoe, also served on the NICAP Board of Governors for five years.' It gives Keyhoe's own background as 'a graduate of the U. S. Naval Academy (class of 1920) and former Marine Corps aircraft and balloon pilot' and says his acquaintance with serving and retired officers is why several of its military cases could be detailed. Source: The UFO Evidence, 1964, in CIA-RDP81R00560R000100010001-0, https://archive.org/details/cia-readingroom-document-cia-rdp81r00560r000100010001-0

## Dylan Borland

Dylan Borland is a person on the board; board tier A, named in sworn testimony or primary documents as directly inside the programme; dates 2025-09-09; axes C1 A2 D3; 5 connections.

In one line: USAF veteran; UAP witness testifying to House Oversight, Sept 2025.

### The longer account

Dylan Borland is a US Air Force veteran who testified publicly for the first time on 9 September 2025 before the House Oversight Task Force on the Declassification of Federal Secrets, describing a 2012 UAP encounter at Langley Air Force Base and, more consequentially for the record, what happened to him afterwards. His testimony to the task force: 'My professional career was deliberately obstructed, and I have endured sustained reprisals.' He described witnesses in his position as feeling 'discarded, isolated, hopeless' after coming forward. Borland's value on this board is as a reprisal datum rather than a phenomenology datum: the hearing was titled 'Restoring Public Trust Through UAP Transparency and Whistleblower Protection,' and the minority witness was POGO's Joe Spielberger on whistleblower channels, which means the majority and minority converged on the proposition that the existing protections do not work for this class of witness. Langley is also not an incidental location - it is the base whose December 2023 drone incursions became a congressional issue in their own right, and a 2012 UAP encounter there sits inside the same air-defence problem set.

## Jared Evan Moskowitz

Jared Evan Moskowitz is a person on the board; board tier B, documented channel and capability, programme role indicated but not yet nailed to a primary source; dates 2025-09-09; axes C0 A1 D3; 5 connections.

In one line: U.S. Rep. (D-FL-23); UAP Caucus; the Democratic anchor of House disclosure.

### The longer account

Moskowitz is the reason House UAP oversight is bipartisan rather than a Republican project, and he is the member who has most consistently framed it as a money question. At the July 2023 hearing he thanked Burchett and Luna for pushing the hearing into existence and read Chuck Schumer's language into the record: 'The American public has a right to learn about technologies of unknown origins, nonhuman intelligence, and unexplainable phenomena.' His questioning went straight at the financing - he asked Grusch directly whether US corporations are overcharging the government for technology and whether the excess is funding these programs, which is the black-budget mechanism the whole thesis rests on. In December 2023 he gave the cleanest statement of the House's structural complaint: 'The problem is when we ask those questions, rather than being provided information that would prove it false, they stonewall the information, and that is what piques the interest.' In November 2024 he served as Vice Ranking Member at the joint subcommittee hearing. At the 9 September 2025 Task Force hearing, waived on, he said the thing that has been quoted most since: 'I don't really know what is true, I don't know, on this subject, but I do know when we're being lied to and we are definitely being lied to, there's just no doubt about that.' He is a cosponsor of Burchett's H.R. 1187 UAP Transparency Act and appeared at the 9 June 2026 Capitol disclosure event - 'Disclosure today, disclosure tomorrow. The American people deserve to know.'

- Which specific IR&D accounts or contractors Grusch had in mind; he offered specifics only in closed session.

## John D. Podesta

John D. Podesta is a person on the board; board tier B, documented channel and capability, programme role indicated but not yet nailed to a primary source; dates 2002-10-22; axes C0 A2 D3; 5 connections.

In one line: White House Staff Secretary 1993-95, Deputy Chief of Staff 1997-98, Chief of Staff 1998-2001; Counselor to the President 2014-15.

### The longer account

Podesta is the only person to have held senior White House staff positions in two administrations and to have publicly and repeatedly demanded UFO declassification. He was Staff Secretary during the years the Rockefeller Initiative was working the Clinton White House and Chief of Staff when the administration ended without acting on it. On 22 October 2002, out of office, he appeared at a National Press Club event for the Coalition for Freedom of Information and called for the government to declassify its UFO records, framing it in the language of open government rather than belief - 'It's time to find out what the truth really is that's out there.' He returned to the White House as Counselor to President Obama in 2014 and, on leaving in February 2015, wrote publicly that his biggest failure of that year was 'once again not securing the disclosure of the UFO files.' Podesta's arc is the cleanest available demonstration that proximity to the presidency is not proximity to the compartments: a man who ran the White House twice could not get the files, and said so out loud. The Clinton Library's own UFO FOIA collections - Greer/CSETI correspondence (2006-0474-F), the Roswell file (2006-0476-F), the Firmage email file (2008-0359-F) - show what did reach the building: petitions and press, not programme records.

### What is left unexplained

Two terms as a senior White House official produced no access to programme records. Either the records do not exist or they sit outside the chain a Chief of Staff can reach.

Affiliations recorded: The White House (1993-2001; 2014-2015), Center for American Progress (founder), Coalition for Freedom of Information (2002).

### Why it holds its tier

Re-graded B on a global pass (rank 483 of 1057, score 30). 5 connections, 2 of them custody/witness/funding/programmatic. Axes C0/A2/D3.

### The claims on the record

- **Tier 1 documented.** The Clinton White House's UFO-related FOIA collections consist of external correspondence - Greer/CSETI material (2006-0474-F), Roswell public correspondence and the GAO report (2006-0476-F), and Joe Firmage email (2008-0359-F) - not intelligence programme records. Source: Clinton Digital Library collection finding aids
- **Tier 2 named person on the record.** On leaving the Obama White House in February 2015, Podesta stated publicly that his biggest failure of the year was 'once again not securing the disclosure of the UFO files.' Source: John Podesta, public statement, 13 Feb 2015
- **Tier 2 named person on the record.** Podesta publicly called for the declassification of US government UFO records at a National Press Club event on 22 October 2002 organised by the Coalition for Freedom of Information. ## Kevin H. Knuth

Kevin H. Knuth is a person on the board; board tier C, contextual, real and relevant, no direct programme linkage on the record yet; dates 2019-09-25; axes C0 A0 D2; 5 connections.

In one line: Professor of Physics, University at Albany (SUNY); former NASA Ames research scientist; Editor-in-Chief, Entropy; Vice President, UAPx; research affiliate, The Galileo Project.

### The longer account

Knuth wrote the paper that made the kinematics argument citable. A physicist with a PhD from Minnesota (1995) who spent 2001-2005 at NASA Ames designing AI algorithms for astrophysical data analysis before joining Albany in 2005, he is Editor-in-Chief of the MDPI journal Entropy and works in information physics and Bayesian inference. In September 2019 he published, with Robert Powell of the Scientific Coalition for UAP Studies and Peter Reali, 'Estimating Flight Characteristics of Anomalous Unidentified Aerial Vehicles' in Entropy 21(10):939, deriving accelerations, speeds and required power from four documented cases including the 2004 Nimitz encounter. Whatever one thinks of the inputs, the paper did something no amount of testimony could: it put numbers with error bars into a peer-reviewed physics journal, and every subsequent argument about whether the observed performance is achievable with known propulsion has had to engage with it. He is Vice President of UAPx, a research affiliate of the Galileo Project, and co-author of the 2025 UAPx field-expedition paper. The editorship of Entropy is worth noting plainly: a journal editor publishing in his own journal invites the obvious criticism, and the paper's critics have made it.

### Where the record conflicts

- Knuth's editorship of Entropy, the journal in which the 2019 paper appeared, is a standing criticism of that paper.

Affiliations recorded: NASA Ames Research Center (2001-2005), University at Albany (SUNY) (2005- ; Professor 2023), Entropy - Editor-in-Chief (2012- ), UAPx - Vice President, The Galileo Project - research affiliate, Scientific Coalition for UAP Studies - collaborator.

## Kevin D. Randle

Kevin D. Randle is a person on the board; board tier B, documented channel and capability, programme role indicated but not yet nailed to a primary source; dates 1949-06-04; axes C0 A1 D3; 5 connections.

In one line: Lt Col (ret.), USAF/Army/Iowa NG intelligence officer; Roswell author.

### The longer account

Kevin Douglas Randle (born 4 June 1949) is the closest thing the retrieval literature has to an internal auditor with a security background. He flew UH-1s with the 116th and 187th Assault Helicopter Companies in Vietnam, served in the Air Force as a public affairs officer, general's aide and intelligence officer, completed Air Force Reserve service as a captain and director of intelligence for an airlift group, then joined the Iowa National Guard as an intelligence officer after 11 September 2001, deploying with the 234th Signal Battalion to Baghdad International Airport in June 2003 and retiring as a lieutenant colonel in 2009. With Donald R. Schmitt he wrote 'UFO Crash at Roswell' (1991) and 'The Truth About the UFO Crash at Roswell' (1994), the two books that drove Roswell into congressional attention and produced the Air Force counter-reports. Randle is important to this board precisely because he moved against the current: he demolished the Majestic 12 documents in 'Conclusions on Operation Majestic Twelve' (1994) and 'Case MJ-12' (2002) while Friedman defended them, he withdrew his and Schmitt's support for witnesses who did not hold up, and he later told Skeptical Inquirer's Robert Sheaffer that he sets a very high bar for evidence, that most abductees are describing sleep paralysis, and eventually that he was 'no longer sure what actually happened' at Roswell and that the case for extraterrestrial involvement 'is no longer robust.' He also wrote 'Project Moon Dust' (1999), 'A History of UFO Crashes' (1995) and 'Crash: When UFOs Fall From the Sky' (2010), which is the field's most systematic case-by-case survey.

### Where the record conflicts

- Randle and Schmitt publicly split; Randle later repudiated several witnesses the two of them had jointly relied on, including Frank Kaufmann and Glenn Dennis.

Affiliations recorded: US Army, Vietnam (116th and 187th Assault Helicopter Companies), US Air Force / Air Force Reserve, intelligence officer, Iowa National Guard, 234th Signal Battalion, Iraq 2003-2004, J. Allen Hynek Center for UFO Studies.

### Why it holds its tier

Re-graded B on a global pass (rank 431 of 1057, score 32). 5 connections, 0 of them custody/witness/funding/programmatic, 3 official denials recorded against it. Axes C0/A1/D3.

## Leonard H. Stringfield

Leonard H. Stringfield is a person on the board; board tier C, contextual, real and relevant, no direct programme linkage on the record yet; dates 1994-12-18; axes C0 A0 D3; 5 connections.

In one line: Founder, CRIFO; MUFON director of public relations; author, UFO Crash/Retrieval Status Reports I-VII.

### The longer account

Leonard H. Stringfield (1920-1994) is the origin point of the crash-retrieval literature as a body of work rather than a rumour. A former Army Air Forces intelligence officer in the Pacific in World War II, he founded CRIFO (Civilian Research, Interplanetary Flying Objects) in Cincinnati in the 1950s and served 1967-1969 as an Early Warning Coordinator feeding sighting reports to the Condon Committee, which put him inside the official investigative loop before he turned on it. After references to crashes and recovered bodies in his 1977 Doubleday book 'Situation Red, The UFO Siege' produced an unexpected flow of new informants, he delivered 'Retrievals of the Third Kind: A Case Study of Alleged UFOs and Occupants in Military Custody' at the ninth annual MUFON Symposium in Dayton, Ohio, on 29 July 1978. That paper opened the modern retrieval literature and he then produced six further Status Reports between January 1980 and February 1994, each a numbered, dated, case-coded compilation of testimony from sources he anonymised behind letter-number codes. His method is the load-bearing weakness and the load-bearing strength of the whole field: he protected sources absolutely, which is why they talked to him, and which is also why almost nothing he published can be independently audited. Stringfield himself said so, opening Status Report IV with a section titled 'The Burden of Proof' in which he wrote that 'we, in the domain of public research, do not have that exalted proof' and that affidavits, leaked documents and photographs do not constitute proof. He worked with the Fund for UFO Research, MUFON, CUFOS and the Mid-Ohio Research Associates, and Richard Hall edited Status Report VI. He died of lung cancer on 18 December 1994, ten months after the last report.

### Where the record conflicts

- Stringfield's sources are almost entirely anonymous. Critics inside ufology, including Kevin Randle, have argued that unverifiable anonymous testimony cannot carry the weight the reports place on it.

### Recorded as unverified

- Stringfield's own wartime Army Air Forces intelligence assignment has not been confirmed here against a service record.
- Which, if any, of the anonymised Status Report informants were later identified by name.

Affiliations recorded: CRIFO (Civilian Research, Interplanetary Flying Objects), founder, MUFON, Director of Public Relations, Condon Committee Early Warning Network coordinator, 1967-1969, Mid-Ohio Research Associates.

### Why it holds its tier

Re-graded C on a global pass (rank 753 of 1057, score 21). 5 connections, 1 of them custody/witness/funding/programmatic. Axes C0/A0/D3.

### The claims on the record

- **Tier 2 named person on the record, allegation shaped wording as stored.** Status Report IV is dated June 1985 and is titled 'UFO CRASH RETRIEVALS STATUS REPORT IV: THE FATAL ENCOUNTER AT FT. DIX-MCGUIRE', concerning an alleged 18 January 1978 incident at McGuire AFB / Fort Dix reported by a USAF Security Police sergeant first published as Case A3 in Status Report III. Source: Stringfield, Status Report IV, title page and abstract (scan), DEMOTED from Tier 1 (node carries no primary-record URL); needs primary replacement
- **Tier 2 named person on the record.** Status Report VI, 'The Inner Sanctum', is dated July 1991 and carries the imprint 'Published by Leonard H. Stringfield, 4412 Grove Avenue, Cincinnati, Ohio 45227' with acknowledgement to Richard Hall for editorial critique. Source: Stringfield, Status Report VI, title and copyright page (scan), DEMOTED from Tier 1 (node carries no primary-record URL); needs primary replacement
- **Tier 2 named person on the record.** Stringfield stated in Status Report IV that public UFO research does not possess proof, and that affidavits, leaked government documents and photographs do not constitute proof - an unusually explicit self-assessment by the field's founding retrieval researcher. Source: Stringfield, Status Report IV, 'Prologue: The Burden of Proof' (scan)
- **Tier 3 stated inference.** Stringfield's anonymised letter-number case coding is the structural reason the retrieval corpus resists tier-1 verification: the coding was designed to protect sources from precisely the security apparatus whose existence the corpus asserts, and it succeeded, at the cost of making the corpus unauditable. Reasoning: the codes are internally consistent across seven reports but map to no released personnel record. Source: Inference from the Status Report corpus

## Matthew Szydagis

Matthew Szydagis is a person on the board; board tier C, contextual, real and relevant, no direct programme linkage on the record yet; dates 2025-06-01; axes C0 A1 D2; 5 connections.

In one line: Associate Professor of Physics, University at Albany (SUNY); dark-matter instrumentation physicist (LUX/LZ, NEST); co-founder of UAPx; member, UAP Science Advisory Council (2026).

### The longer account

Szydagis is the instrumentation conscience of the field. His day job is liquid-xenon dark-matter detection - he is a principal author of the Noble Element Simulation Technique (NEST) and has published on xenon bubble chambers and low-energy nuclear recoils - which means his professional standard for what counts as a detection is set by an experiment that has spent decades not finding something. He carried that standard into UAP work by co-founding UAPx with Kevin Knuth and running an instrumented field campaign off Catalina Island, publishing 'Initial results from the first field expedition of UAPx to study unidentified anomalous phenomena' in Progress in Aerospace Sciences 156:101099 in June 2025 with Knuth, Benjamin Kugielsky and Cecilia Levy. That paper matters less for what it found than for what it establishes: a multi-instrument, multi-band, published protocol with stated false-positive handling, in a mainstream aerospace journal, done by people who build particle detectors. He also published in Limina, the journal of UAP studies, in March 2025 on disclosure timelines. In June 2026 he was named to the UAP Science Advisory Council in the instrumentation and data-collection role, which puts a working detector physicist on the government's outside science body.

### Recorded as unverified

- Whether UAPx has ever been given access to government sensor data.

Affiliations recorded: University at Albany (SUNY), Department of Physics, LUX / LZ dark matter collaborations; NEST, UAPx (co-founder), UAP Science Advisory Council (2026- ).

### Why it holds its tier

Re-graded C on a global pass (rank 852 of 1057, score 17). 5 connections, 0 of them custody/witness/funding/programmatic. Axes C0/A1/D2.

### The claims on the record

- **Tier 1 documented.** Szydagis is first author of 'Initial results from the first field expedition of UAPx to study unidentified anomalous phenomena', Progress in Aerospace Sciences 156:101099 (June 2025), with Kevin H. Knuth, Benjamin Kugielsky and Cecilia Levy. Source: Progress in Aerospace Sciences, DOI 10.1016/j.paerosci.2025.101099
- **Tier 2 named person on the record.** Szydagis was named to the UAP Science Advisory Council in June 2026 with responsibility for instrumentation and data collection. Source: Avi Loeb, Medium, 29 June 2026; Skeptics Society, 15 June 2026

### Its strongest connections

The link reads: Employs Szydagis; Associate Professor of Physics, with UAP listed as a departmental research interest. The department's own faculty page for Szydagis lists his research interests as 'Experimental astroparticle physics, in particular direct detection of dark matter WIMPs (Weakly Interacting Massive Particles) and general detector development for rare event searches, neutron activation, and Unidentified Anomalous Phenomena'. That the phrase appears on a university department page, not a personal site, is the load-bearing fact: UAP is here a declared institutional research interest rather than a private hobby. ## Richard J. Genik II

Richard J. Genik II is a person on the board; board tier A, named in sworn testimony or primary documents as directly inside the programme; dates 2010-12-15; axes C1 A2 D3; 5 connections.

In one line: Wayne State University School of Medicine; author of three DIRDs including 'Cognitive Limits on Simultaneous Control of Multiple Unmanned Spacecraft' (DIA-08-1101-001).

### The longer account

Richard Genik wrote three of the thirty-eight DIRDs, more than anyone except Eric Davis, and all three are human-factors papers rather than physics papers. The DIA list credits him with "Technological Applications to Controlling External Devices in the Absence of Limb-Operated Interfaces" (item 20, released as "Technological Approaches to..."), "Cognitive Limits on Simultaneous Control of Multiple Unmanned Spacecraft" (item 28), and "Quantum Computing and Utilizing Organic Molecules in Automation Technology" (item 33). His affiliation on the list is Wayne State University, which is where the human-effects strand of AAWSAP concentrated.

That concentration is the reason he matters to this board. Wayne State is also the institution the DIA list gives for Christopher "Kit" Green, author of the field-effects-on-biological-tissues DIRD. Two of the thirty-eight papers, and three counting Genik's second and third, were written inside one medical school, and the human-effects line of AAWSAP inquiry runs through it. Genik is a physicist by training, with a PhD in physics from Michigan State University and a BS in applied physics from Wayne State, working in a psychiatry and behavioural neurosciences department on functional MRI measurement of cognitive workload in naturalistic multitasking environments. He directs Wayne State's Emergent Technology Research Division. He was one of fifteen experts selected by the US Army to advise on how the service should direct its neuroscience research funding over a twenty-year horizon.

His DIRD on limb-independent device control is DIA-08-1003-012, dated 23 March 2010 with an Intelligence Cut-Off Date of 1 December 2009, in the Acquisition Threat Support series. In substance it is a brain-machine interface survey; the KLAS-TV list of the same document, published July 2018, titles it simply "Brain-Machine Interfaces." That is the shortest description of what AAWSAP wanted from Wayne State: a forecast of how a pilot might fly something without hands.

Genik has no documented custody proximity and no documented special access. He is wired here as an author and as the connective tissue between the DIRD series and the Wayne State human-effects cluster.

### Why it holds its tier

Re-graded A on a global pass (rank 138 of 1057, score 53). 5 connections, 0 of them custody/witness/funding/programmatic. Axes C1/A2/D3.

### The claims on the record

- **Tier 1 documented.** Named on the DIA list of AATIP/AAWSAP products supplied to the Senate Armed Services Committee as author of 'Quantum Computing and Utilizing Organic Molecules in Automation Technology', affiliation Wayne State University. Source: https://irp.fas.org/dia/aatip-list.pdf
- **Tier 1 documented.** Named on the same DIA list as author of item 20, 'Technological Approaches to Controlling External Devices in the Absence of Limb-Operated Interfaces', affiliation Wayne State University, making him a multi-deliverable AAWSAP contributor. ## Richard M. Bissell Jr.

Richard M. Bissell Jr. is a person on the board; board tier B, documented channel and capability, programme role indicated but not yet nailed to a primary source; dates 1955-04-12; axes C1 A3 D1; 5 connections.

In one line: CIA Special Assistant for Planning and Coordination from 1954; ran the U-2 project and selected Groom Lake.

### The longer account

Richard Bissell is where the documented record of Area 51 begins, and the board's Nevada material has until now had no origin point. The CIA's own History Staff monograph, in its fuller 2013 release, records that Allen Dulles put him in charge of the U-2 project on 26 November 1954; that on 12 April 1955 he flew over Nevada with Osmund Ritland, Kelly Johnson and Tony LeVier and spotted the airstrip at Groom Lake; that on finding it lay outside the AEC's proving ground he asked the Commission to absorb it, which Chairman Lewis Strauss agreed to and Eisenhower approved; and that the strip's map designation was Area 51. The same monograph records that Blue Book investigators checked their UFO reports against the project's flight logs and were not permitted to tell correspondents the cause. Nothing in it touches the deathbed briefing claim the Michels corpus routes through him, and that claim must not travel with this node.

### Recorded as unverified

- The 1933 Magenta briefing claim two Michels lead maps route through Bissell on a single deathbed account. His name appears 193 times in the CIA monograph and not once in any such context; the claim must not travel with this node.

### Why it holds its tier

Re-graded B on a global pass (rank 218 of 1057, score 42). 5 connections, 1 of them custody/witness/funding/programmatic, 1 official denial recorded against it. Axes C1/A3/D1.

### The claims on the record

- **Tier 1 documented.** Area 51 exists because he asked for it: 'Bissell and Miller asked the Atomic Energy Commission to add the Groom Lake area to its real estate holdings in Nevada. AEC Chairman Adm. Lewis Strauss readily agreed, and President Eisenhower also approved the addition of this strip of wasteland, known by its map designation as Area 51, to the Nevada Test Site.' Source: Pedlow and Welzenbach, The Central Intelligence Agency and Overhead Reconnaissance: The U-2 and OXCART Programs, 1954-1974, CIA History Staff, 2013 release, https://archive.org/download/cia-readingroom-document-0000190094/0000190094_djvu.txt, chapter 2
- **Tier 1 documented.** Dulles gave him the U-2 project by hand: 'On 26 November 1954, the day after Thanksgiving, Allen Dulles called his special assistant, Richard Bissell, into his office to tell him that President Eisenhower had just approved a very secret program and that Dulles wanted Bissell to take charge of it.' Source: Pedlow and Welzenbach, The Central Intelligence Agency and Overhead Reconnaissance: The U-2 and OXCART Programs, 1954-1974, CIA History Staff, 2013 release, https://archive.org/download/cia-readingroom-document-0000190094/0000190094_djvu.txt
- **Tier 1 documented.** He came to the Agency as an economist and a Marshall Plan administrator, not an operator: a Yale doctorate in 1939, a full professorship at MIT in 1948, executive officer of the Combined Shipping Adjustment Board in the war and deputy director of the Marshall Plan from 1948 to the end of 1951. 'Bissell joined the Agency in late January 1954.' Source: Pedlow and Welzenbach, The Central Intelligence Agency and Overhead Reconnaissance: The U-2 and OXCART Programs, 1954-1974, CIA History Staff, 2013 release, https://archive.org/download/cia-readingroom-document-0000190094/0000190094_djvu.txt, chapter 1
- **Tier 1 documented.** He found the site personally and on a dated flight: 'On 12 April 1955 Richard Bissell and Col. Osmund Ritland (the senior Air Force officer on the project staff) flew over Nevada with Kelly Johnson in a small Beechcraft plane piloted by Lockheed's chief test pilot, Tony LeVier. They spotted what appeared to be an airstrip by a salt flat known as Groom Lake, near the northeast corner of the Atomic Energy Commission's (AEC) Nevada Proving Ground.' Source: Pedlow and Welzenbach, The Central Intelligence Agency and Overhead Reconnaissance: The U-2 and OXCART Programs, 1954-1974, CIA History Staff, 2013 release, https://archive.org/download/cia-readingroom-document-0000190094/0000190094_djvu.txt, chapter 2
- **Tier 1 documented.** His project staff serviced Blue Book and withheld the answer: 'BLUE BOOK investigators regularly called on the Agency's Project Staff in Washington to check reported UFO sightings against U-2 flight logs. This enabled the investigators to eliminate the majority of the UFO reports, although they could not reveal to the letter writers the true cause of the UFO sightings.' Source: Pedlow and Welzenbach, The Central Intelligence Agency and Overhead Reconnaissance: The U-2 and OXCART Programs, 1954-1974, CIA History Staff, 2013 release, https://archive.org/download/cia-readingroom-document-0000190094/0000190094_djvu.txt, chapter 2, section 'U-2s, UFOs, and Operation BLUE BOOK'

## Robert L. Hastings

Robert L. Hastings is a person on the board; board tier B, documented channel and capability, programme role indicated but not yet nailed to a primary source; dates 2026-06-20; axes C0 A0 D3; 5 connections.

In one line: Researcher, UFO incursions at US nuclear weapons sites; author, 'UFOs and Nukes'.

### The longer account

Robert Hastings has spent more than forty years doing one thing: locating and interviewing US Air Force personnel who were present at nuclear weapons sites during unexplained aerial incursions, and pairing their testimony with declassified documents obtained under FOIA. He states he has interviewed over 150 military veterans involved in such incidents at missile sites, weapons storage facilities and bomb test ranges, and he has published the underlying paper - FBI memoranda on Los Alamos in December 1948 and on Los Alamos and Sandia in August 1950, F. E. Warren AFB documents from 1 August 1965, Minot AFB material from 1966, the 17 March 1967 Air Force teletype reporting loss of strategic alert at Malmstrom, and 1975 Malmstrom documents - alongside signed witness affidavits. On 27 September 2010 he hosted the UFO-Nukes Connection press conference at the National Press Club in Washington DC, at which seven US Air Force veterans described incursions at nuclear weapons sites; CNN streamed the event live. That event is the single most important item in this section of the board, because it converted a body of anonymous or semi-anonymous claims into named, on-the-record testimony from identifiable former officers with signed affidavits on file - which is the difference between tier 3 and tier 2. His book 'UFOs and Nukes: Extraordinary Encounters at Nuclear Weapons Sites' is in a revised second edition of 580 pages, and his documentary 'UFOs and Nukes: The Secret Link Revealed' followed. He has lectured at more than 500 colleges and universities since 1981 and continues publishing, most recently on Grusch-era material in June 2026.

### Where the record conflicts

- Air Force historians and sceptics including James Carlson have contested the Malmstrom Echo Flight interpretation specifically, attributing the March 1967 shutdown to an engineering fault and, in a later analysis, to a nearby electromagnetic pulse simulator test.

### Recorded as unverified

- Hastings's date of birth. The identity of the seventh speaker at the 2010 press conference is inferred here from the affidavit set on his own site rather than from a programme.

Affiliations recorded: ufohastings.com, UFOs and Nukes (book and documentary).

### Why it holds its tier

Re-graded B on a global pass (rank 400 of 1057, score 33). 5 connections, 3 of them custody/witness/funding/programmatic. Axes C0/A0/D3.

### The claims on the record

- **Tier 1 documented.** Hastings publishes the primary documents underlying his cases, including FBI memoranda on Los Alamos (December 1948) and Los Alamos/Sandia (August 1950), F. E. Warren AFB documents of 1 August 1965, Minot AFB documents of 25 August 1966 and 6 December 1966, an Air Force teletype of 17 March 1967 headed loss of strategic alert, and 1975 Malmstrom material. Source: ufohastings.com document archive, retrieved 12 August 2026
- **Tier 2 named person on the record.** Hastings holds signed witness affidavits from Robert Salas, Charles Halt, Patrick McDonough, Robert Jamison, Dwynne Arneson, Bruce Fenstermacher and Jerry Nelson, published in facsimile. Source: ufohastings.com, 'Witness Affidavits' section
- **Tier 2 named person on the record.** Hastings states he has interviewed more than 150 military veterans about UFO-related incidents at US missile sites, weapons storage facilities and nuclear test ranges. Source: Hastings, ufohastings.com and 'UFOs and Nukes' (2nd ed.)
- **Tier 2 named person on the record.** On 27 September 2010 Hastings convened seven US Air Force veterans at the National Press Club to describe UFO incursions at nuclear weapons sites; CNN carried the event. Source: ufohastings.com; CNN coverage of the press conference, 27 September 2010
- **Tier 2 named person on the record.** On American Alchemy with Jesse Michels, 19 October 2024, at 0:34:57, Michels gave Hastings's figure as '167 UFO sighting cases at nuclear bases', and in the same breath as '167 ICBM security personnel, radar operators, people who work at these nuclear sites'. ## Steven M. Greer

Steven M. Greer is a person on the board; board tier C, contextual, real and relevant, no direct programme linkage on the record yet; dates 2001-05-09; axes C0 A0 D3; 5 connections.

In one line: Emergency physician; founder of CSETI (1990) and the Disclosure Project (1993); National Press Club event 9 May 2001.

### The longer account

Greer built the first mass witness-aggregation project and demonstrated both its power and its limits. A practising emergency physician, he founded the Center for the Study of Extraterrestrial Intelligence in 1990 and the Disclosure Project in 1993 with the explicit aim of assembling former military and government personnel willing to testify to Congress. On 9 May 2001 he convened roughly twenty of them at the National Press Club in Washington - radar operators, missile launch officers, air traffic controllers, FAA personnel - and announced that more than four hundred had given testimony, demanding congressional hearings with subpoena power and amnesty for witnesses. The event drew heavy international coverage and produced no hearing. Its consequence for the record is twofold. First, it created a large videotaped corpus of named former personnel making specific, dated, on-the-record claims, some of which - notably the missile-facility shutdown accounts - align with the FOIA-released overflight documents from 1975. Second, Greer's subsequent trajectory into claims that outran his evidence gave the institutions he was pressuring a durable reason to treat the whole category as unserious. The Clinton White House's own UFO FOIA collection (2006-0474-F) consists in the majority of documents Greer sent it, which is a precise measure of how the material was received: filed as correspondence.

### Where the record conflicts

- Greer's later claims - including assertions about non-human biological specimens - are unsupported and are routinely used to discredit the 2001 witness corpus as a whole.
- Central claims (Atacama skeleton, CE-5 contact) are disproven or unfalsifiable; treated by structural investigators as a discrediting vector rather than a source.

### Recorded as unverified

- The full four-hundred-plus witness archive has never been independently catalogued or corroborated.

Affiliations recorded: Center for the Study of Extraterrestrial Intelligence (founder, 1990), The Disclosure Project (founder, 1993), CSETI (founder, 1990).

### Why it holds its tier

Re-graded C on a global pass (rank 629 of 1057, score 25). 5 connections, 1 of them custody/witness/funding/programmatic. Axes C0/A0/D3.

### The claims on the record

- **Tier 1 documented.** The Clinton Presidential Library's UFO FOIA collection 2006-0474-F 'consists of letters, correspondence tracking forms, memos, reports, email and cables relating to UFO's or extraterrestrial beings. A majority of the collection are documents which were sent to the White House by Steven M. Greer, the head of a nongovernmental organization called the Center for the Study of Extraterrestrial Intelligence (CSETI).' Source: Clinton Digital Library collection finding aid, identifier 2006-0474-F
- **Tier 2 named person on the record.** Greer founded CSETI in 1990 and the Disclosure Project in 1993, and in May 2001 held a National Press Club press conference featuring 20 retired Air Force, FAA and intelligence officers. Source: Wikipedia, 'Steven M. Greer', retrieved 12 August 2026, DEMOTED from Tier 1 (cited to a secondary/encyclopedic source); needs primary replacement
- **Tier 2 named person on the record.** Greer held a National Press Club event on 8 May 2026. Source: UAP Gerb livestream description, 12 May 2026
- **Tier 2 named person on the record.** On 9 May 2001 Greer presented roughly twenty former military, intelligence and government personnel at the National Press Club and stated that more than four hundred witnesses had provided testimony, calling for congressional hearings with subpoena power. Source: Disclosure Project National Press Club event, Washington DC, 9 May 2001; contemporaneous international press coverage
- **Tier 2 named person on the record.** The Atacama skeleton presented in Sirius was shown by genetic evidence to be human. Source: Wikipedia, 'Steven M. Greer', DEMOTED from Tier 1 (cited to a secondary/encyclopedic source); needs primary replacement

## Steven H. Schiff

Steven H. Schiff is a person on the board; board tier B, documented channel and capability, programme role indicated but not yet nailed to a primary source; dates 1995-07-28; axes C0 A1 D3; 5 connections.

In one line: US Representative, New Mexico (R).

### The longer account

Schiff is the proof that a single member of Congress asking a records question can produce a durable federal finding. Representing New Mexico's first district, he requested information from the Department of Defense about the 1947 Roswell events in 1993, was routed to the National Archives rather than given an answer, and responded by directing the General Accounting Office to conduct a formal search of government records. GAO reported to him in July 1995 as GAO/NSIAD-95-187. Its finding is the one that matters and is still cited three decades later: Roswell Army Air Field administrative records from March 1945 through December 1949 and RAAF outgoing messages from October 1946 through December 1949 had been destroyed, and 'the document disposition form does not indicate what organization or person destroyed the records and when or under what authority the records were destroyed.' GAO also recorded that only two government records originating in 1947 could be located. Schiff had asked for the paper trail and GAO told him, in the flat language of an audit agency, that the paper trail covering the relevant period had been eliminated by persons unknown under authority unknown. He died in 1998.

### What is left unexplained

No agency has ever produced a disposition authority for the destroyed RAAF message traffic, and no accountability review of that destruction has been conducted in the thirty-one years since GAO reported it.

### Where the record conflicts

- The Air Force's own 1994-1997 Roswell reports attributed the events to Project MOGUL and anthropomorphic test dummies; GAO made no finding on the cause and confined itself to the records.

Affiliations recorded: US House of Representatives, NM-01 (1989-1998), House Judiciary Committee, House Science Committee, US House of Representatives, New Mexico 1st district (1989-1998).

### Why it holds its tier

Re-graded B on a global pass (rank 285 of 1057, score 38). 5 connections, 0 of them custody/witness/funding/programmatic. Axes C0/A1/D3.

### The claims on the record

- **Tier 1 documented.** 'RAAF outgoing messages (from Oct. 1946 through Dec. 1949) were destroyed.' Source: GAO/NSIAD-95-187, p.3
- **Tier 1 documented.** 'The document disposition form does not indicate what organization or person destroyed the records and when or under what authority the records were destroyed.' Source: GAO/NSIAD-95-187, p.3
- **Tier 1 documented.** GAO conducted its Roswell records review at the request of Representative Steven H. Schiff, working from March 1994 to June 1995. Source: GAO/NSIAD-95-187, letter of transmittal and scope section
- **Tier 1 documented.** GAO reported that RAAF administrative records (March 1945-December 1949) and RAAF outgoing messages (October 1946-December 1949) were destroyed, and that 'the document disposition form does not indicate what organization or person destroyed the records and when or under what authority the records were destroyed.' Source: GAO/NSIAD-95-187, 'Government Records: Results of a Search for Records Concerning the 1947 Crash Near Roswell, New Mexico,' 28 July 1995
- **Tier 1 documented.** GAO's list of records searched includes 'National Security Agency, Fort Meade, Md. - FOIA records, Citizens Against UFO Secrecy.' Source: GAO/NSIAD-95-187, appendix
- **Tier 1 documented.** GAO's search for government records concerning the Roswell crash 'yielded two records originating in 1947.' Source: GAO/NSIAD-95-187
- **Tier 1 documented.** RAAF administrative records from March 1945 through December 1949 were also destroyed. Source: GAO/NSIAD-95-187, p.3

## Walter Bedell Smith

Walter Bedell Smith is a person on the board; board tier B, documented channel and capability, programme role indicated but not yet nailed to a primary source; dates 1952-12-02; axes C1 A3 D0; 5 connections.

In one line: Director of Central Intelligence; the DCI who ordered the UFO problem into the intelligence community.

### The longer account

Walter Bedell Smith is the Director of Central Intelligence whose own instruction, on 20 August 1952, turned unidentified flying objects from an Air Force nuisance into an intelligence-community problem with a draft National Security Council directive behind it. Chadwell's memoranda run to him, name him as the approving authority on the recommendation that the DCI take the subject to the Psychological Strategy Board, and attach a memorandum to the NSC Executive Secretary over his signature block proposing a coordinated research programme. The Agency's own later correspondence states plainly that the Robertson Panel 'was convened at the direction of General Smith'. The board carries the Robertson Panel at 112 occurrences and has one occurrence of his name; he is the office from which it issued. The Michels corpus describes a 1953 Bedell Smith letter proposing psychological warfare; the psychological-warfare document is Chadwell's, dated 2 October 1952, the approval line over Smith's name on the released copy is blank, and Smith's own 2 December 1952 draft proposes research rather than exploitation.

### Recorded as unverified

- A 1953 letter by Bedell Smith proposing the use of the UFO phenomenon for psychological warfare against the USSR. No such document was found: the psychological-warfare text is Chadwell's of 2 October 1952 and Smith's own attached draft of 2 December 1952 proposes a research programme.
- That Paul Mellon saw a German disc in a Czechoslovak hangar with Dulles present. Nothing bearing on this was found in the CIA reading room mirror.

Affiliations recorded: Central Intelligence Agency, Director of Central Intelligence.

### Why it holds its tier

Re-graded B on a global pass (rank 399 of 1057, score 33). 5 connections, 1 of them custody/witness/funding/programmatic. Axes C1/A3/D0.

### The claims on the record

- **Tier 1 documented.** A memorandum to the National Security Council was drafted over his name proposing research, not exploitation: 'It is my view that this situation has possible implications for our national security which transcend the interests of a single service. A broader, coordinated effort should be initiated to develop a firm scientific understanding of the several phenomena which apparently are involved in these reports, and to assure ourselves that the incidents will not hamper our present efforts in the Cold War or confuse our early warning system in case of an attack.' The typed signature block reads 'Walter B. Smith / Director'. Source: Draft Memorandum to the Executive Secretary, National Security Council, subject: Unidentified Flying Objects (Flying Saucers), attached to CIA document C00015340, 2 December 1952, para. 2, https://archive.org/details/cia-readingroom-document-0005515935
- **Tier 1 documented.** He is named as the approving authority on the recommendation that the DCI take the subject to the Psychological Strategy Board, and on the released copy the approval was never recorded. The 2 October 1952 routing page reads 'ACTION BY APPROVING AUTHORITY: Date: ____ Approved (disapproved): ____ WALTER B. SMITH, Director'; both lines are blank. The recommendation itself is action (b): 'That the DCI discuss this subject with the Psychological Strategy Board. A memorandum to the Director, Psychological Strategy Board, is attached for signature as TAB C.' Source: H. Marshall Chadwell, Memorandum to Director of Central Intelligence, subject: Flying Saucers, 2 October 1952, CIA document C00015339, para. 4 and routing page, https://archive.org/details/cia-readingroom-document-0005515933
- **Tier 1 documented.** Smith personally started the intelligence-community effort. Chadwell's 2 December 1952 memorandum to the DCI opens: 'On 20 August, the DCI, after a briefing by OSI on the above subject, directed the preparation of an NSCID for submission to the Council stating the need for investigation and directing agencies concerned to cooperate in such investigations.' Source: H. Marshall Chadwell, Memorandum for Director of Central Intelligence, subject: Unidentified Flying Objects, 2 December 1952, CIA document C00015340, para. 1, https://archive.org/details/cia-readingroom-document-0005515935
- **Tier 1 documented.** The CIA attributed the Robertson Panel to him in writing. Allen W. Dulles, transmitting the panel report on 13 March 1953: 'This panel was convened at the direction of General Smith, following the recommendation of the Intelligence Advisory Committee.' Source: Allen W. Dulles to the Secretary of Defense, 13 March 1953, SECRET, CIA-RDP80B01676R001000160043-9, https://archive.org/details/cia-readingroom-document-cia-rdp80b01676r001000160043-9

## Alex Dietrich

Alex Dietrich is a person on the board; board tier C, contextual, real and relevant, no direct programme linkage on the record yet; dates 2021-05-16; axes C0 A1 D2; 4 connections.

In one line: LCDR, USN (Ret.); VFA-41 wingman on the 14 Nov 2004 Nimitz intercept.

### The longer account

Dietrich was the second F/A-18F aircrew on the 14 November 2004 intercept, flying as Fravor's wingman and observing the object for roughly five minutes. She is named alongside Fravor in the Navy's own range fouler report on the incident, released through the Navy FOIA reading room on 13 January 2023 - which matters because it puts her at the event in a government record rather than only in a media account. She stayed silent publicly for sixteen years, then gave her first public account on CBS 60 Minutes on 16 May 2021, opening with 'I never wanted to be on national TV, no offense.' That reluctance is itself the datum: the stigma structure the ODNI's own June 2021 preliminary assessment described - 'disparagement associated with observing UAP, reporting it, or attempting to discuss it with colleagues' - operated on her for a decade and a half. Mike Gold, testifying before House Oversight on 13 November 2024, cited her by name, saying she 'was mocked by her colleagues' and that her incident 'went years without any investigation.' Dietrich has since argued in print for cooperative, data-first investigation rather than advocacy, publishing an opinion piece in The Debrief on 17 February 2023.

Affiliations recorded: US Navy (2001-2019), VFA-41 Black Aces (2004), Naval Postgraduate School / Pentagon staff tours.

### Why it holds its tier

Re-graded C on a global pass (rank 577 of 1057, score 27). 4 connections, 3 of them custody/witness/funding/programmatic. Axes C0/A1/D2.

### The claims on the record

- **Tier 1 documented.** Navy range fouler documentation on the 14 Nov 2004 incident names the F/A-18 aircrew as including Commander David Fravor and Lt. Cmdr. Alex Dietrich. Source: Navy range fouler reports released via the Navy FOIA reading room, 13 January 2023, reported by The War Zone
- **Tier 2 named person on the record.** 'I never wanted to be on national TV, no offense.' Source: Alex Dietrich, CBS 60 Minutes, 16 May 2021
- **Tier 2 named person on the record.** Dietrich 'was mocked by her colleagues' and the incident 'went years without any investigation.' Source: Michael Gold, written testimony, House Oversight joint subcommittee hearing, 13 November 2024

## Alexandro Wiggins

Alexandro Wiggins is a person on the board; board tier A, named in sworn testimony or primary documents as directly inside the programme; dates 2025-09-09; axes C1 A1 D3; 4 connections.

In one line: Senior Chief Petty Officer (Operations Specialist), USN; USS Jackson (LCS-6) witness, 15 Feb 2023.

### The longer account

Alexandro C. Wiggins is a US Navy Senior Chief Petty Officer, an Operations Specialist with roughly twenty-three years of service, who testified in uniform and on active duty before the House Oversight Task Force on the Declassification of Federal Secrets on 9 September 2025. That fact alone distinguishes him from almost every witness who preceded him: Fravor, Graves, Grusch and Elizondo all testified after leaving service. Wiggins's written statement places him aboard USS Jackson (LCS-6) on 15 February 2023 at approximately 1915 PST in the W-291 Warning Area off Southern California. He states he 'visually observed a light rising from the waterline, consistent with a platform emerging from the ocean rather than distant surface lights or celestial rise.' The object was self-luminous and Tic Tac-shaped; it joined three similar objects; the four held controlled geometry and departed simultaneously with near-instantaneous acceleration, producing no sonic boom, no exhaust plume and displaying no visible control surfaces - characteristics he described as inconsistent with conventional aircraft or drones. Combat Information Center personnel recorded the event on the ship's Star SAFIRE multi-spectral EO/IR system and reported to the Tactical Action Officer. The footage was subsequently published by journalists Jeremy Corbell and George Knapp. Wiggins is therefore a rare complete chain: a named, credentialed, serving witness; a specified date, time and operating area; a named sensor system; a documented internal report; and released imagery.

### What is left unexplained

Four self-luminous objects, one transmedium, recorded on a calibrated EO/IR sensor with intact metadata in a US Navy warning area - and no released government assessment of the footage exists.

### Recorded as unverified

- Whether the USS Jackson Star SAFIRE recording was ever submitted to AARO, and if so under what case number.
- Whether the event appears in AARO's FY2023, FY2024 or FY2025 case holdings.
- Whether the USS Jackson sensor data was ever transmitted to AARO or appears in its case holdings.
- Whether the USS Jackson Star SAFIRE recording was ever entered into AARO's case system, and under what case number.
- The identities of the other CIC watchstanders who observed the event.

Affiliations recorded: US Navy, Senior Chief Operations Specialist, USS Jackson (LCS-6) (2023), U.S. Navy (23 years, Senior Chief Petty Officer), USS Jackson (LCS-6), US Navy, Senior Chief Petty Officer, US Navy.

### Why it holds its tier

Re-graded A on a global pass (rank 126 of 1057, score 55). 5 connections, 4 of them custody/witness/funding/programmatic. Axes C1/A1/D3.

### The claims on the record

- **Tier 1 documented.** Rep. Burlison from the dais: 'You are the first witness to come forward that is currently serving, and it is recognized, and so I thank you.' Source: GPO CHRG-119hhrg61718, 9 September 2025

## Adm. Bobby Ray Inman

Adm. Bobby Ray Inman is a person on the board; board tier B, documented channel and capability, programme role indicated but not yet nailed to a primary source; dates 1981-02-03; axes C1 A3 D1; 4 connections.

In one line: Director, National Security Agency 1977-1981; Deputy Director of Central Intelligence 1981-1982; Director of SAIC from 1982.

### The longer account

The documented officeholder who joins three institutions the board already carries and nothing currently connects through a person: NSA, CIA and SAIC. He told the Senate Select Committee on Intelligence on 3 February 1981 that he had been Director of Naval Intelligence, Vice Director for Plans, Operations and Support of the Defense Intelligence Agency, and Director of the National Security Agency since 5 July 1977, and he was there to be confirmed as Deputy Director of Central Intelligence. He joined SAIC's board in 1982, the year he retired from the Navy, and from 1990 was also a paid part-time employee of the company advising it on strategic planning while chairing its Executive Committee. He did not head SAIC: the transcript at corpus 12 file 3, 0:19:28 says he did, and SAIC's own 2003 proxy statement names J. R. Beyster as Chairman of the Board, Chief Executive Officer and President. That correction is the finding this node carries. He is on the board for the career, which is a single traceable line from signals intelligence to the deputy directorship of the CIA to the boardroom of the government's most sensitive technical services house. He is not on it for the anecdotes attached to him in the podcast corpus, none of which produced an artefact.

### Recorded as unverified

- A recorded call in which Inman discussed UFOs with the researcher Bob Oechsler. No recording, transcript or date was opened.
- A cold call in which Inman denied knowledge of Andrija Puharich or Bob Beck. This is a documentary maker's account of his own telephone call and there is no artefact to grade.
- A video in which Inman refers to a National Underwater Reconnaissance Office. The recording was not located in this pass.
- That SAIC moved into psychotronic research and psychotronic weaponry under SDI funding. Not established by anything opened.

### Why it holds its tier

Re-graded B on a global pass (rank 240 of 1057, score 41). 4 connections, 0 of them custody/witness/funding/programmatic. Axes C1/A3/D1.

### The claims on the record

- **Tier 1 documented.** He dates the NSA directorship himself and states the nomination: 'since July 5, 1977, I have had the pleasure of serving as Director, National Security Agency', and 'I have been nominated by the President to serve as the Deputy Director of Central Intelligence.' Source: Nomination of Admiral B.R. Inman, Senate Select Committee on Intelligence, 3 February 1981, https://www.intelligence.senate.gov/wp-content/uploads/2024/08/sites-default-files-hearings-97inman.pdf
- **Tier 1 documented.** He did not head SAIC. The same proxy names 'J.R. Beyster, age 78, Chairman of the Board, Chief Executive Officer and President, Director since 1969' and records that Beyster 'founded the Company in 1969 and has served as Chairman of the Board and Chief Executive Officer since that time'. The Michels corpus at corpus 12 file 3, 0:19:28 says Inman headed SAIC; the company's own proxy statement contradicts it. Source: Science Applications International Corporation, DEF 14A filed 28 May 2003, SEC EDGAR accession 0001047469-03-019902, https://www.sec.gov/Archives/edgar/data/353394/000104746903019902/a2110788zdef14a.htm
- **Tier 1 documented.** He gave his own career to the Senate: 'Subsequent assignments included service as the fleet intelligence officer on the staff of the commander, 7th Fleet; attendance at the National War College; executive assistant and senior aide to the Vice Chief of Naval Operations; assistant chief of staff for intelligence to the commander in chief, U.S. Pacific Fleet; Director of Naval Intelligence; and Vice Director for Plans, Operations, and Support of the Defense Intelligence Agency.' Source: Nomination of Admiral B.R. Inman, hearing before the Senate Select Committee on Intelligence, 97th Congress 1st session, 3 February 1981, GPO print 74-227 O, https://www.intelligence.senate.gov/wp-content/uploads/2024/08/sites-default-files-hearings-97inman.pdf
- **Tier 1 documented.** He is not among the directors of The Wackenhut Corporation in any proxy statement EDGAR holds. The proxy for the annual meeting of 7 May 1999 lists twelve directors by name, age and year of first election, among them Julius W. Becton Jr., Benjamin R. Civiletti, Paul X. Kelley, Thomas P. Stafford and George R. Wackenhut, and the name Inman appears nowhere in it. It appears nowhere in the proxy for the 1998 annual meeting or in the 2002 merger proxy either. The corporation reported under CIK 0000104030, SIC 7381; its earliest filing of any kind is dated 24 March 1994 and its earliest proxy statement is a preliminary 14A of 24 March 1997, so no proxy exists on EDGAR for any earlier year and the board's composition before 1997 is unestablished in either direction. Source: SEC EDGAR company filing index, CIK 0000104030, https://data.sec.gov/submissions/CIK0000104030.json ; The Wackenhut Corporation DEF 14A filed 31 March 1999, https://www.sec.gov/Archives/edgar/data/0000104030/000095014499003822/0000950144-99-003822.txt ; DEF 14A for the 1998 annual meeting and DEFM14A filed 10 April 2002, https://www.sec.gov/Archives/edgar/data/0000104030/000095014498003074/0000950144-98-003074.txt
- **Tier 1 documented.** SAIC's own proxy records the relationship: 'B.R. Inman, age 72, Director, Director since 1982. Admiral Inman, USN (Ret.) joined the Company in 1990 as a part-time employee and, in that capacity, advises the Company on a wide variety of strategic planning issues.' He is listed as Chairperson of the Executive Committee and a member of the Nominating Committee, and received $132,129 of compensation under the part-time employee arrangement in fiscal 2003. Source: Science Applications International Corporation, DEF 14A filed 28 May 2003, SEC EDGAR accession 0001047469-03-019902, https://www.sec.gov/Archives/edgar/data/353394/000104746903019902/a2110788zdef14a.htm
- **Tier 1 documented.** The directorship is independently on file as a Section 16 insider report: SEC Form 4 for INMAN, BOBBY R, statement for 10 April 2003, issuer Science Applications International Corporation, with the 'Director' box checked and 'Form filed by One Reporting Person'. The corporation's own proxy statements for 1997 to 2002 do not name him. Source: Danny Jones Podcast, ep. 320, 4 August 2025, 2:06:30, mapped at research/leads/dannyjones-corpus-20.md

A third corpus running has failed to open any record of the office: nro.gov and nsa.gov returned HTTP 403, irp.fas.org has no NURO page, the govinfo search API returned a count of zero for the phrase, and the strings NURO and National Underwater Reconnaissance occur nowhere in the fifty released pages of the CIA's own AZORIAN history. ## Christopher Sherwood

Christopher Sherwood is a person on the board; board tier C, contextual, real and relevant, no direct programme linkage on the record yet; dates 2019-06-01; axes C0 A1 D0; 4 connections.

In one line: DoD spokesperson, Office of the Assistant to the Secretary of Defense for Public Affairs.

### The longer account

Sherwood made two statements eleven days apart in 2019 that between them define the Department's problem. On 22 May 2019 he told the New York Post that AATIP 'did pursue research and investigation into unidentified aerial phenomena', the first unambiguous official acknowledgement that the Pentagon's aerospace-threat program looked at UAP. On 1 June 2019 he told The Intercept's Keith Kloor that 'Mr. Elizondo had no responsibilities with regard to the AATIP program while he worked in OUSDI, up until the time he resigned effective 10/4/2017.' Six months later Susan Gough repudiated the first statement, telling reporters Sherwood had merely repeated what an earlier spokesperson had said and that his account was 'not entirely fact-based', while keeping the second statement intact. The Department has therefore been in the position of saying one of its own spokesmen was wrong about the program but right about the man.

### Where the record conflicts

- His own two 2019 statements sit uneasily together and the first was later disowned by the Department.

Affiliations recorded: Office of the Assistant to the Secretary of Defense for Public Affairs.

### Why it holds its tier

Re-graded C on a global pass (rank 747 of 1057, score 21). 4 connections, 0 of them custody/witness/funding/programmatic, 2 official denials recorded against it. Axes C0/A1/D0.

### The claims on the record

- **Tier 1 documented.** Stated on 1 June 2019: 'Mr. Elizondo had no responsibilities with regard to the AATIP program while he worked in OUSDI, up until the time he resigned effective 10/4/2017.' Source: Keith Kloor, The Intercept, 1 June 2019
- **Tier 1 documented.** Stated on 22 May 2019 that AATIP 'did pursue research and investigation into unidentified aerial phenomena'. Source: Christopher Sherwood to the New York Post, 22 May 2019

### Its strongest connections

- Christopher Sherwood runs out to **Luis D. Elizondo** (person, board tier A), a denial link at Tier 1 documented. The link reads: 1 June 2019: 'no responsibilities with regard to the AATIP program'. Obtained by Keith Kloor for The Intercept in response to the History Channel series. This sentence has anchored every subsequent Departmental statement about Elizondo. Sources: The Intercept, https://theintercept.com/2019/06/01/ufo-unidentified-history-channel-luis-elizondo-pentagon/, dated 2019-06-01.
- Christopher Sherwood runs out to **Advanced Aerospace Threat Identification Program (AATIP / AAITP)** (program, board tier A), a institutional link at Tier 1 documented. The link reads: 22 May 2019: AATIP 'did pursue research and investigation into unidentified aerial phenomena'. The Department's first unambiguous acknowledgement that its aerospace-threat program looked at UAP. Repudiated by its own spokesperson six months later. Sources: The Black Vault, The Pentagon Corrects Record, https://www.theblackvault.com/documentarchive/the-pentagon-corrects-record-on-secret-ufo-program/, dated 2019-12-06.

## Edward J. Ruppelt

Edward J. Ruppelt is a person on the board; board tier B, documented channel and capability, programme role indicated but not yet nailed to a primary source; dates 1956-01-01; axes C2 A2 D3; 4 connections.

In one line: First director, Project Blue Book.

### The longer account

Captain Edward Ruppelt ran Project Blue Book from March 1952 to August 1953 and is the only insider of the early era who wrote it down. He inherited Project GRUDGE at its most degraded, professionalised the reporting chain, coined the term 'unidentified flying object' to replace 'flying saucer,' and presided over the July 1952 Washington sightings and the January 1953 Robertson Panel, both of which he attended in an official capacity. His 1956 book, 'The Report on Unidentified Flying Objects,' is the origin of public knowledge about the 1948 Estimate of the Situation, about the Air Force's internal split between believers and debunkers, and about the Robertson Panel's function. He was not a crank: he was the serving officer in charge, describing the machinery from inside it. The Condon Report, working under Air Force contract twelve years later, corroborated his account of the Estimate and its destruction. Ruppelt added three sceptical chapters to a 1960 edition shortly before dying of a heart attack at 37, a reversal that is variously read as genuine disillusionment or as pressure; the board records both readings and resolves neither.

### Where the record conflicts

- The 1960 edition's added chapters contradict the tone of the 1956 first edition; no record explains the reversal

Affiliations recorded: Air Technical Intelligence Center, Wright-Patterson AFB (1951-1953), Project GRUDGE / Project Blue Book, director (1951-1953), Northrop Corporation (1954-1960).

### Why it holds its tier

Re-graded B on a global pass (rank 210 of 1057, score 43). 4 connections, 3 of them custody/witness/funding/programmatic. Axes C2/A2/D3.

### The claims on the record

- **Tier 1 documented.** Ruppelt directed Blue Book from March 1952 through August 1953, the period covering the Washington flap and the Robertson Panel. Source: USAF Project Blue Book records, NARA RG 341; Condon Report Sec. V Ch. 2
- **Tier 1 documented.** The Condon Report, produced under USAF contract, independently records that the Estimate was prepared, rejected by Vandenberg and destroyed. Source: Condon Report, Section V Chapter 2
- **Tier 2 named person on the record.** Ruppelt added sceptical chapters to the 1960 edition of his book, reversing tone shortly before his death. Source: Ruppelt, 'The Report on Unidentified Flying Objects', 2nd edition, 1960
- **Tier 2 named person on the record.** Ruppelt published the existence of the 1948 Top Secret 'Estimate of the Situation' and its rejection and destruction by Gen. Vandenberg. Source: Ruppelt, 'The Report on Unidentified Flying Objects', 1956

### Its strongest connections

Ruppelt directed the project from March 1952 to August 1953, covering the Washington flap and the Robertson Panel, and later published the only insider account of the period. Sources: NARA, Project Blue Book records, https://www.archives.gov/research/military/air-force/ufos, dated 2026-08-12; Ruppelt, The Report on Unidentified Flying Objects, https://www.gutenberg.org/ebooks/17346, dated 1956-01-01.

## Elijah Crane

Elijah Crane is a person on the board; board tier B, documented channel and capability, programme role indicated but not yet nailed to a primary source; dates 2025-07-24; axes C0 A1 D3; 4 connections.

In one line: U.S. Rep. (R-AZ-2); wrote DoD on Grusch's reprisal; cosponsor H.R. 5060.

### The longer account

Crane produced the single most specific piece of documented retaliation evidence in the House record. A former Navy SEAL, he sits on the Task Force on the Declassification of Federal Secrets and cosponsored Burchett's H.R. 5060. On 24 July 2025 he wrote the Department of Defense on behalf of Major David Charles Grusch, and on 9 September 2025 he entered that letter into the hearing record while stating that he was still waiting for a reply. His description of what happened to Grusch is the operative fact: 'due to his participation in the disclosure of UAP, he suffered reprisal like the removal of his clearance, denial of promotion, and loss of medical retirement.' That is three discrete adverse actions against the government's own credible-and-urgent whistleblower, stated by a sitting member on a hearing record, with a letter to the Department unanswered for six weeks and counting. Crane also told the witnesses at that hearing that he had grown up thinking the subject was 'a little kooky' and had become a believer after hearing servicemembers testify and seeing the Yemen footage Burlison played.

### What is left unexplained

A sitting member formally notified the Secretary of Defense that a whistleblower had lost his clearance, his promotion and his medical retirement for making a protected disclosure - and got no answer on the record.

### Recorded as unverified

- Whether DoD ever replied to the 24 July 2025 letter; the date and authority of the clearance revocation.

Affiliations recorded: U.S. House of Representatives, AZ-2 (2023- ), Committee on Oversight and Government Reform (2025- ), Task Force on the Declassification of Federal Secrets (2025- ), U.S. Navy SEAL (2001-2013), US House of Representatives, AZ-02 (2023- ), Task Force on the Declassification of Federal Secrets.

### Why it holds its tier

Re-graded B on a global pass (rank 343 of 1057, score 35). 4 connections, 0 of them custody/witness/funding/programmatic, 1 official denial recorded against it. Axes C0/A1/D3.

### The claims on the record

- **Tier 1 documented.** 'I would like to enter into the testimony a letter I sent to the DOD regarding the case of Major David Charles Grusch, a UAP whistleblower who has been extremely helpful to this Committee. Unfortunately, due to his participation in the disclosure of UAP, he suffered reprisal like the removal of his clearance, denial of promotion, and loss of medical retirement. I wrote the DOD on July 24, 2025, on behalf of Major Grusch, and I am still waiting for a reply.' Source: Rep. Eli Crane, 9 September 2025, GPO CHRG-119hhrg61718
- **Tier 1 documented.** Cosponsor of H.R. 5060, the UAP Whistleblower Protection Act, introduced 29 August 2025. Source: Congress.gov, H.R. 5060 (119th Congress), cosponsor record
- **Tier 1 documented.** Submitted for the record of the 9 September 2025 hearing a 'Letter to SECDEF Regarding Major David Charles Grusch Reprisal', listed in the printed INDEX OF DOCUMENTS of CHRG-119hhrg61718. Source: CHRG-119hhrg61718 INDEX OF DOCUMENTS
- **Tier 1 documented.** The hearing's index of documents lists 'Letter to SECDEF Regarding Major David Charles Grusch Reprisal; submitted by Rep. Crane.' Source: Index of documents, Serial No. 119-44, 9 September 2025
- **Tier 1 documented.** The hearing's printed Index of Documents lists 'Letter to SECDEF Regarding Major David Charles Grusch Reprisal; submitted by Rep. Crane.' Source: CHRG-119hhrg61718, Index of Documents

### Its strongest connections

The link reads: Did not answer his 24 July 2025 letter on the reprisal against Grusch. Crane stated on the record on 9 September 2025 that he was still waiting for a reply six weeks later. ## Glenn S. Grothman

Glenn S. Grothman is a person on the board; board tier C, contextual, real and relevant, no direct programme linkage on the record yet; dates 2023-07-26; axes C0 A1 D2; 4 connections.

In one line: U.S. Rep. (R-WI-6); Chairman of the subcommittee that held the 26 July 2023 hearing.

### The longer account

Grothman gavelled in the first UAP hearing of the modern disclosure era. As Chairman of the Subcommittee on National Security, the Border, and Foreign Affairs he presided on 26 July 2023, waived six non-members onto the panel - Burchett, Luna, Gaetz, Burlison, Ocasio-Cortez and Ogles - swore the witnesses, and opened by saying that lack of transparency on UAP 'has fueled wild speculation and debate for decades, eroding public trust in the very institutions that are meant to serve and protect them.' He co-chaired the 13 November 2024 joint hearing with Mace and co-led H.R. 6967 with Garcia. His 2024 opening statement is also the board's cleanest artifact of the containment position from inside the disclosure caucus: he stated that no agency had been able to substantiate Grusch's claims 'despite our Committee Members endlessly questioning these agencies inside and outside of the SCIF.' Read against Luna's 2025 disclosure that Oversight members could not be read in on the relevant SAPs at all without HASC and HAC-D approval, the two statements do not describe the same access - which is exactly the seam the coverup strand exists to record.

Affiliations recorded: U.S. House of Representatives, WI-6 (2015- ), Subcommittee on National Security, the Border, and Foreign Affairs, Chairman (2023-2025), US House of Representatives, WI-06 (2015- ), House Oversight Subcommittee on National Security, the Border, and Foreign Affairs, Chairman (2023-2024).
