## Gordon Gray

Gordon Gray is a person on the board; board tier B, documented channel and capability, programme role indicated but not yet nailed to a primary source; dates 1951-04-04; axes C2 A2 D1; 4 connections.

In one line: First Director of the Psychological Strategy Board, appointed 1951; Secretary of the Army 1949-50; Special Assistant to the President for National Security Affairs 1958-61.

### The longer account

Gordon Gray held, in sequence, four offices that between them cover the whole of this board's early period: Secretary of the Army, first Director of the Psychological Strategy Board, Assistant Secretary of Defense for International Security Affairs, and Special Assistant to the President for National Security Affairs. The Psychological Strategy Board is the one that matters here. President Truman established it by a secret directive of 4 April 1951 to coordinate the psychological operations of the whole government, sitting a Board of the Under Secretary of State, the Deputy Secretary of Defense and the Director of Central Intelligence over a Director designated by the President, and Gray was that Director. The directive gives the Director a claim on the participating departments for assistance and access to information, and in the same breath forbids the Board and the Director to conduct any psychological operations themselves. Gray reaches this corpus only as a name inside the MJ-12 attribution, and none of the documents opened here bears on that; the reason to carry him is the office.

### Recorded as unverified

- That Gordon Gray was president of the University of North Carolina at Chapel Hill in 1957, or at any date after April 1951. The only opened record placing him at UNC is the FRUS footnote of April 1951, and the UNC department history of the Institute of Field Physics never names him.
- Every MJ-12 attribution. The 1987 Lear document names him as the last surviving original member and the corpus gives two different death years; nothing opened bears on either.

### Why it holds its tier

Re-graded B on a global pass (rank 300 of 1057, score 37). 4 connections, 0 of them custody/witness/funding/programmatic. Axes C2/A2/D1.

### The claims on the record

- **Tier 1 documented.** Gray was the first Director. The directive's own editorial footnote reads: 'Gordon Gray, President of the University of North Carolina and former Secretary of the Army, was appointed Director of the new board.' Source: FRUS 1951 vol. I doc. 16, footnote 3, https://history.state.gov/historicaldocuments/frus1951v01/d16
- **Tier 1 documented.** Gray's offices and their dates, from the Office of the Historian's own persons list: 'Secretary of the Army from June 20, 1949 to April 12, 1950; Director of the Psychological Strategy Board from 1951 to 1952; ... Assistant Secretary of Defense for International Security Affairs from July 1955 to February 1957; Director of Defense Mobilization from March 1957 to July 1958; Special Assistant to the President for National Security Affairs from July 1958 to January 1961'. Source: Persons, FRUS 1950-1955, The Intelligence Community, https://history.state.gov/historicaldocuments/frus1950-55Intel/persons
- **Tier 1 documented.** He is recorded chairing the Board's business in its own minutes: PSB M-2 of 13 August 1951 lists 'Mr. Gordon Gray, Director, Psychological Strategy Board' present with the Director of Central Intelligence as acting chairman, the Under Secretary of State and the Deputy Secretary of Defense. Source: Minutes of a Meeting of the Psychological Strategy Board, PSB M-2, 13 August 1951, FRUS 1950-1955 The Intelligence Community doc. 82, https://history.state.gov/historicaldocuments/frus1950-55Intel/d82
- **Tier 1 documented.** The Director's charter includes a standing claim on other departments: 'The participating departments and agencies shall afford to the Director and the staff such assistance and access to information as may be specifically requested by the Director in carrying out his assigned duties.' The same directive states: 'This directive does not authorize the Board nor the Director to perform any "psychological operations".' Source: FRUS 1951 vol. I doc. 16, https://history.state.gov/historicaldocuments/frus1951v01/d16
- **Tier 1 documented.** Truman established the Board by directive of 4 April 1951: 'It is the purpose of this directive to authorize and provide for the more effective planning, coordination and conduct, within the framework of approved national policies, of psychological operations.' The Board reports to the National Security Council and is composed of the Under Secretary of State, the Deputy Secretary of Defense and the Director of Central Intelligence. Source: Directive by the President to the Secretary of State, the Secretary of Defense (Marshall), and the Director of Central Intelligence (Smith), 4 April 1951, file 100.4-PSB/4-451, FRUS 1951 vol. I doc. 16, https://history.state.gov/historicaldocuments/frus1951v01/d16
- **Tier 2 named person on the record, allegation shaped wording as stored.** On AREA52 - DEBRIEFED with Chris Ramsay, episode 22, 17 January 2025, at 0:16:02, Ramsay read a prepared list describing Gray as having 'experience in managing information and propaganda during the cold war aligning with MJ12's need for controlling UFO related narratives' and as 'allegedly involved in developing policies to manage public perception of UFO phenomena'. No document opened for this node connects the Psychological Strategy Board to UFO matters. Source: AREA52 - DEBRIEFED with Chris Ramsay, ep. 22, 17 January 2025, 0:16:02

## H. Marshall Chadwell

H. Marshall Chadwell is a person on the board; board tier B, documented channel and capability, programme role indicated but not yet nailed to a primary source; dates 1952-12-02; axes C1 A3 D0; 4 connections.

In one line: Assistant Director, CIA Office of Scientific Intelligence.

### The longer account

H. Marshall Chadwell headed the CIA's Office of Scientific Intelligence in 1952 and is the man who moved the UFO problem out of the Air Force and into the intelligence community. After the July 1952 Washington flap he wrote a series of memoranda to the Director of Central Intelligence, 11 September and 2 October 1952 among them, noting that official reports had totalled 250 in July 1952 alone and that 'the flying saucer situation contains two elements of danger which... have national security implications.' The two elements were psychological warfare vulnerability and the saturation of air defence reporting channels. Chadwell proposed a study group to systematise the problem, floating MIT's Project Lincoln and Caltech as venues, and it was Chadwell who, with H. P. Robertson, assembled the January 1953 panel. His own agency's official history states that CIA sponsorship of that panel was forbidden from being mentioned. Chadwell is therefore the origin point of a documented US government decision to treat public belief, rather than the objects, as the thing to be managed.

Affiliations recorded: CIA Office of Scientific Intelligence, Assistant Director (1951-1953), Atomic Energy Commission (prior).

### Why it holds its tier

Re-graded B on a global pass (rank 515 of 1057, score 29). 4 connections, 0 of them custody/witness/funding/programmatic. Axes C1/A3/D0.

### The claims on the record

- **Tier 1 documented.** 'In January 1953, Chadwell and H. P. Robertson... put together a distinguished panel of nonmilitary scientists to study the UFO issue.' Source: Gerald K. Haines, 'A Die-hard Issue: CIA's Role in the Study of UFOs, 1947-1990', Studies in Intelligence, 1997
- **Tier 1 documented.** CIA recorded that during July 1952 alone official reports of unidentified aerial phenomena totalled 250. Source: Same CIA 1952 memoranda series
- **Tier 1 documented.** Chadwell proposed a study group to analyse and systematise the factors, determine fields requiring investigation and make research recommendations, suggesting MIT's Project Lincoln or Caltech. Source: CIA Office of Scientific Intelligence memoranda, September-December 1952
- **Tier 1 documented.** Chadwell wrote to the DCI on 11 September 1952 that 'the flying saucer situation contains two elements of danger which... have national security implications.' Source: Chadwell memorandum to Director of Central Intelligence, subject: Flying Saucers, 11 Sep 1952 (declassified CIA document sampler)

## J. Allen Hynek

J. Allen Hynek is a person on the board; board tier B, documented channel and capability, programme role indicated but not yet nailed to a primary source; dates 1968-07-29; axes C1 A2 D3; 4 connections.

In one line: Astronomer; USAF scientific consultant 1948-1969.

### The longer account

J. Allen Hynek was the Air Force's scientific consultant on unidentified objects continuously from Project SIGN in 1948 through Blue Book's termination in 1969, twenty-one years, longer than anyone else in the system. He was hired to explain sightings away as astronomical misidentifications and did so effectively for a decade. His conversion is the single best-documented insider defection of the legacy era. In March 1966, sent to Michigan after mass sightings near Dexter and Hillsdale, he offered swamp gas as a possible explanation for some cases; the press stripped the qualifiers, the explanation became a national joke, and Hynek concluded he had been used as the front for an institutional posture rather than an inquiry. He testified to the House Committee on Science and Astronautics on 29 July 1968 and by then was stating publicly that Blue Book was procedurally inadequate, under-resourced and 'totally inadequate' as a research effort, a public relations exercise with 'little or no research undertaken using the scientific method.' Hynek is the proof that the Air Force's own scientist concluded the Air Force was not investigating.

Affiliations recorded: Ohio State University (1936-1956), Project SIGN / GRUDGE / Blue Book, scientific consultant (1948-1969), Northwestern University, Department of Astronomy chair (1960-1978), Center for UFO Studies, founder (1973).

### Why it holds its tier

Re-graded B on a global pass (rank 275 of 1057, score 39). 4 connections, 3 of them custody/witness/funding/programmatic. Axes C1/A2/D3.

### The claims on the record

- **Tier 1 documented.** Hynek served as scientific consultant across Project SIGN (1947-1949), Project GRUDGE (1949-1951) and Project Blue Book (1952-1969). Source: Project Blue Book administrative records, NARA RG 341; Condon Report Sec. V Ch. 2
- **Tier 1 documented.** Hynek testified before the House Committee on Science and Astronautics at its UFO symposium on 29 July 1968, alongside James E. McDonald, Carl Sagan, Robert L. Hall, James A. Harder and Robert M. L. Baker Jr. Source: US House Committee on Science and Astronautics, 'Symposium on Unidentified Flying Objects', 90th Congress, 2nd Session, 29 July 1968
- **Tier 2 named person on the record.** Hynek stated that Blue Book had become 'a public relations exercise' with 'little or no research undertaken using the scientific method.' Source: Hynek, statements from the late 1960s and 'The UFO Experience: A Scientific Inquiry' (1972)
- **Tier 2 named person on the record.** Hynek's March 1966 swamp gas explanation was offered as provisional and for only some cases, but was reported as a blanket dismissal; he later expressed regret. Source: Hynek's own account in 'The UFO Experience' (1972) and contemporaneous press coverage

## James Comer Jr.

## James E. McDonald

James E. McDonald is a person on the board; board tier B, documented channel and capability, programme role indicated but not yet nailed to a primary source; dates 1968-07-29; axes C0 A1 D3; 4 connections.

In one line: Senior physicist, Institute of Atmospheric Physics, University of Arizona.

### The longer account

James E. McDonald was a senior atmospheric physicist at the University of Arizona and the most aggressive scientific critic the Air Force UFO program ever faced. He obtained access to Blue Book files, re-investigated cases himself, and concluded the program was not an investigation at all. On 29 July 1968 he testified to the House Committee on Science and Astronautics, telling Congress that 'credible observers are observing relatively incredible objects' and that the phenomenon warranted serious scientific attention rather than dismissal by ridicule. He was the conduit through which the Low memo reached the press, and he attacked the Condon Report publicly after its release. McDonald was subsequently humiliated in a 1971 congressional hearing on the SST, an unrelated subject on which he was a technical critic, when a member used his UFO interest to discredit him from the dais. He shot himself in the Arizona desert on 13 June 1971. His death is the earliest entry in the pattern this board tracks of scientific and official careers destroyed on this subject, and it is recorded here as such without any implication beyond the documented sequence.

Affiliations recorded: University of Arizona, Institute of Atmospheric Physics (1954-1971), American Meteorological Society.

### Why it holds its tier

Re-graded B on a global pass (rank 373 of 1057, score 34). 4 connections, 1 of them custody/witness/funding/programmatic. Axes C0/A1/D3.

### The claims on the record

- **Tier 1 documented.** McDonald is indexed in the Condon Report at pages 927 and 928, and separately for his part in Case 50 (657-662), Case 52 (683-684, 690, 692), his views on future research (1269), the northeast power outage (167) and the extraterrestrial hypothesis (34). He is the most heavily indexed outside critic in the study that was meant to close the question. Source: Condon Report, Volume 3, index https://archive.org/details/DTIC_AD0680977
- **Tier 1 documented.** McDonald testified before the House Committee on Science and Astronautics on 29 July 1968 as senior physicist at the University of Arizona Institute of Atmospheric Physics. Source: US House Committee on Science and Astronautics, 'Symposium on Unidentified Flying Objects', 90th Congress, 2nd Session, 29 July 1968
- **Tier 1 documented.** McDonald told the committee that 'credible observers are observing relatively incredible objects' and argued the phenomenon deserved serious scientific investigation rather than dismissal through ridicule. Source: Same hearing transcript
- **Tier 1 documented.** The Condon Report acknowledges McDonald directly: 'The help of Dr. McDonald, Institute for Atmospheric Physics, University of Arizona, who conducted a study of UFO phenomena concurrently with this study, was invaluable in bringing a number of cases to our attention.' The board's description of him as a senior atmospheric physicist is the project's own. Source: Condon Report, Volume 1, acknowledgements https://archive.org/details/DTIC_AD0680975
- **Tier 1 documented.** The report records that 'in August 1967 Prof. James McDonald of Arizona made a 20-day trip to Australia, Tasmania and New Zealand in the course of which he interviewed some 80 persons who had made UFO reports there', and that on his return he briefed the Colorado project on what he found. Source: Condon Report, Volume 1 https://archive.org/details/DTIC_AD0680975
- **Tier 2 named person on the record.** McDonald concluded after reviewing Blue Book case files that the program was not conducting genuine scientific investigation. Source: McDonald's 1968 testimony and contemporaneous papers to scientific societies
- **Tier 3 stated inference.** The 1971 SST hearing ridicule and McDonald's death three months later form the earliest instance of the reputational-destruction pattern that recurs through the disclosure record; no causal claim beyond the documented sequence is asserted. Source: Inference from the hearing record and the coroner's finding

### Its strongest connections

- James E. McDonald runs out to **Project Blue Book** (program, board tier A), a investigative link at Tier 1 documented. The link reads: McDonald reviewed Blue Book files and told Congress it was not an investigation. Testifying on 29 July 1968 he told the House Science and Astronautics Committee that 'credible observers are observing relatively incredible objects' and that the subject deserved science rather than ridicule. Sources: House Committee on Science and Astronautics, Symposium on UFOs, 29 July 1968, http://nicap.org/books/1968Sym/1968_UFO_Symposium.pdf, dated 1968-07-29.
- James E. McDonald comes in from **J. Allen Hynek** (person, board tier B), a witness-chain link at Tier 1 documented. The link reads: Both testified at the 29 July 1968 congressional symposium. The Air Force's own long-serving consultant and its most aggressive scientific critic appeared at the same hearing, both concluding the official program was inadequate. Sources: House Committee on Science and Astronautics, Symposium on UFOs, 29 July 1968, http://nicap.org/books/1968Sym/1968_UFO_Symposium.pdf, dated 1968-07-29.
- James E. McDonald runs out to **University of Colorado UFO Project (Condon Committee)** (program, board tier A), a behavioral link at Tier 2 named person on the record. The link reads: McDonald attacked the Condon Report and was professionally destroyed. He publicly criticised the report on release, was ridiculed over his UFO work during 1971 congressional SST hearings on an unrelated technical subject, and died by suicide in June 1971. Sources: House Committee on Science and Astronautics, Symposium on UFOs, 29 July 1968, http://nicap.org/books/1968Sym/1968_UFO_Symposium.pdf, dated 1968-07-29.
- James E. McDonald runs out to **Low Memorandum ("the trick would be...")** (document, board tier C), a investigative link at Tier 2 named person on the record. The link reads: McDonald was the conduit that put the memo into public circulation. McDonald received the memo from project staff and it reached John Fuller, who published it in Look in May 1968 as 'Flying Saucer Fiasco'. Sources: Scientific Study of Unidentified Flying Objects (Condon Report), https://files.ncas.org/condon/, dated 1968-11-01.

## Jay Stratton

Jay Stratton is a person on the board; board tier A, named in sworn testimony or primary documents as directly inside the programme; dates 2009-01-01; axes C2 A3 D2; 4 connections.

In one line: DIA intelligence officer; AAWSAP participant; later Director, UAP Task Force.

### The longer account

Stratton is the pivot between the legacy effort and the official one. He is publicly identified as the director of the Unidentified Aerial Phenomena Task Force during its operative period, 2020-2021, the body Deputy Secretary of Defense David Norquist approved on 4 August 2020 and placed under the Department of the Navy with Office of Naval Intelligence cognizance. It was Stratton's task force that produced the classified underpinnings of the ODNI Preliminary Assessment delivered to Congress on 25 June 2021 - the report that put 144 incidents on the public record and conceded that 143 of them could not be explained. Grusch's account places the same office at the centre of the containment problem: he testified that in 2019 the UAP Task Force Director tasked him to identify every special access programme relevant to the Task Force's congressionally mandated mission, and that the read-ons were then refused. Stratton's earlier career runs through the DIA and the Advanced Aerospace Weapon System Applications Program orbit, and he has spoken publicly about a personal sighting and about the Task Force's findings. He retired from government at the end of 2021, before AARO existed, and in September 2024 HarperCollins imprint William Morrow acquired rights to his memoir - an announcement that is itself a documented fact about the trajectory of a man who once ran the government's UAP portfolio. AARO absorbed his Task Force by direction of the 15 July 2022 establishment memorandum, which ordered the Secretary of the Navy to disestablish the UAPTF and transfer its data and materials.

### Where the record conflicts

- Wikipedia's AARO entry names Brennan McKernan as UAPTF director; the widely reported and self-attested directorship in the operative 2020-2021 period is Stratton's. The two may reflect different phases or Navy-versus-OUSD(I&S) lines of authority.

### Recorded as unverified

- Stratton's exact title and duties on AAWSAP; the DIA personnel record has not been released.
- Whether Stratton was aware of, or party to, the denial of Grusch's read-on requests; whether the memoir has been published or held.
- The exact dates of Stratton's tenure as UAPTF director have never been published in a DoD document.

Affiliations recorded: Defense Intelligence Agency (2000s-2021), AAWSAP/AATIP participant (2008-2012), Director, Unidentified Aerial Phenomena Task Force (2020-2021), DIA, AAWSAP era, Office of Naval Intelligence, UAP Task Force, Director, Author, forthcoming memoir (William Morrow/HarperCollins, acquired 2024), Defense Intelligence Agency, UAP Task Force, Director (2020-2021).

### Why it holds its tier

Re-graded A on a global pass (rank 103 of 1057, score 61). 6 connections, 5 of them custody/witness/funding/programmatic. Axes C2/A3/D2.

### The claims on the record

- **Tier 1 documented.** HarperCollins imprint William Morrow acquired the memoir rights of the former director of the UAP Task Force in September 2024. Source: Deadline, HarperCollins Acquires Rights To Bombshell Memoir From Jay Stratton, September 2024
- **Tier 1 documented.** The 15 July 2022 AARO establishment memorandum directed the Secretary of the Navy to disestablish the UAPTF and transfer its data and materials to AARO. Source: DoD memorandum, Establishment of the All-domain Anomaly Resolution Office, 15 July 2022
- **Tier 1 documented.** The UAP Task Force was formally approved by Deputy Secretary of Defense David L. Norquist on 4 August 2020 under the Department of the Navy, with OUSD(I&S) cognizance. Source: US Navy press release, 'Establishment of Unidentified Aerial Phenomena Task Force', 14 August 2020
- **Tier 2 named person on the record.** Grusch testified that the UAP Task Force Director tasked him in 2019 to identify all special access programmes needed to satisfy the Task Force's congressionally mandated mission. Source: David Grusch, sworn testimony, House Oversight Committee, 26 July 2023
- **Tier 2 named person on the record.** Stratton has stated he has had personal experience with the phenomenon. Source: Jay Stratton public remarks and interviews, 2022-2023
- **Tier 2 named person on the record.** Stratton participated in the AAWSAP effort and later directed the UAP Task Force, providing direct institutional continuity from the 2008 DIA contract to the 2021 ODNI assessment. Source: Lacatski, Kelleher and Knapp, 'Skinwalkers at the Pentagon' (2021); Stratton public statements 2022-2023
- **Tier 2 named person on the record.** Stratton served as director of the UAP Task Force during 2020-2021 and had previously worked in the DIA/AAWSAP orbit. Source: Named-source journalism and his own public statements; publisher announcement of his memoir, Deadline, September 2024
- **Tier 3 stated inference.** Stratton is the UAP Task Force Director who in 2019 tasked Grusch to identify all SAPs and CAPs relevant to the Task Force's congressional mandate. Reasoning: the sworn testimony fixes the tasking to 2019 and to the Task Force Director; Stratton is the individual publicly identified as directing that effort in that period. Source: Inference from Grusch sworn testimony, 26 July 2023, plus public reporting on Stratton's UAPTF directorship

A further 1 claims sit on this node on the board and are not reproduced here.

## Karl E. Nell

Karl E. Nell is a person on the board; board tier B, documented channel and capability, programme role indicated but not yet nailed to a primary source; dates 2023-06-05; axes C1 A3 D3; 4 connections.

In one line: Col. (ret.), US Army; Army liaison to the UAP Task Force 2021-2022.

### The longer account

Nell is the most consequential corroborator in the Grusch record because he attached his own name to it while Grusch's credibility was still being contested. A retired Army colonel who served as the Army's liaison to the UAP Task Force from 2021 to 2022, Nell told Kean and Blumenthal on the record for the 5 June 2023 Debrief piece that Grusch is 'beyond reproach' and, more importantly, that Grusch's assertion concerning the existence of a terrestrial arms race occurring sub rosa over the past eighty years focused on reverse engineering technologies of unknown origin is fundamentally correct, as is the indisputable realization that at least some of those technologies derive from non-human intelligence. That is not a character reference; it is a Task Force liaison independently affirming the substance. He escalated in May 2024 at the SALT iConnections conference: non-human intelligence exists, has been interacting with humanity, the interaction is not new, unelected people in government are aware of it, and there is zero doubt. AARO's Historical Record Report never engages Nell's affirmation by name; it addresses interviewees in the aggregate and asserts none had firsthand knowledge - a formulation that does not answer a named Task Force liaison speaking in the first person.

### Recorded as unverified

- Which specific programs or individuals underpin Nell's 'zero doubt' formulation - he has never enumerated them publicly.

Affiliations recorded: US Army, Colonel (ret.), UAP Task Force, Army liaison (2021-2022), The Sol Foundation (advisory), UAP Disclosure Fund (board, resigned 2025).

### Why it holds its tier

Re-graded B on a global pass (rank 347 of 1057, score 35). 4 connections, 2 of them custody/witness/funding/programmatic. Axes C1/A3/D3.

### The claims on the record

- **Tier 2 named person on the record.** 'Beyond reproach' - Nell's on-record characterization of Grusch. Source: Karl Nell to Leslie Kean and Ralph Blumenthal, The Debrief, 5 June 2023
- **Tier 2 named person on the record.** 'Non-human intelligence exists, non-human intelligence has been interacting with humanity. This interaction is not new, and it's been ongoing, and unelected people in the government are aware of that. There's zero doubt.' Source: Karl Nell, SALT iConnections conference, May 2024
- **Tier 2 named person on the record.** Grusch's assertion of an eighty-year sub rosa arms race focused on reverse engineering technologies of unknown origin is 'fundamentally correct,' and at least some of those technologies derive from non-human intelligence. Source: Karl Nell, on the record, The Debrief, 5 June 2023

### Its strongest connections

- Karl E. Nell comes in from **The Sol Foundation** (institution, board tier A), a institutional link at Tier 1 documented. The link reads: Nell published 'The UFO Enigma: A Call for US Government Disclosure' through Sol in May 2025. The foundation's white-paper series is the venue through which former programme-adjacent officials put structured disclosure arguments into the public record under their own names. Sources: The Sol Foundation - White Papers and Policy Briefings index, https://thesolfoundation.org/white-papers/, dated 2026-08-12.
- Karl E. Nell runs out to **House Oversight joint hearing, 13 November 2024 - 'Unidentified Anomalous Phenomena: Exposing the Truth'** (event, board tier A), a witness-chain link at Tier 1 documented. The link reads: Mace read his 'nonhuman intelligence exists' statement into the hearing record. Nell did not testify; his May 2024 conference statement was quoted from the chair. Sources: U.S. GPO, Hearing transcript, Serial No. 118-135 (CHRG-118hhrg57440), https://www.govinfo.gov/content/pkg/CHRG-118hhrg57440/html/CHRG-118hhrg57440.htm, dated 2024-11-13.
- Karl E. Nell runs out to **David Charles Grusch** (person, board tier A), a witness-chain link at Tier 2 named person on the record. The link reads: On-record corroboration: 'beyond reproach' and the arms race is 'fundamentally correct'. A named retired Army colonel who served as the Army's UAP Task Force liaison affirmed both Grusch's character and the substance of the eighty-year reverse-engineering allegation, in print, on the record, on 5 June 2023. AARO has never addressed this statement by name. Sources: The Debrief, https://thedebrief.org/intelligence-officials-say-u-s-has-retrieved-non-human-craft/, dated 2023-06-05; A Very Exciting Time - Karl Nell at SALT, https://veryexcitingtime.com/episodes/2024/45-nell-salt/, dated 2024-05-27.
- Karl E. Nell runs out to **Unidentified Aerial Phenomena Task Force (UAPTF)** (program, board tier A), a institutional link at Tier 2 named person on the record. The link reads: Army liaison to the UAP Task Force, 2021-2022. Nell's Task Force role is what converts his corroboration from opinion into positional testimony: he sat inside the same organization Grusch reported to. Sources: The Debrief, https://thedebrief.org/intelligence-officials-say-u-s-has-retrieved-non-human-craft/, dated 2023-06-05.

## Kevin Day

Kevin Day is a person on the board; board tier C, contextual, real and relevant, no direct programme linkage on the record yet; dates 2004-11-14; axes C1 A1 D2; 4 connections.

In one line: Senior Chief Operations Specialist / Air Warfare Coordinator, USS Princeton (CG-59), Nov 2004.

### The longer account

Day was the Senior Chief Operations Specialist running the air picture in USS Princeton's combat information center during the two weeks of contacts that culminated in the 14 November 2004 intercept. His role is the load-bearing one for the radar side of the case: the Princeton's SPY-1B, freshly upgraded, was the sensor that established the objects were descending from above 80,000 feet, that they were numerous, and that they were not tracks the Aegis library recognised. Fravor's own sworn statement to House Oversight corroborates the substance of Day's picture - that Princeton 'had been observing these objects on their Aegis combat system for the previous 2 weeks' descending 'from above 80,000ft ... rapidly down to 20,000ft.' Day has said publicly in documentary and broadcast interviews that within a day or two of the intercept, personnel who did not identify themselves arrived aboard Princeton and removed recording media, and that he was told to stop discussing it. That confiscation account is the pivot of the whole coverup strand for this incident and it has not yet been anchored to a primary record - no FOIA release, no visit log, no message traffic identifying the personnel or the authority under which they acted has surfaced. It is recorded here as a tier-2 attributed account with the primary-record gap stated plainly, because a fabricated citation would destroy exactly the claim that most needs to survive audit.

### Where the record conflicts

- Fravor testified under his own name that 'tapes were never taken.' Day says media were taken from Princeton. The reconciliation is platform-specific collection, but the conflict is real and should be presented, not smoothed.

### Recorded as unverified

- The identity, service, and authorising command of the personnel Day says came aboard USS Princeton.
- Any Princeton visit log, quarterdeck log entry, or message traffic for 14-17 Nov 2004 covering an unscheduled embarkation.
- Whether Princeton's SPY-1B recorded data tapes for that period exist in any Navy archive.

Affiliations recorded: US Navy (retired Senior Chief Operations Specialist), USS Princeton (CG-59), Carrier Strike Group 11 (2004).

### Why it holds its tier

Re-graded C on a global pass (rank 541 of 1057, score 28). 4 connections, 2 of them custody/witness/funding/programmatic. Axes C1/A1/D2.

### The claims on the record

- **Tier 1 documented.** Princeton tracked the objects on Aegis for roughly two weeks before the intercept, observing descents from above 80,000 ft to 20,000 ft. Source: David Fravor, written statement to House Oversight, 26 July 2023 (corroborating the Princeton radar picture Day ran)
- **Tier 1 documented.** The Navy's own range fouler report on the 14 Nov 2004 incident states 'Reporting terminated at 3rd Fleet, there was no process to share the reporting.' Source: Navy range fouler reports released via Navy FOIA reading room 13 Jan 2023, quoted by The War Zone, 14 Jan 2023
- **Tier 3 stated inference.** Recording media documenting the Princeton radar tracks were removed by personnel who did not identify themselves within roughly 48 hours of the intercept. Reasoning: the account is consistent across Day's public interviews and with Gary Voorhis's independent account of being ordered to reformat/hand over data, and it is compatible with - not contradicted by - Fravor's statement that no tapes were taken from his aircrew, since the two men describe collection aimed at different platforms. It remains inference until a visit log, message, or FOIA release names the personnel. Source: Kevin Day, public documentary and broadcast interviews, 2018-2019; no primary record located as of Aug 2026

### Its strongest connections

- Kevin Day comes in from **David Fravor** (person, board tier B), a behavioral link at Tier 1 documented. The link reads: Direct conflict over whether data was taken. Fravor testified under his own name that 'tapes were never taken'; Day says media were removed from Princeton. The conflict resolves only if collection was platform-specific, and it is recorded here unresolved. Sources: House Oversight - Fravor written statement (PDF), https://oversight.house.gov/wp-content/uploads/2023/07/David-Fravor-Statement-for-House-Oversight-Committee.pdf, dated 2023-07-26; The War Zone - Tic Tac incident in newly released range fouler reports, https://www.twz.com/tic-tac-uap-incident-included-in-72-newly-released-range-incursion-reports, dated 2023-01-14.
- Kevin Day runs out to **USS Nimitz Carrier Strike Group Encounters, 10-16 November 2004** (event, board tier A), a witness-chain link at Tier 2 named person on the record. The link reads: Ran the Princeton air picture and vectored the intercept. As Senior Chief Operations Specialist Day was the air warfare coordinator holding the tracks. Fravor's sworn statement corroborates the substance of the radar picture Day describes. Sources: House Oversight - Fravor written statement (PDF), https://oversight.house.gov/wp-content/uploads/2023/07/David-Fravor-Statement-for-House-Oversight-Committee.pdf, dated 2023-07-26.
- Kevin Day runs out to **USS Princeton (CG-59)** (institution, board tier B), a institutional link at Tier 2 named person on the record. The link reads: Senior Chief Operations Specialist aboard USS Princeton, Nov 2004. Day's billet is the reason his account of the radar record and its removal carries weight; he is the man who owned the air picture. Sources: House Oversight - Fravor written statement (PDF), https://oversight.house.gov/wp-content/uploads/2023/07/David-Fravor-Statement-for-House-Oversight-Committee.pdf, dated 2023-07-26.
- Kevin Day runs out to **Gary Voorhis** (person, board tier C), a witness-chain link at Tier 3 stated inference. The link reads: Independent shipmate corroboration of the post-encounter data collection. Two Princeton crewmen in unrelated divisions give converging accounts of unidentified personnel removing recorded material - evidentiary convergence short of documentary proof. Sources: The War Zone - Tic Tac incident in newly released range fouler reports, https://www.twz.com/tic-tac-uap-incident-included-in-72-newly-released-range-incursion-reports, dated 2023-01-14.

## Louis Witten

Louis Witten is a person on the board; board tier C, contextual, real and relevant, no direct programme linkage on the record yet; dates 1957-01-18; axes C1 A1 D0; 4 connections.

In one line: General relativist; first researcher hired by RIAS, Inc., the Glenn L. Martin Company's research institute.

### The longer account

Louis Witten is on this board for one documented fact and its context: in January 1957 he attended the Chapel Hill conference on the role of gravitation in physics as a representative of RIAS, Inc., Baltimore. The participants list of the conference report prints it plainly -- 'Witten, L. | RIAS, Inc., Baltimore' -- immediately above 'Bass, R.W. | RIAS, Inc., Baltimore'. His contribution, with R. W. Bass, was 'Some Remarks on Cosmological Models'. The report's own designation is WADC Technical Report 57-216 and it exists because of a conference funding agreement with the Wright Air Development Center, which is to say an Air Force body paid for the meeting at which an aerospace prime's research institute discussed gravitation.

What is documented about his work is classical general relativity, not gravity control. The conference participants themselves recorded a concern about 'the absolute necessity to avoid identification with so-called anti-gravity research', and nothing read in this pass shows RIAS producing gravity-control results. The board carries him as the hire that establishes what RIAS actually was, and it does not carry the claim that George Trimble recruited him -- the one source that could be opened says Witten approached Bryce DeWitt to recruit DeWitt, and the oral history that would settle the direction refuses this environment.

### Why it holds its tier

Re-graded C on a global pass (rank 607 of 1057, score 26). 4 connections, 0 of them custody/witness/funding/programmatic. Axes C1/A1/D0.

### The claims on the record

- **Tier 1 documented.** The January 1957 Chapel Hill conference had one sponsor and four funders, and the Air Force was not the sponsor. The report's title page reads: 'UNDER THE SPONSORSHIP OF THE International Union of Pure and Applied Physics, with financial support from UNESCO / National Science Foundation / Wright Air Development Center, U.S. Air Force / Office of Ordnance Research, U.S. Army'. The Foreword gives the reason the report exists at all: 'The present report was undertaken as a necessary requirement to obtain conference funds.' WADC's imprint is on the cover because the report was the condition of the money, not because the Air Force convened the meeting. Source: The Role of Gravitation in Physics: Report from the 1957 Chapel Hill Conference, WADC Technical Report 57-216, title page and Foreword by Cecile M. DeWitt, 15 March 1957, https://edition-open-sources.org/sources/5/5/index.html
- **Tier 1 documented.** The conference report's official designation is WADC Technical Report 57-216, and it exists because of a conference funding agreement with the Wright Air Development Center -- an Air Force body. Source: The Role of Gravitation in Physics: Report from the 1957 Chapel Hill Conference, title page and editorial matter
- **Tier 1 documented.** The participants list of the January 1957 Chapel Hill conference on the role of gravitation in physics prints 'Witten, L. | RIAS, Inc., Baltimore', directly above 'Bass, R.W. | RIAS, Inc., Baltimore'. Witten's contribution, with R. W. Bass, is 'Some Remarks on Cosmological Models'. Source: The Role of Gravitation in Physics: Report from the 1957 Chapel Hill Conference, eds. Rickles and DeWitt, participants list
- **Tier 2 named person on the record.** A named historian on the record states that 'Louis Witten (the father of Ed Witten, and participant in the Chapel Hill conference) was the first researcher hired by RIAS, of the Glenn Martin Company.' Source: Dean Rickles, 'The Chapel Hill Conference in Context', in the Edition Open Sources edition of the 1957 conference report
- **Tier 2 named person on the record.** On American Alchemy with Jesse Michels, 31 August 2025, at 2:07:12 and 2:08:06, Michels said that at the time of the Avro car project Wright Field also had 'a dedicated theoretical physics group' headed by a man the caption renders as 'Josh Goldberg', and that Agnew Bahnson, who ran the University of North Carolina's Institute of Field Physics with Bryce DeWitt, recorded in diaries found years later that he had been told to get in touch with Townsend Brown because he wanted to build a spaceship. Neither Goldberg nor Bahnson appears anywhere in the board's research files, the caption spells Bahnson 'Bonson', and no diary, archive or document is named on air. Source: American Alchemy with Jesse Michels, 'NASA Doctor: I Saw A UFO Hovering In THIS Secret Hangar (Ft. Greg Rogers)', 31 August 2025, 2:07:12, https://mgln.ai/e/1119/pscrb.fm/rss/p/traffic.megaphone.fm/HPL2173204669.mp3?updated=1757589247
- **Tier 2 named person on the record.** The conference participants recorded a concern about 'the absolute necessity to avoid identification with so-called anti-gravity research'. Witten's documented output is classical general relativity, including exact electrovacuum solutions to the Einstein field equation; nothing read shows RIAS producing gravity-control results. Source: Dean Rickles, 'The Chapel Hill Conference in Context'; and the American Institute of Physics catalog abstract for the Louis Witten oral history

## Marik von Rennenkampff

Marik von Rennenkampff is a person on the board; board tier C, contextual, real and relevant, no direct programme linkage on the record yet; dates 2025-06-01; axes C0 A0 D2; 4 connections.

In one line: Opinion contributor, The Hill; former State Department official.

### The longer account

Marik von Rennenkampff occupies a narrow but useful position: he writes about UAP for The Hill, the trade paper of Congress, which means his arguments land in front of staffers rather than subscribers. His columns have pressed on the specific institutional questions this board cares about - whether the Pentagon has spread false UFO stories, what H.R. McMaster's remarks imply, why the alleged programme allegations strengthen the case for the UAP Disclosure Act, and why anomalous 'orbs' warrant scientific study. His value is that he argues from the public record and from official statements rather than from witness testimony, which makes his pieces usable as citations in a way that video interviews are not. He is a former Obama administration official at the State Department, which is the credential that gets him the column inches.

### Recorded as unverified

- thehill.com returns HTTP 403 to automated retrieval, so the byline on each of these specific pieces could not be re-verified in this pass; attribution to von Rennenkampff should be confirmed against the live page before citation.
- His exact State Department role and years of service are not established here.

Affiliations recorded: The Hill (opinion contributor), US Department of State (former).

## Michael Gold

Michael Gold is a person on the board; board tier B, documented channel and capability, programme role indicated but not yet nailed to a primary source; dates 2024-11-13; axes C0 A1 D2; 4 connections.

In one line: Former NASA Associate Administrator for Space Policy and Partnerships; NASA UAP Independent Study Team member.

### The longer account

Gold sat on the NASA UAP Independent Study Team and then, on 13 November 2024, took the panel's findings into the House Oversight hearing room as a witness. His written testimony is the clearest official-adjacent articulation of the aviator problem as a stigma problem: 'the stigmatization of the UAP phenomena is so harmful. The stigma prevents scientific inquiry.' He named Alex Dietrich specifically, saying she 'was mocked by her colleagues' and that her incident 'went years without any investigation' - a serving officer's career cost, entered into the congressional record by a former NASA associate administrator. His recommendations were structural rather than speculative: NASA should sponsor symposia to combat stigma, review archival data with machine learning, contact international partners, work with the FAA to establish civilian pilot reporting procedures, and leverage the Aviation Safety Reporting System. He also praised Daniel Evans, the study team's designated federal officer, for working 'under difficult conditions' - the euphemism that documents the harassment the panel absorbed.

Affiliations recorded: Bigelow Aerospace (Washington DC operations, prior), NASA Associate Administrator for Space Policy and Partnerships, NASA UAP Independent Study Team member (2022-2023), Redwire Space, NASA, Associate Administrator for Space Policy and Partnerships, NASA UAP Independent Study Team (2022-2023), Redwire Space (prior), Bigelow Aerospace (prior).

## Michael Shellenberger

Michael Shellenberger is a person on the board; board tier B, documented channel and capability, programme role indicated but not yet nailed to a primary source; dates 2024-11-13; axes C1 A0 D3; 4 connections.

In one line: Co-founder, Public; University of Austin faculty.

### The longer account

Shellenberger is the person who put a purported unacknowledged special access program into the Congressional Record. On the morning of 13 November 2024 he delivered to Congress a twelve-page document describing Immaculate Constellation as a central or parent USAP consolidating UAP observations from tasked and untasked collection platforms and holding high-quality imagery and measurement-and-signature intelligence; Chairwoman Nancy Mace moved it into the Record from the dais during the hearing, where it appears in the Index of Documents as 'Report, Pentagon, Immaculate Constellation; submitted by Rep. Mace.' Under adversarial questioning from Rep. Clay Higgins he confirmed he knows the author and the sources, has confidence in them, and was withholding their identity by choice. The Pentagon, through spokesperson Sue Gough, says it has no record of such a programme; former AARO director Sean Kirkpatrick denied its existence. Neither AARO annual report since - FY2024 or FY2025 - addresses it, and Kosloski's Senate statement for the record does not mention it. In 2025 a separate self-identified whistleblower claimed to have found the programme inside a folder labelled '2018 Schriever Wargame'; analyst Jason Colavito argued that person had mistaken fictitious wargame scenario documents for a real programme. That claim goes to the 2025 claimant's provenance story, not automatically to the 2024 twelve-page report, and the distinction is one the board keeps open rather than collapsing in either direction.

### Recorded as unverified

- The identity of the Immaculate Constellation source has not been publicly established.
- AARO's formal written response to the Immaculate Constellation submission was not retrieved in this pass.

Affiliations recorded: Public (co-founder), University of Austin (faculty, 2026), Environmental Progress, Breakthrough Institute (founder, 2003), Public (Substack), founder, Environmental Progress, founder, University of Austin, professor of politics.

### Why it holds its tier

Re-graded B on a global pass (rank 200 of 1057, score 44). 6 connections, 3 of them custody/witness/funding/programmatic. Axes C1/A0/D3.

- **Tier 1 documented.** Witness of record at the 13 November 2024 House Oversight joint hearing: 'Mr. Michael Shellenberger, Founder of Public'. Chairwoman Mace stated on the record that the 12-page Pentagon document describing the unacknowledged special access program 'Immaculate Constellation' was the document 'that Michael Shellenberger brought today', and entered it into the record without objection. Source: CHRG-118hhrg57440 CONTENTS and statement of Rep. Mace
- **Tier 2 named person on the record.** 'Immaculate Constellation serves as a central or parent USAP that consolidates observations of UAPs by both tasked and untasked collection platforms.' Source: Michael Shellenberger, sworn testimony, 13 November 2024

## Mike Rounds

Mike Rounds is a person on the board; board tier B, documented channel and capability, programme role indicated but not yet nailed to a primary source; dates 2024-07-11; axes C0 A2 D3; 4 connections.

In one line: R-SD; SASC and SSCI; lead Republican on the UAPDA.

### The longer account

Rounds is the Republican half of the UAPDA and, unlike Schumer, he has never stopped filing it. He was an original cosponsor of S.Amdt. 797 on 13 July 2023. When the provision was gutted in conference he took over as lead sponsor: on 11 July 2024 the full text, eminent domain clause intact, was submitted to the FY2025 NDAA as S.Amdt. 2610, captioned 'Mr. ROUNDS (for himself and Mr. Schumer),' and printed in the Congressional Record for that day. It was ordered to lie on the table and was never called up; the FY2025 NDAA, P.L. 118-159, signed 23 December 2024, contains no UAP disclosure division at all. Rounds sits on both the Armed Services and Intelligence Committees, the two panels that would have to be rolled to move this, and his position on the inside is what makes his persistence significant rather than symbolic. As of 2026 he is still at it: in his own newsletter for the week of 22-28 June 2026 he wrote, 'On Thursday, I spoke at the 2026 Disclosure Symposium, where I discussed the future of the Unidentified Anomalous Phenomena (UAP) Disclosure Act. I am once again cosponsoring as an amendment to this year's National Defense Authorization Act.' That FY2027 NDAA, S. 4784, was reported by the Armed Services Committee in June 2026 containing no UAP provisions of any kind, which means the vehicle Rounds is aiming at has again been built without a place for him.

Affiliations recorded: U.S. Senate, South Dakota (2015- ), Senate Armed Services Committee, Senate Select Committee on Intelligence, Governor of South Dakota (2003-2011).

### Why it holds its tier

Re-graded B on a global pass (rank 424 of 1057, score 32). 4 connections, 1 of them custody/witness/funding/programmatic. Axes C0/A2/D3.

### The claims on the record

- **Tier 1 documented.** First-listed cosponsor of S.Amdt. 797, the UAP Disclosure Act of 2023, submitted 13 July 2023. Source: CREC-2023-07-13-pt1-PgS2953
- **Tier 1 documented.** Rounds's own June 2026 list of provisions he secured in the SASC-reported FY2027 NDAA contains no UAP item, and the reported bill S. 4784 contains no UAP provisions. Source: rounds.senate.gov press release, 16 June 2026; S. 4784 (Reported in Senate), GPO
- **Tier 1 documented.** S.Amdt. 2610 to S. 4638, submitted 11 July 2024, is captioned 'Mr. ROUNDS (for himself and Mr. Schumer)' and contains the complete UAP Disclosure Act of 2024 including the eminent domain subsection; it was 'ordered to lie on the table.' Source: Congressional Record, 11 July 2024, S4577 et seq. (GPO)
- **Tier 2 named person on the record.** 'On Thursday, I spoke at the 2026 Disclosure Symposium, where I discussed the future of the Unidentified Anomalous Phenomena (UAP) Disclosure Act. I am once again cosponsoring as an amendment to this year's National Defense Authorization Act.' Source: Sen. Mike Rounds, Weekly Round[s] Up, 22-28 June 2026, rounds.senate.gov

## Michael R. Turner

Michael R. Turner is a person on the board; board tier B, documented channel and capability, programme role indicated but not yet nailed to a primary source; dates 2023-12-22; axes C0 A3 D0; 4 connections.

In one line: R-OH; Chairman, House Permanent Select Committee on Intelligence (2023-2025).

### The longer account

Turner is the name most consistently attached to the killing of the UAP Disclosure Act. As Chairman of the House Permanent Select Committee on Intelligence through the FY2024 NDAA conference he held one of the four corner positions on the intelligence side of the negotiation, and the two provisions reported to have been his targets, the eminent domain clause and the Review Board's subpoena power, are precisely the two the enacted statute does not contain. Attorney Daniel Sheehan, who lobbied the conference directly, has stated on the record that 'Mike Turner first attacked those two elements,' demanding the eminent domain provision and the subpoena power be taken out. Turner had already positioned himself against the underlying testimony: after the 26 July 2023 House Oversight hearing he said the striking thing was that 'repeatedly over and over again, the whistleblowers had to say, actually, I don't have any knowledge.' The documentary result is not in dispute even if the attribution is journalistic: the Senate passed Sections 9001-9015 on 27 July 2023; the conference product signed 22 December 2023 contains Sections 1841, 1842 and 1843 only. Sections 9007 through 9011, Review Board, Board personnel, review of records, eminent domain, court-sealed materials, have no enacted counterpart. Under the board's premise this is the single most consequential act of concealment in the modern legislative record, and it was performed by a committee chairman with access to the underlying compartments.

### Where the record conflicts

- Turner has not, so far as the public record shows, confirmed or denied Sheehan's account of what he demanded in conference.

### Recorded as unverified

- No conference document has been released naming which members struck which sections; the attribution rests on participant testimony and reporting, not on a signed record.

Affiliations recorded: U.S. House, Ohio (2003- ), Chairman, HPSCI (2023-2025), House Armed Services Committee.

### Why it holds its tier

Re-graded B on a global pass (rank 396 of 1057, score 33). 4 connections, 1 of them custody/witness/funding/programmatic, 2 official denials recorded against it. Axes C0/A3/D0.

- **Tier 2 named person on the record.** 'The most striking aspect of all the testimony was, repeatedly over and over again, the whistleblowers had to say, actually, I don't have any knowledge.' Source: Turner, 30 July 2023, quoted in The Debrief
- **Tier 2 named person on the record.** Daniel Sheehan states Turner 'first attacked those two elements', the eminent domain provision and the Review Board's subpoena power, and demanded both be removed. Source: Micah Hanks, The Debrief, 27 November 2023
- **Tier 2 named person on the record.** Turner and Rep. Mike Rogers are identified as leading the effort to eliminate or significantly alter the UAP Disclosure Act, with support from Sen. Mitch McConnell and Speaker Mike Johnson. Source: Micah Hanks, The Debrief, 27 November 2023

## Nathan F. Twining

Nathan F. Twining is a person on the board; board tier B, documented channel and capability, programme role indicated but not yet nailed to a primary source; dates 1947-09-23; axes C2 A3 D1; 4 connections.

In one line: Cmdr, Air Materiel Command; later CJCS.

### The longer account

Lieutenant General Nathan Farragut Twining commanded Air Materiel Command at Wright Field when the 1947 disc wave broke, which placed him at the exact node where recovered foreign materiel was exploited. On 23 September 1947 he signed the memorandum 'AMC Opinion Concerning "Flying Discs"' to Brig. Gen. George Schulgen stating flatly that 'the phenomenon reported is something real and not visionary or fictitious', the earliest surviving statement of official belief, written eleven weeks after Roswell. The same memo hedged in a way that has never been adequately explained: Twining told Schulgen consideration must be given to the possibility the objects were 'of domestic origin, the product of some high security project not known to AC/AS-2 or this Command,' which is a two-star general telling Air Force intelligence that a compartment might exist above them. He recommended a priority classified project, and Project SIGN was directed on 30 December 1947. Twining rose to Air Force Chief of Staff (1953-1957) and Chairman of the Joint Chiefs of Staff (1957-1960), meaning the man who wrote the founding memo then held the two positions best placed to keep whatever followed off the books. The 1954 revision of AFR 200-2, which restricted public discussion of unexplained cases, was issued under his tenure as Chief of Staff. He is also the named recipient in the disputed 14 July 1954 Cutler-Twining memo, which the FBI declared bogus, a fact recorded here as an artifact of the MJ-12 document problem, not as evidence for it.

### Recorded as unverified

- Whether Twining's 7 July 1947 trip to New Mexico was connected to the Roswell recovery; no primary itinerary has been released

Affiliations recorded: US Army Air Forces / Air Materiel Command (1945-1947), US Air Force Chief of Staff (1953-1957), Chairman, Joint Chiefs of Staff (1957-1960).

### Why it holds its tier

Re-graded B on a global pass (rank 299 of 1057, score 37). 4 connections, 0 of them custody/witness/funding/programmatic, 1 official denial recorded against it. Axes C2/A3/D1.

### The claims on the record

- **Tier 1 documented.** The same memo directed that consideration be given to the possibility the objects were 'of domestic origin -- the product of some high security project' unknown to Air Force intelligence or to AMC. Source: Condon Report, Section V Chapter 2, 'UFOs 1947-1968', quoting the Twining letter
- **Tier 1 documented.** Twining wrote that 'the phenomenon reported is something real and not visionary or fictitious.' Source: Twining to Schulgen, 'AMC Opinion Concerning "Flying Discs"', 23 Sep 1947; scan hosted by NICAP; quoted in Condon Report Sec. V Ch. 2
- **Tier 1 documented.** Twining's 23 September 1947 letter directed the establishment of a study of UFOs, which became Project SIGN. Source: Condon Report, Section V Chapter 2
- **Tier 3 stated inference.** An AMC commander writing that a 'high security project not known to this Command' may be responsible implies awareness in September 1947 that compartments existed above the Air Force's own technical intelligence chain, the structural precondition for a waived, unacknowledged program. Source: Inference from the text of the Twining memo; no primary record identifying such a compartment has been released

## Neill Tipton

Neill Tipton is a person on the board; board tier B, documented channel and capability, programme role indicated but not yet nailed to a primary source; dates 2019-05-07; axes C1 A3 D0; 4 connections.

In one line: Director for Defense Intelligence (Collection & Special Programs), OUSD(I).

### The longer account

Tipton is where the 'no assigned responsibilities' formula originates. Released emails show that on 7 May 2019, two weeks before Sherwood's public statements, Tipton, who had been Elizondo's superior from roughly 2010 to 2012, wrote internally that Elizondo 'had no assigned responsibilities for this program... At the time he was assigned to me, he brought with him no responsibilities for AATIP.' The same email chain shows a redacted official immediately flagging the risk: 'Neill raises an important issue here, the Department may be putting itself in an awkward position of both contradicting previous statements,' and another noting 'so while he may not have had any assigned responsibilities for these programs while in OUSD(I), if he did work on these programs while assigned to a different DoD organization... we have to confirm that.' The chain closes with the instruction 'we will have to keep our messages in synch.' What the coordination did not address were the September 2017 emails in which Elizondo drafted a memorandum helping Tipton 'assume the new responsibilities for AATIP'. Tipton is one of three officials Elizondo named in his May 2021 DoD IG complaint.

### Where the record conflicts

- Tipton's 2019 assertion that Elizondo brought no AATIP responsibilities is difficult to square with the September 2017 emails in which Elizondo is drafting AATIP transition paperwork for Tipton himself.

Affiliations recorded: OUSD(I), Director for Defense Intelligence (Collection & Special Programs).

## Nick Pope

Nick Pope is a person on the board; board tier C, contextual, real and relevant, no direct programme linkage on the record yet; dates 2026-04-06; axes C0 A1 D3; 4 connections.

In one line: MoD UFO desk officer, Sec(AS)2a, 1991-1994; author and disclosure commentator.

### The longer account

Nick Pope is the single most consequential figure in the British record because he is the only person who ran the Ministry of Defence's UFO desk and then spent thirty years describing what that desk actually did. Born 19 September 1965 to George Pope, an aeronautical engineer at the MoD's Royal Aircraft Establishment at Farnborough, he joined the Ministry of Defence as a civil servant in 1985 and was posted in 1991 to the Secretariat (Air Staff) division that handled unidentified flying object reports, staying three years. Sky HISTORY, the broadcaster for which he worked for over a decade, records that the British programme deliberately had no formal name of the Project Blue Book kind because the government wanted to play down the extent of its own interest. Pope's 1996 book Open Skies, Closed Minds argued from inside knowledge that the MoD's public line - that UFO reports were of no defence significance - did not match the internal handling. When the MoD and The National Archives began releasing the UFO files in 2008 he was approached to help with the publicity and became, in the broadcaster's phrase, the face of the Ministry's new-found transparency, a role that made him the recurring British witness in the 2017-2026 American disclosure wave. In February 2026 he disclosed on social media that he had been diagnosed with stage 4 oesophageal cancer that had metastasised to his liver, writing that he would not have swapped his life 'for the world' and referring to 'the secrets I've been privy to'. His wife Elizabeth Weiss announced that he died at their home on the afternoon of 6 April 2026, aged 60; obituaries followed in The Telegraph, The Times, The Economist and The New York Times through April 2026.

### Recorded as unverified

- Obituaries in The Telegraph (10 April 2026), The Times (15 April 2026), The Economist (16 April 2026) and The New York Times (28 April 2026, 'Nick Pope, U.F.O. Sleuth Who Chased the Truth, Dies at 60') were located by headline via Google News but the full texts were not retrieved; the paywalled obituaries should be read for the exact MoD post title and grade.
- Pope's precise MoD job title and division designation (commonly given as Secretariat (Air Staff) 2a) was not confirmed from a primary MoD document in this pass.

Affiliations recorded: uk-mod, uk-mod-ufo-desk.

### Why it holds its tier

### The claims on the record

- **Tier 2 named person on the record.** February 2026 statement: 'A while ago, following some digestive issues, I was diagnosed with oesophageal cancer. Unfortunately, it's Stage 4 and has metastasized to my liver' - adding a reference to 'the secrets I've been privy to'. ## Brigadeiro Octávio Júlio Moreira Lima

Brigadeiro Octávio Júlio Moreira Lima is a person on the board; board tier B, documented channel and capability, programme role indicated but not yet nailed to a primary source; dates 2011; axes C1 A2 D3; 4 connections.

In one line: Minister of Aeronautics of Brazil; held the 23 May 1986 'Night of the UFOs' press conference.

### The longer account

Octávio Júlio Moreira Lima (1926-2011) was Brazil's Minister of Aeronautics on 19 May 1986, the night on which air traffic control at São José dos Campos, the CINDACTA radar network and five scrambled Brazilian Air Force fighters - two F-5Es from Santa Cruz Air Base and three Mirage IIIEs from Anápolis - engaged twenty-one unidentified radar plots over São Paulo, Rio de Janeiro, Minas Gerais and Goiás. Four days later, at 16:30 on 23 May 1986, he convened a press conference in Brasília and did something no defence minister of a major state had done before or has done since: he produced the intercept pilots and the air traffic controllers in person, confirmed the events, and said on the record that the armed forces had no explanation. His formulation was careful and complete: 'It is not a question of believing or not believing [in extraterrestrials or flying saucers]. We can only give technical information. There are several hypotheses. Technically, I would tell you gentlemen that we have no explanation.' He undertook to investigate and to publish a full dossier within thirty days. The dossier did not appear. The Air Defence Command's own report on the night, dated 2 June 1986 and signed by Brigadier José Pessoa Cavalcanti de Albuquerque, was classified CONFIDENCIAL and not released until 25 September 2009 - twenty-three years later.

### Recorded as unverified

- The promised 30-day dossier was never published so far as the record shows; whether one was written and withheld was not established.

Affiliations recorded: fab, Ministério da Aeronáutica.

### Why it holds its tier

Re-graded B on a global pass (rank 487 of 1057, score 30). 4 connections, 0 of them custody/witness/funding/programmatic, 1 official denial recorded against it. Axes C1/A2/D3.

Tecnicamente, diria aos senhores que não temos explicação.' Source: Brigadeiro Octávio Júlio Moreira Lima, press conference, Brasília, 23 May 1986, quoted by BBC Brasil, 14 January 2022

Source: André Bernardo, BBC Brasil, 14 January 2022

## Pete Hegseth

Pete Hegseth is a person on the board; board tier B, documented channel and capability, programme role indicated but not yet nailed to a primary source; dates 2026-05-08; axes C1 A3 D3; 4 connections.

In one line: Secretary of War; releasing authority for PURSUE.

### The longer account

Hegseth is the named releasing authority for every PURSUE tranche. On 20 February 2026, hours after Trump's Truth Social post directing him to begin identifying and releasing UAP files, Hegseth answered publicly with a screenshot and emoji rather than a policy statement; on 25 February he told reporters the Department would be 'in full compliance with that executive order, [and we're] eager to provide that for the president,' while hedging on schedule - 'I don't want to over-promise and under-deliver. So, we're going to take a little time to make sure we assess what this should look like.' He also committed the Department to work 'closely with the Office of the Director of National Intelligence' on implementation, which is why ODNI-numbered documents appear inside a war.gov release series. His published statement on the PURSUE portal is that the Department is 'in lockstep with President Trump to bring unprecedented transparency regarding our government's understanding of Unidentified Anomalous Phenomena.' Between 8 May and 7 August 2026 his department published five tranches - roughly 400-plus files including well over a hundred sensor videos - through a portal that is not AARO's. That routing is the substantive fact about Hegseth in this domain: the material went out over the head of the statutory UAP office, from the Secretary's own web estate, with an explicit departmental refusal to characterise any of it. Asked directly whether extraterrestrial life exists, he said: 'We'll see... I get to do the review and find out along with you.'

### Where the record conflicts

- PURSUE releases material AARO's annual reports had described as insufficiently supported or unavailable; the two positions have never been reconciled by the Department.

### Recorded as unverified

- Whether any formal executive order text exists beyond the President's 19 February 2026 public direction.

Affiliations recorded: Secretary of Defense / Secretary of War (2025-), Fox News (2014-2024), Army National Guard, Secretary of War / Secretary of Defense (2025-present).

## Robert Garcia

Robert Garcia is a person on the board; board tier C, contextual, real and relevant, no direct programme linkage on the record yet; dates 2023-07-26; axes C0 A1 D2; 4 connections.

In one line: U.S. Rep. (D-CA-42); Ranking Member, 2023 hearing; later Oversight Ranking Member.

### The longer account

Garcia was Ranking Member of the National Security, the Border and Foreign Affairs subcommittee when it held the 26 July 2023 hearing, and his handling of it is why the hearing read as an oversight proceeding rather than a spectacle. He opened by thanking Burchett for the leadership that produced the hearing, framed the matter as national security and institutional trust, and anchored it to the December 2017 New York Times reporting on DIA-funded UAP research. His questioning concentrated on the reporting gap: Graves's estimate of the number of pilots he was working with, the absence of any usable reporting channel for aviators, and the stigma that suppresses reports. He converted that into legislation - on 11 January 2024 he introduced H.R. 6967, the Safe Airspace for Americans Act, to let civilian pilots and personnel report UAP encounters to the FAA for onward transmission to AARO with legal safeguards, co-led with Republican subcommittee chairman Glenn Grothman. He attended the 12 January 2024 closed ICIG briefing and said afterward that everyone in the room 'probably received new information.' He is now Ranking Member of the full Committee on Oversight and Government Reform in the 119th Congress, which places the ranking-member half of the committee's UAP posture with him.

### Recorded as unverified

- How many of the roughly 40 witnesses actually filed their own protected disclosures, and whether the ICIG adjudicated any of them.

Affiliations recorded: U.S. House of Representatives, CA-42 (2023- ), Subcommittee on National Security, the Border, and Foreign Affairs, Ranking Member (2023-2024), Committee on Oversight and Government Reform, Ranking Member (2025- ), Mayor of Long Beach, California (2014-2022), US House of Representatives, CA-42 (2023- ), House Oversight Committee, Ranking Member (2025- ).

- **Tier 1 documented.** Ranking Member of the subcommittee that held the 26 July 2023 hearing; opened it by crediting Burchett's leadership and framing UAP as a national-security and institutional-trust question. Source: CHRG-118hhrg53022, 26 July 2023

## Sidney Gottlieb

Sidney Gottlieb is a person on the board; board tier B, documented channel and capability, programme role indicated but not yet nailed to a primary source; dates 1955-03-31; axes C0 A3 D0; 4 connections.

In one line: Chief, Technical Services Staff Chemical Division 1955; Deputy Chief, Technical Services Division 1963; Chief of TSD, and the officer whose verbal order destroyed the MKULTRA records, January 1973.

### The longer account

Sidney Gottlieb is the officer at the centre of the destruction event the board's own MKULTRA hearing node argues from, and three separate opened documents give him three offices in sequence. In March and June 1955 he certifies the invoices of a numbered MKULTRA subproject as Chief of the Chemical Division of the Technical Services Staff, which is the closest thing in the released record to a signature on the money. In 1963 the Agency's Inspector General calls him the present Deputy Chief of the Technical Services Division and credits him with a six-month study of scientific and technical problems in covert action operations. In January 1973 Technical Services Division personnel destroy the MKULTRA files on his verbal order, which he and Richard Helms both later testified was Helms's order carried out. The 1977 Senate hearing that reopened the subject could not produce him: the questioning turns again and again on the fact that the one man who would know was not there.

### Why it holds its tier

Re-graded B on a global pass (rank 273 of 1057, score 39). 4 connections, 3 of them custody/witness/funding/programmatic. Axes C0/A3/D0.

### The claims on the record

- **Tier 1 documented.** A CIA memorandum of 22 June 1977 reprinted in the hearing print explains why the 1973 searches missed the finance folders: 'Because Dr. Gottlieb retrieved and destroyed all the MKULTRA documents he was able to locate, it is not surprising that the earlier search for MKULTRA documents, directed at areas where they were most likely to be found, was unsuccessful.' Source: Memorandum for the Deputy Director of Central Intelligence, 'Request for Guidance on Handling Recently Located MKULTRA Material', 22 June 1977, from David S. Brandwein, Director, Office of Technical Service, Appendix B of the Project MKULTRA joint hearing, https://www.intelligence.senate.gov/sites/default/files/hearings/95mkultra.pdf
- **Tier 1 documented.** Gottlieb signed the money. The released MKULTRA Subproject 43 file carries invoice certifications over the signature 'SIDNEY GOTTLIEB, Chief, TSS/CD' attesting 'that performance is satisfactory, that the services are being accomplished in accordance with mutual agreements, that a detailed agenda of the payments and receipts is on file in TSS/CD'. Invoice No. 1 is dated 31 March 1955 and Invoice No. 2 is dated 28 June 1955. Source: CIA Reading Room document 00017441, MKULTRA SUBPROJECT 43 (18 documents, pubdates 550321 to 560308), Internet Archive mirror, https://archive.org/details/cia-readingroom-document-00017441
- **Tier 1 documented.** He gave the destruction order. 'In January 1973, MKULTRA records were destroyed by Technical Services Division personnel acting on the verbal orders of Dr. Sidney Gottlieb, Chief of TSD. Dr. Gottlieb has testified, and former Director Helms has confirmed, that in ordering the records destroyed, Dr. Gottlieb was carrying out the verbal order of then DCI Helms.' Source: Project MKULTRA joint hearing, 3 August 1977, Appendix B, https://www.intelligence.senate.gov/sites/default/files/hearings/95mkultra.pdf
- **Tier 1 documented.** He is one of the two officers named as running the experiment that killed Frank Olson: 'The drug had been placed in the bottle by a CIA officer, Dr. Robert Lashbrook, as part of an experiment he and Dr. Sidney Gottlieb performed at a meeting of Army and CIA scientists.' The same text records that neither the Chief of TSS nor the Deputy Director for Plans specifically authorized that experiment. Source: Project MKULTRA joint hearing, 3 August 1977, Appendix B, https://www.intelligence.senate.gov/sites/default/files/hearings/95mkultra.pdf
- **Tier 1 documented.** The Agency's own Inspector General placed him one rung below the top of TSD in 1963: 'The present Deputy Chief of TSD, Dr. Sidney Gottlieb, in his then capacity of scientific advisor to the Deputy Director/Plans (DD/P), released a study ... on 21 April 1960, covering his six-month investigation of "Scientific and Technical Problems in Covert Action Operations".' Source: Report of Inspection of MKULTRA, CIA Inspector General, 26 July 1963, https://nsarchive.gwu.edu/sites/default/files/documents/1963-07-26%20JM%20Box%208%20F2%20MKUltra-IG_Report-ocr.pdf
- **Tier 2 named person on the record.** On the Danny Jones Podcast, episode 100, 1 July 2021, at 1:08:19 to 1:10:07, Tom O'Neill described Gottlieb as Louis Jolyon West's handler, said their correspondence began at Lackland Air Force Base and said Gottlieb offered West an operational alias which West declined as more conspicuous. No record opened for this node bears on any of it, and the released Subproject 43 file names no researcher. Source: Danny Jones Podcast, ep. 100, 1 July 2021, 1:08:19 to 1:10:07

## Rear Adm. Sidney W. Souers

Rear Adm. Sidney W. Souers is a person on the board; board tier B, documented channel and capability, programme role indicated but not yet nailed to a primary source; dates 1946-01-22; axes C2 A3 D1; 4 connections.

In one line: First Director of Central Intelligence, Central Intelligence Group, 1946; first Executive Secretary, National Security Council, 1947-1950.

### The longer account

Souers held, in sequence, the two offices through which the postwar national security paper actually moved. He was the first Director of Central Intelligence, designated under the Presidential directive of 22 January 1946 that created the National Intelligence Authority and the Central Intelligence Group, and he served until 10 June 1946. From August 1947 he was the first Executive Secretary of the National Security Council, the civilian staff head the National Security Act of 1947 created by statute, and he held that post until January 1950. At the Council's first meeting on 26 September 1947 the Council routed attendance requests and meeting scheduling through him, gave him full powers over its funds, resolved that its affairs would be conducted with the utmost secrecy, and admitted the Director of Central Intelligence as an observer and adviser. The board argues at length about what the National Security Council was and was not shown and has no node for the man who built its secretariat. He is named in the MJ-12 Eisenhower Briefing Document, and that is not why he is here.

### Recorded as unverified

## Capitão Uyrangê Bolívar Soares Nogueira de Hollanda Lima

Capitão Uyrangê Bolívar Soares Nogueira de Hollanda Lima is a person on the board; board tier B, documented channel and capability, programme role indicated but not yet nailed to a primary source; dates 1997; axes C2 A2 D2; 4 connections.

In one line: Brazilian Air Force captain; commander of Operação Prato, 1977-1978.

### The longer account

Captain Uyrangê Bolívar Soares Nogueira de Hollanda Lima commanded Operação Prato, the Brazilian Air Force operation mounted in late 1977 on the island of Colares in Pará after residents reported being injured by descending lights they called the Chupa-Chupa and the mayor, José Ildone Favacho Soeiro, formally asked the Air Force for help. The operation ran in two phases, 20 October to 11 November 1977 and 25 November to 5 December 1977, and was executed by the Brazilian Air Force with the National Information Service, the military dictatorship's intelligence agency. Photographs of lights were taken; the operation was closed after roughly four months and its official finding was that no unusual phenomena had been found. Twenty years later, in 1997, Hollanda gave an interview to the Brazilian ufologists Ademar José Gevaerd and Marco Antônio Petit in which he recounted what he and his men had lived through - an account fundamentally at odds with the official negative finding. Three months after that interview he was found dead at his home, apparently having hanged himself with the belt of his bathrobe. He is the closest thing the Brazilian record has to a custody-adjacent officer who spoke and then died, and the sequence - operation, official denial, twenty years of silence, interview, death - is the reason the case has never settled.

### Where the record conflicts

- {'text': "The Air Force's official finding was that nothing unusual was found; Hollanda's 1997 interview and the accounts of Colares residents describe injuries, burns and puncture wounds. Both cannot be true; the released Arquivo Nacional file is the arbiter and has not been fully worked in this pass."}

### Recorded as unverified

- Hollanda's exact date of death and the date of the Gevaerd/Petit interview were not established from a primary source.
- Whether any official inquest was held into his death was not established.

Affiliations recorded: fab, Serviço Nacional de Informações (SNI).

Source: Wikipedia 'Operação Prato', citing ISTOÉ (22 July 2013) and Folha de S. Paulo (11 January 2009)
