## Walter Haut

Walter Haut is a person on the board; board tier B, documented channel and capability, programme role indicated but not yet nailed to a primary source; dates 1947-07-08; axes C2 A1 D3; 4 connections.

In one line: Public information officer, 509th Bomb Group, RAAF.

### The longer account

Walter G. Haut (3 June 1922 - 15 December 2005) wrote the sentence that started all of it. A bombardier who flew thirty-five missions against Japan and dropped instrument packages at the Bikini tests in 1946, he became public information officer for the 509th Bomb Group at Roswell Army Air Field, and on 8 July 1947 base commander Col. William H. Blanchard - a close personal friend - told him to write and distribute a release stating the field had come into possession of a flying disc. He did, and the story went round the world before Fort Worth retracted it the same day. Haut's own testimony evolved in a documented, dateable sequence that this board records in full because the sequence is the evidence. In 1980's 'The Roswell Incident' he is explicitly 'not a witness.' In 1979 and again in a March 1989 interview he said he knew nothing of what was recovered and had been refused when he asked to see it. In a sworn affidavit signed 14 May 1993 he added that Blanchard must have seen the material himself and that neither Blanchard nor Marcel could have mistaken it for a weather balloon. Then, in December 2002, Donald Schmitt composed and emailed a further affidavit which Haut signed at the International UFO Museum in front of his daughter Julie Shuster and a notary, on the agreement that it would not be released until after his death; it asserts first-hand sight of a craft and bodies. Haut died in 2005 and the document was published by Carey and Schmitt in 'Witness to Roswell' (2007). A witness whose claims escalate monotonically over twenty-two years, with the largest escalation drafted by his interviewer and embargoed until he could not be cross-examined, is a specific kind of evidence, and this board grades it as such.

### Where the record conflicts

- Haut's 2002 affidavit contains details absent from, and in tension with, his own interviews from the 1980s and 1990s
- Haut's 1979/1989 statements, his 1993 affidavit and the 2002 document are mutually inconsistent as to whether he saw anything. The 2007 book was criticised for presenting the 2002 document as an affidavit and for the inconsistencies.

### Recorded as unverified

- The claim that Col. Blanchard personally dictated the press release rests on Haut's posthumous affidavit alone
- Whether the 2002 document was executed with the formalities of an affidavit under New Mexico law; the notarisation is attested but the jurat has not been examined here.

Affiliations recorded: 509th Bomb Group, Roswell Army Air Field (1946-1948), International UFO Museum and Research Center, co-founder (1991-2005), 509th Bomb Group, Roswell Army Air Field, public information officer, International UFO Museum and Research Center, co-founder (1991) and president to 1996.

### Why it holds its tier

Re-graded B on a global pass (rank 209 of 1057, score 43). 4 connections, 2 of them custody/witness/funding/programmatic. Axes C2/A1/D3.

### The claims on the record

- **Tier 1 documented.** Haut authored and released the 8 July 1947 Roswell Army Air Field press release announcing that the 509th Bomb Group had come into possession of a flying disc. Source: RAAF press release, 8 July 1947; Roswell Daily Record, 8 July 1947
- **Tier 1 documented.** On 8 July 1947 RAAF publicly announced that the intelligence office of the 509th Bomb Group 'was fortunate enough to gain possession of a disc through the cooperation of one of the local ranchers and the sheriff's office of Chaves County.' Source: RAAF press release, 8 July 1947, reproduced in USAF, 'The Roswell Report: Fact vs. Fiction in the New Mexico Desert' (1995)
- **Tier 1 documented.** On 8 July 1947 an Eighth Air Force headquarters official telephonically informed the FBI's Dallas office of the recovery near Roswell of 'a hexagonal-shaped disc suspended from a large balloon by cable.' Source: GAO/NSIAD-95-187, p.4, describing the FBI teletype of 8 July 1947
- **Tier 2 named person on the record.** Haut stated in a sealed affidavit executed 26 December 2002 and released after his 2005 death that he saw a craft and bodies and that Col. Blanchard ordered the press release. Source: Walter Haut affidavit, executed 2002, released 2007
- **Tier 2 named person on the record.** In 1979 and in a March 1989 interview Haut said he knew nothing about what was recovered and was refused permission to see it - directly inconsistent with the 2002 document's first-hand claims. Source: Interviews quoted in Berlitz and Moore (1980) and Randle and Schmitt, 'UFO Crash at Roswell' (1991)
- **Tier 2 named person on the record.** In an affidavit signed 14 May 1993 Haut stated that Col. Blanchard had seen the material, that there was no chance Blanchard would have mistaken it for a weather balloon, and no chance Marcel would have been mistaken. Source: Haut affidavit, 14 May 1993 (Fund for UFO Research affidavit series)
- **Tier 3 stated inference.** The 2002 'sealed affidavit' should be treated as Schmitt's composition attested by Haut rather than as Haut's testimony, because Schmitt composed and emailed the text and the release was embargoed until Haut could not be questioned about it. Reasoning: stated on the record by Schmitt himself as to authorship and by the family as to the embargo. Source: Schmitt's and the Haut family's own account of the document's creation

## William B. Adkins

William B. Adkins is a person on the board; board tier C, contextual, real and relevant, no direct programme linkage on the record yet; dates 2026-08-12; axes C2 A3 D0; 4 connections.

In one line: Acting Director, National Reconnaissance Office.

### The longer account

William B. Adkins is listed by the National Reconnaissance Office as its Acting Director as of August 2026. The NRO he leads describes a workforce that is 31 percent NRO cadre, 34 percent government personnel detailed from other agencies and 35 percent military - a hybrid structure that is itself part of the concealment story, because it means most of the people who work there are on someone else's books. The NRO is the agency whose very existence was an official secret for three decades, the agency named among the contributing elements to ODNI's June 2021 Preliminary Assessment on UAP, and the agency that seconded David Grusch to the Unidentified Aerial Phenomena Task Force - Grusch's official House Oversight witness title on 26 July 2023 was 'Former National Reconnaissance Officer Representative, Unidentified Anomalous Phenomena Task Force, Department of Defense'. Despite that, the NRO has transferred no records to the statutory UAP collection at the National Archives, published no UAP FOIA collection, and put up no witness at any hearing. Adkins has made no public statement on UAP.

## William Culbreth

William Culbreth is a person on the board; board tier B, documented channel and capability, programme role indicated but not yet nailed to a primary source; dates 2010-11-20; axes C1 A2 D3; 4 connections.

In one line: Professor of Mechanical/Nuclear Engineering (ret.), University of Nevada, Las Vegas; founder of the UNLV Nuclear Engineering Lab; author of two AAWSAP DIRDs.

### The longer account

William Culbreth is a now-retired professor of mechanical engineering at the University of Nevada, Las Vegas, and the founder of the UNLV Nuclear Engineering Lab, a facility since led by Alexander Barzilov and embedded in UNLV's national-security engineering research area. UNLV sits about sixty-five miles from the Nevada National Security Site, and Culbreth's academic career was built on that adjacency: reactor physics, radiation detection and transport, and the nuclear engineering pipeline that feeds the Department of Energy's Nevada operations.

The geography matters for how he ended up on a DIA contract. AAWSAP was executed by Bigelow Aerospace Advanced Space Studies out of Las Vegas, and BAASS drew on subcontracted subject-matter experts. Culbreth was a credentialed nuclear engineer at the city's own research university, and he is credited on the DIA list with two of the 38 deliverables, more than any author outside EarthTech: item 29, "Detection and High Resolution Tracking of Vehicles at Hypersonic Velocities", and item 30, "Aneutronic Fusion Propulsion II". The pairing is telling. One paper is about seeing something moving very fast, the other about what might make it move, which are the two halves of the question AAWSAP was chartered to ask.

The released aneutronic paper attributed to him reads like a nuclear engineering course text extended into speculation: chapters on fission rocket history at the Nevada Test Site and Project Rover, radiation shielding, muon-catalysed and cavitation fusion, antimatter annihilation, and an appendix containing a relativistic rocket worksheet. It cites standard nuclear engineering textbooks rather than primary plasma-physics literature. Culbreth has no documented role in AAWSAP's UAP casework or in the paranormal lines of effort later described by AARO.

### Why it holds its tier

Re-graded B on a global pass (rank 212 of 1057, score 43). 4 connections, 1 of them custody/witness/funding/programmatic. Axes C1/A2/D3.

### The claims on the record

- **Tier 1 documented.** Culbreth is a professor of mechanical engineering at the University of Nevada, Las Vegas. Source: https://www.unlv.edu/people/william-culbreth
- **Tier 1 documented.** George Knapp released the hypersonic tracking DIRD and published an interview with Culbreth in mid-2019, making it the fifth DIRD to reach the public before the March 2022 bulk FOIA release. Source: https://silvarecord.com/2019/08/01/5th-aawsap-d-i-r-d-released-by-george-knapp-covers-detection-of-hypersonic-vehicles/
- **Tier 1 documented.** The 9 January 2018 DIA list credits item 30, "Aneutronic Fusion Propulsion II", to "Dr W Culbreth, Univ of Nevada-Las Vegas", and item 29, "Detection and High Resolution Tracking of Vehicles at Hypersonic Velocities", to the same author. Source: https://irp.fas.org/dia/aatip-list.pdf

## Andy Biggs

Andy Biggs is a person on the board; board tier C, contextual, real and relevant, no direct programme linkage on the record yet; dates 2023-07-26; axes C0 A1 D1; 3 connections.

In one line: U.S. Rep. (R-AZ-5); questioned witnesses on training ranges and the Phoenix Lights.

### The longer account

Biggs questioned all three witnesses at the 2023 hearing and returned to the subject at the 2024 hearing. His most useful contribution is a small, exact demonstration of the SCIF trap the committee kept falling into: pursuing the 1997 Phoenix Lights in his own state, he asked Grusch and Fravor where he should go to find the files and whom to address, and pre-empted the answer himself - 'I know you are going to tell me we need to go to a SCIF so you can tell us in a SCIF.' Fravor replied that he could 'potentially give you a vector on that... in a closed environment.' Grusch said the Phoenix Lights were outside the scope of his duties and he knew only what was in the public vernacular. Biggs also pressed on the operational cost - whether UAP are affecting training ranges and what the administration considers doing about it.

Affiliations recorded: U.S. House of Representatives, AZ-5 (2017- ), Committee on Oversight and Accountability / Government Reform.

### Why it holds its tier

Re-graded C on a global pass (rank 984 of 1057, score 11). 3 connections, 0 of them custody/witness/funding/programmatic. Axes C0/A1/D1.

### The claims on the record

- **Tier 1 documented.** 'If we wanted to find out more about that where would we go to find the files and where - and who would we address? I know you are going to tell me we need to go to a SCIF so you can tell us in a SCIF.' Fravor: 'I could potentially give you a vector on that... in a closed environment.' Source: Rep. Andy Biggs, 26 July 2023, GPO CHRG-118hhrg53022

### Its strongest connections

- Andy Biggs runs out to **David Charles Grusch** (person, board tier A), a investigative link at Tier 1 documented. The link reads: pressed Grusch on Phoenix Lights and withheld information. Biggs put the 1997 Phoenix Lights case to Grusch directly - 'I am going to take you to specific instances around the Phoenix Valley because that is where I live' - and asked whether he knew anything beyond the official explanation of a military training range off Luke and the Barry Goldwater range. Grusch: 'Only what is in the public vernacular about it. That was outside the scope of my duties.' Sources: House Oversight and Accountability Committee hearing, 'Unidentified Anomalous Phenomena: Implications on National Security, Public Safety, and Government Transparency', 26 July 2023 (CHRG-118hhrg53022), https://www.govinfo.gov/content/pkg/CHRG-118hhrg53022/html/CHRG-118hhrg53022.htm, dated 2023-07-26.

Chairman Grothman recognised Biggs, who questioned Grusch, Graves and Fravor on misdirection by the government regarding UAP, the 1997 Phoenix Lights, and the 2019 UAP classification guidelines. He is recorded in the transcript as a member of the Committee on Oversight and Accountability. Asked by Biggs whether the 2019 classification guidelines interfere with the government's ability to be transparent, Grusch answered: 'Yes, I am familiar with the UAP Task Force 2019 Security Classification guide. I think it is fair. I did actually help author that with the Director.' Biggs: 'Uh-oh. You got a bias then.' Grusch then characterised the practice rather than the guide - 'I will call it a lazy attitude about declassifying videos'. ## Andrew Ogles

Andrew Ogles is a person on the board; board tier C, contextual, real and relevant, no direct programme linkage on the record yet; dates 2023-07-26; axes C0 A1 D2; 3 connections.

In one line: U.S. Rep. (R-TN-5); UAP Caucus; threatened the Holman Rule over denied SCIF access.

### The longer account

Ogles produced the single most concrete enforcement threat any member has made on this subject. Waived onto the subcommittee on 26 July 2023, he questioned the witnesses on whether UAP behaviour is consistent with reconnaissance collection, then closed the member rounds with a demand and a lever: 'I would encourage the Chairman to demand that we have any and all, but in particular Mr. Grusch, talk to us in a SCIF and if that access is denied I will personally volunteer to initiate the Holman Rule against any personnel or any program or any agency that denies access to Congress.' The Holman Rule permits a floor amendment to an appropriations bill reducing the salary of a specific federal employee or the funding of a specific program to as little as one dollar - it is the one instrument Congress holds that reaches an individual official who stonewalls. No such amendment has been offered. A member of the UAP Caucus but not of the Oversight Committee, Ogles attended the 12 January 2024 closed ICIG briefing and came out saying what 'most Americans fear is true,' describing 'a concerted effort to conceal as much information as possible - both in Congress and to the general public.' He was waived onto the September 2025 Task Force hearing as well.

Affiliations recorded: U.S. House of Representatives, TN-5 (2023- ), Congressional UAP Caucus (2023- ), House Financial Services Subcommittee on National Security.

### Why it holds its tier

Re-graded C on a global pass (rank 822 of 1057, score 18). 3 connections, 0 of them custody/witness/funding/programmatic. Axes C0/A1/D2.

Source: Rep. Andy Ogles, 26 July 2023, GPO CHRG-118hhrg53022

## Lt. Gen. Arthur G. Trudeau

Lt. Gen. Arthur G. Trudeau is a person on the board; board tier B, documented channel and capability, programme role indicated but not yet nailed to a primary source; dates 1958-01-18; axes C2 A1 D2; 3 connections.

In one line: Chief of Research and Development, Department of the Army, 1958 to 1962.

### The longer account

Arthur G. Trudeau held the Army post that sat directly above the office Philip Corso's service record places him in. Two CIA records fix it. Allen Dulles congratulated him on the appointment in January 1958, and in February 1962 Trudeau wrote to Director of Central Intelligence John McCone over his own signature as Lieutenant General, GS, Chief of Research and Development. Corso's DA Form 66 records him as Chief of the Foreign Technology Division, OCRD, from 18 April to 18 July 1962, which falls inside Trudeau's tenure. That is the whole of the documented relationship. No independent record of a Foreign Technology Division inside OCRD was opened, so this node is Trudeau and the office, not the division. The distribution-conduit account attached to Corso is not evidence about Trudeau, and nothing opened for this pass connects him to it.

### Recorded as unverified

- The existence and structure of a Foreign Technology Division inside the Office of the Chief of Research and Development. No independent record of it was opened; it rests on Corso's DA Form 66 alone.
- Whether OCRD's Foreign Technology Division and the US Army Foreign Science and Technology Center are the same office renamed. Not asserted here.
- The identity of the 'Gen. Arthur S. Trudeau' of the 1955 FBI material with this officer.

### Why it holds its tier

Re-graded B on a global pass (rank 457 of 1057, score 31). 3 connections, 0 of them custody/witness/funding/programmatic. Axes C2/A1/D2.

### The claims on the record

- **Tier 1 documented.** Corso's own DA Form 66 records 'Chief Foreign Technology Div, OCRD USA (8556) Wash DC' effective 18 April 1962, a period falling entirely inside Trudeau's documented tenure as Chief of Research and Development. Source: DA Form 66, Officer's Qualification Record, Philip J. Corso, already carried on this board at philip-corso
- **Tier 1 documented.** DCI Allen W. Dulles wrote to him on 18 January 1958 as 'Chief of Research and Development, Department of the Army': 'Congratulations on your appointment as Chief of the Army Research and Development program.' Source: CIA-RDP80M01009A000500610124-1, letter of 18 January 1958, CIA reading room, declassified 14 August 2013, https://archive.org/details/cia-readingroom-document-cia-rdp80m01009a000500610124-1
- **Tier 1 documented.** He signed his letter of 24 February 1962 to DCI John A. McCone as 'ARTHUR G. TRUDEAU / Lieutenant General, GS / Chief of Research and Development', inviting the Director to a symposium on 'The U. S. Army's Limited-War Mission and Social Science Research' hosted by the Special Operations Research Office of The American University on 26 to 28 March 1962. Source: CIA-RDP80B01676R003000130017-9, Trudeau to McCone, 24 February 1962, https://archive.org/details/cia-readingroom-document-cia-rdp80b01676r003000130017-9
- **Tier 1 documented.** McCone's reply of 12 March 1962, reference ER 62-1185/a, is addressed 'Lieut. General Arthur G. Trudeau, Chief of Research and Development, Department of the Army' and declines, designating a representative whose name is withheld from the release. Source: CIA-RDP80B01676R003000130017-9, McCone to Trudeau, 12 March 1962, https://archive.org/details/cia-readingroom-document-cia-rdp80b01676r003000130017-9
- **Tier 3 stated inference.** The 'Gen. Arthur S. Trudeau' named in the 1955 FBI material quoted in philip-corso.narrative is most probably this officer. Reasoning: the two primary letters opened here give the middle initial G over his own signature, and no second Army general officer of that surname was found. The discrepancy is unresolved and no record opened here reconciles it. Source: Inference from CIA-RDP80B01676R003000130017-9 read against the FBI quotation already on the board, https://archive.org/details/cia-readingroom-document-cia-rdp80b01676r003000130017-9

## Cecilia Levy

Cecilia Levy is a person on the board; board tier C, contextual, real and relevant, no direct programme linkage on the record yet; dates 2025-06-01; axes C0 A0 D1; 3 connections.

In one line: Associate Professor of Physics, University at Albany (SUNY); LZ dark-matter physicist; third UAlbany author on the 2025 UAPx field-expedition paper.

### The longer account

Cecilia Levy is the third University at Albany physicist on the only peer-reviewed UAP field-expedition paper published to date, and she is on this board for a narrow and checkable reason: the University named her, in its own press release, as one of three 'UAlbany authors' on Szydagis, Knuth, Kugielsky & Levy (2025). Her career is otherwise entirely conventional astroparticle physics. She took a BSc at the Universite de Montreal, an MSc at Queen's University on the PICASSO dark matter experiment, a PhD at the University of Muenster on XENON100, a postdoc at RPI on XENON1T, and a postdoc at Albany on LUX/LZ, and she is now an Associate Professor in the same department as Szydagis and Knuth. Her stated research is the direct detection of dark matter: she works on the seven-tonne LZ detector at the Sanford Underground Research Facility, where her group handled detector cleanliness and the 206Pb wall background, and she co-develops the Snowball Chamber, a supercooled-water low-mass dark matter detector she describes as 'a research topic completely unique to the UAlbany dark matter group'. She co-authored the founding Snowball Chamber paper with Szydagis in 2018. That shared instrument work is the mechanism by which rare-event detection practice reached the UAP field campaign: the University's release explains that Szydagis's C-TAP frame-differencing software applies an approach 'used by physicists like him and Levy to look for direct evidence of dark matter'. One honest qualification belongs on the record. Unlike Szydagis, whose departmental page lists Unidentified Anomalous Phenomena among his research interests, Levy's faculty page makes no mention of UAP at all. Her involvement is visible in the publication and in the press office, not in her declared research profile - which is a reasonable description of how most working scientists have touched this subject.

### Why it holds its tier

Re-graded C on a global pass (rank 880 of 1057, score 16). 3 connections, 0 of them custody/witness/funding/programmatic. Axes C0/A0/D1.

### The claims on the record

- **Tier 1 documented.** Levy is an Associate Professor in the Department of Physics at the University at Albany, with a PhD from the University of Muenster on XENON100 and postdoctoral work at RPI (XENON1T) and Albany (LUX/LZ). Source: University at Albany, Department of Physics faculty page
- **Tier 1 documented.** She co-authored the Snowball Chamber paper with Szydagis: 'Szydagis, Knight, Levy, The Snowball chamber: neutron induced nucleation in supercooled water' (arXiv:1807.09253, 2018). Source: University at Albany faculty page publication list
- **Tier 1 documented.** UAlbany's news office names her as one of three 'UAlbany authors' on the 2025 Progress in Aerospace Sciences UAPx field-expedition paper: 'UAlbany authors Matthew Szydagis, Kevin Knuth and Cecilia Levy, along with Ben Kugielsky of UAPx'. Source: UAlbany News Center, 4 June 2025
- **Tier 2 named person on the record.** Her UAlbany faculty page lists no UAP research interest, in contrast to Szydagis's page, which does. Her UAP involvement is documented through co-authorship and the university press office only. Source: Comparison of the two UAlbany Department of Physics faculty pages, retrieved 21 August 2026

## David N. Spergel

David N. Spergel is a person on the board; board tier B, documented channel and capability, programme role indicated but not yet nailed to a primary source; dates 2023-09-14; axes C0 A1 D2; 3 connections.

In one line: Astrophysicist; President, Simons Foundation; Chair, NASA UAP Independent Study Team.

### The longer account

Spergel chaired the only formal scientific panel the US government has convened on this subject in the modern era, and the way that panel was constrained is itself evidence. NASA commissioned the Unidentified Anomalous Phenomena Independent Study Team on 24 May 2022; it began work on 24 October 2022, held a single public meeting on 31 May 2023, and delivered its report on 14 September 2023, the same day it dissolved. Its sixteen members were a genuinely serious cross-section - Federica Bianco, Joshua Semeter, Shelley Wright, Matt Mountain, Walter Scott of Maxar, Reggie Brothers, Mike Gold, Scott Kelly, plus FAA leadership in Warren Randolph and Karlin Toner. The report's conclusions were unremarkable by design: no evidence of extraterrestrial origin, data quality is the binding constraint, NASA should apply rigorous evidence-based methods including AI and crowdsourced observation, and NASA is well placed to lead. What the panel did not have was access to the classified holdings that the entire controversy is about. It was a study of the poverty of the unclassified record, conducted by people barred from the classified one, and NASA then declined at first to name the new director of UAP research it announced the same day. That is the shape of the concealment problem rendered in an organisation chart.

### Recorded as unverified

- Whether the study team was given access to any classified DoD or IC UAP holdings; the report's own framing suggests it was not.
- Whether any panel member was read on to any classified UAP holding.
- The identity of the NASA Director of UAP Research announced on 14 September 2023 and that post's current status.

Affiliations recorded: Princeton University, Department of Astrophysical Sciences, Simons Foundation, President (2021- ), NASA UAP Independent Study Team, Chair (2022-2023), Princeton University, Department of Astrophysical Sciences (chair, 2006-2016), Flatiron Institute / Simons Foundation (President, 2021- ), NASA UAP Independent Study Team - chair (2022-2023).

## Donald H. Menzel

Donald H. Menzel is a person on the board; board tier B, documented channel and capability, programme role indicated but not yet nailed to a primary source; dates 1976; axes C2 A1 D2; 3 connections.

In one line: Director, Harvard College Observatory; author of three books arguing against the extraterrestrial hypothesis, 1953, 1963 and 1977.

### The longer account

Donald Howard Menzel, 1901-1976, was the director of the Harvard College Observatory and the period's most prominent scientific opponent of the extraterrestrial hypothesis. The board's interest is not his astronomy but the fact that the Air Force's own commissioned study endorsed him: in the Condon Report's history chapter, written by Condon himself, Menzel's 1953 Flying Saucers is called 'an excellent book' and is noted as 'generally regarded as "debunking" and "negative."' That is a study accused of reaching a predetermined conclusion praising, in its own text, the man whose conclusion it reached. The claim the Area 52 corpus attaches to him, that Stanton Friedman found in his Harvard papers in April 1986 a thirty-year NSA association and a CIA Top Secret Ultra clearance, could not be opened from here and is carried as a gap, not as a fact.

- That Stanton Friedman found in Menzel's Harvard papers in April 1986 a thirty-year NSA association and a CIA Top Secret Ultra clearance. The Harvard University Archives were not reached and nsa.gov refuses this environment.
- That Menzel made government-funded visits to New Mexico in 1947 and 1948. No travel record was reached.

## Donald J. Trump

Donald J. Trump is a person on the board; board tier B, documented channel and capability, programme role indicated but not yet nailed to a primary source; dates 2026-02-19; axes C0 A3 D3; 3 connections.

In one line: President; source of the PURSUE directive.

### The longer account

The entire 2026 release architecture rests on a single social-media post. On 19-20 February 2026 Trump posted on Truth Social that he would direct Secretary of War Pete Hegseth and other relevant departments and agencies to 'begin the process of identifying and releasing Government files related to alien and extraterrestrial life, unidentified aerial phenomena (UAP), and unidentified flying objects (UFOs), and any and all other information connected to these highly complex, but extremely interesting and important, matters.' The programme that followed - PURSUE, the Presidential Unsealing and Reporting System for UAP Encounters - takes its name from that direction and is described on the Department of War's own portal as standing on it. Hegseth referred to the direction as an 'executive order' in remarks five days later, but the contemporaneous DefenseScoop reporting on the announcement records a Truth Social post and no signed order, and no executive order text has surfaced in the public record as of 12 August 2026. That gap matters: a programme releasing hundreds of files under presidential direction, with no published instrument establishing scope, standards, review authority or exemptions, is a programme whose limits are set entirely by the executive that runs it and can be withdrawn as informally as it was created. In July 2026 the same channel produced a second directive - the waiver of UAP-related nondisclosure agreements for former employees and contractors speaking to authorised government investigators - which officials were careful to describe as 'not a declassification directive.'

Affiliations recorded: President of the United States (2017-2021, 2025-).

## Edward U. Condon

Edward U. Condon is a person on the board; board tier B, documented channel and capability, programme role indicated but not yet nailed to a primary source; dates 1969-01-08; axes C0 A2 D0; 3 connections.

In one line: Scientific director, USAF-funded Colorado UFO Project.

### The longer account

Edward Condon was a distinguished quantum physicist, former director of the National Bureau of Standards, and a man who had himself been hounded by the House Un-American Activities Committee, which made him an unusually credentialled front for the Air Force's exit strategy. Under contract 44620-67-C-0035, awarded October 1966, he directed the University of Colorado UFO Project and personally wrote the summary, conclusions and recommendations that were placed at the front of the 1968 report and were the only part most readers ever saw. Those conclusions declared further study unwarranted. The body of the report did not support them: roughly a third of the cases studied were left unexplained by the project's own investigators. Condon spent much of the project period giving speeches disparaging the subject, told an audience in January 1967 that the study was 'a bunch of nonsense' and that he might recommend the government get out of it, and fired the two staff members who exposed the Low memo. His report was blessed by a National Academy of Sciences panel and used by the Air Force as the stated basis for terminating Blue Book on 17 December 1969. Condon delivered exactly what the Robertson Panel had prescribed fifteen years earlier, with academic letterhead attached.

### What is left unexplained

Roughly a third of the cases investigated by the project's own staff were left unexplained, a residue the summary conclusions do not address.

### Where the record conflicts

- The report's front-matter conclusions are inconsistent with the unexplained residue in its own case chapters

Affiliations recorded: National Bureau of Standards, director (1945-1951), University of Colorado (1963-1970), Colorado UFO Project, scientific director (1966-1968).

## Friedwardt Winterberg

Friedwardt Winterberg is a person on the board; board tier B, documented channel and capability, programme role indicated but not yet nailed to a primary source; dates 2010-01-01; axes C1 A1 D2; 3 connections.

In one line: Theoretical physicist, Research Professor, University of Nevada, Reno; author of two AAWSAP DIRDs.

### The longer account

Friedwardt Winterberg is a German-American theoretical physicist and research professor at the University of Nevada, Reno, with a long publication record spanning general relativity, Planck-scale physics, nuclear fusion and plasma physics. He appears twice on the DIA list of AATIP/AAWSAP products transmitted to the Senate Armed Services Committee: as author of item 2, 'Advanced Nuclear Propulsion for Manned Deep Space Missions', and as author of 'Negative Mass Propulsion', both with the affiliation University of Nevada, Reno.

His geographic placement matters to the shape of the programme. AAWSAP funding was directed by Senator Harry Reid toward a Nevada constituent, and the DIRD author list carries an unusually heavy Nevada concentration, Winterberg at UNR, and separate deliverables credited to the University of Nevada, Las Vegas. Winterberg is the only author on the list credited with two Nevada-institution papers, and both of his are propulsion physics rather than the anomalous-phenomena material that dominates the EarthTech contributions.

'Negative Mass Propulsion' carries DIA document number DIA-08-1101-023, published 3 January 2011 with an Intelligence Cut-Off Date of 30 August 2010, in the 'Defense Futures' series, and its cover page identifies it as one of a series of advanced technology reports produced in FY 2010 under the AAWSA Program. The subject is the classic Bondi problem of runaway motion between positive and negative gravitational mass, considered as a propulsion concept: a vehicle accelerating without expelling reaction mass. Winterberg published closely related work in the peer-reviewed literature, which distinguishes his DIRD from several others on the list that have no independent scholarly footprint. The document was released by DIA under FOIA and posted to the DIA FOIA Electronic Reading Room; the author name is redacted on the released cover page under (b)(6), so the attribution rests on the congressional list rather than on the document itself.

### Why it holds its tier

Re-graded B on a global pass (rank 401 of 1057, score 33). 3 connections, 1 of them custody/witness/funding/programmatic. Axes C1/A1/D2.

### The claims on the record

- **Tier 1 documented.** Named on the DIA list of AATIP/AAWSAP products supplied to the Senate Armed Services Committee as author of 'Negative Mass Propulsion', affiliation University of Nevada, Reno. Source: https://irp.fas.org/dia/aatip-list.pdf
- **Tier 1 documented.** Named on the same DIA list as author of item 2, 'Advanced Nuclear Propulsion for Manned Deep Space Missions', affiliation University of Nevada, Reno, making him a two-deliverable AAWSAP contributor. Source: https://www.theblackvault.com/documentarchive/the-advanced-aviation-threat-identification-program-aatip-dird-report-research/

## Garry P. Reid

Garry P. Reid is a person on the board; board tier C, contextual, real and relevant, no direct programme linkage on the record yet; dates 2017-12-23; axes C1 A3 D0; 3 connections.

In one line: Director for Defense Intelligence (Intelligence & Security), OUSD(I).

### The longer account

Reid is the author of the Department's internal counter-narrative. As Director for Defense Intelligence within OUSD(I), the office running counterintelligence, security and law enforcement policy for the whole Department, he wrote the memoranda that first framed Elizondo as a man who 'aggrandized his role,' and those memoranda went to the Secretary of Defense. FOIA case 18-F-0324 released two of them: an undated memorandum for record on media reporting related to AATIP and Elizondo's actions, and a response to six questions put by the Secretary's office in late December 2017 concerning why Elizondo resigned, the 'uncertain provenance' of the second resignation letter, and the handling of the videos. Reid asserted that 'to the best of his knowledge' Elizondo 'had no job responsibilities related to the AATIP', a formulation that became the Department's public line eighteen months later and has never been retracted. Reid is also the official Elizondo named in his May 2021 DoD Inspector General complaint. Reid was removed from his position by April 2022 after DoD IG investigations substantiated Joint Ethics Regulation violations, mishandling of controlled unclassified information via personal email on 65 occasions, and unwelcome physical contact with female subordinates. Defense officials told The Debrief that Reid 'played a central role in obfuscating information' about UAP.

### What is left unexplained

Reid's memo says AATIP ended in 2012 and Elizondo had no role in it, yet it also records that Elizondo sought assistance in August 2017 to get the AATIP-associated videos cleared. The memo does not explain why an officer with no program responsibilities was the one processing the program's imagery five years after the program allegedly ended.

### Recorded as unverified

- Whether Reid personally directed the June 2019 public statements; the released May 2019 messaging emails show Neill Tipton originating the 'no assigned responsibilities' formulation, with Reid's role described by unnamed defense officials rather than documents.

Affiliations recorded: U.S. Special Operations Command / OSD SO-LIC, OUSD(I&S), Director for Defense Intelligence (Intelligence & Security) (c.2015-2022).

### Why it holds its tier

Re-graded C on a global pass (rank 575 of 1057, score 27). 3 connections, 0 of them custody/witness/funding/programmatic. Axes C1/A3/D0.

### The claims on the record

- **Tier 1 documented.** DoD IG investigations found he violated the Joint Ethics Regulation through a relationship creating a 'widespread perception of an inappropriate relationship and favoritism', used personal email for official business 65 times, and engaged in unwelcome physical contact with female subordinates; he was removed by April 2022. Source: The Debrief, reporting on DoD IG findings
- **Tier 1 documented.** His memorandum answered six questions from the Secretary of Defense's office covering the abruptness of the resignation, the 'uncertain provenance' of the second resignation letter, Elizondo's claim of secretly working for the Secretary, video handling, and the TTSA press conference. Source: OSD/JS FOIA 18-F-0324
- **Tier 1 documented.** Wrote memoranda in December 2017 asserting Elizondo served as an action officer from 2008 to 2017 with no documented AATIP responsibilities, that AATIP terminated in 2012, and that Elizondo 'aggrandized his role'. Source: OSD/JS FOIA 18-F-0324, released 17 August 2022
- **Tier 2 named person on the record.** Defense officials told The Debrief that Reid 'played a central role in obfuscating information' about UAP and deliberately misled the public about Elizondo's program involvement. Source: The Debrief, 'Sex, Lies, and UFOs: Pentagon's Head of Counterintelligence and Security Ousted'

### Its strongest connections

- Garry P. Reid runs out to **Garry Reid memoranda on AATIP and Elizondo, December 2017** (document, board tier B), a institutional link at Tier 1 documented. The link reads: Author of both December 2017 memoranda. Reid wrote the memorandum for record and the memorandum for the Secretary asserting Elizondo 'aggrandized his role'. Sources: OSD/JS FOIA 18-F-0324, https://documents2.theblackvault.com/documents/osd/18-F-0324.pdf, dated 2022-08-17.
- Garry P. Reid comes in from **James N. Mattis** (person, board tier C), a institutional link at Tier 1 documented. Rather than engaging Elizondo, the Secretary's office tasked the Director for Defense Intelligence to explain him. Reid's answer is the origin of the Department's counter-narrative. ## Gary Voorhis

Gary Voorhis is a person on the board; board tier C, contextual, real and relevant, no direct programme linkage on the record yet; dates 2004-11-15; axes C1 A1 D1; 3 connections.

In one line: Petty Officer, CEC/Aegis computer and radar technician, USS Princeton (CG-59), Nov 2004.

### The longer account

Voorhis was a Cooperative Engagement Capability and Aegis systems technician aboard USS Princeton in November 2004, which put him at the exact node where the incident's machine record lived - the tapes and drives that captured what the SPY-1B and the CEC network saw. His public account, given repeatedly in documentary and broadcast interviews from 2018 onward, is that in the days after the intercept he was directed to hand over or wipe recording media, including data-tape cartridges, and that personnel he did not recognise were aboard doing the collecting. If accurate, it is the cleanest single instance of the program's evidence-handling behaviour in the entire modern aviator record: not a denial, not a classification decision anyone signed in public, but a physical removal of the primary sensor product within days. It has not been corroborated by any released document. The Navy has never produced Princeton radar data for 14 November 2004, and the FOIA-released range fouler report for the incident says only that reporting 'terminated at 3rd Fleet.' Voorhis is carried at tier C precisely because the claim is important enough that it must not be over-graded.

### Where the record conflicts

- Directly contradicts Fravor's sworn 'tapes were never taken' if read as a fleet-wide claim; compatible if read as Princeton-specific.

### Recorded as unverified

- Any documentary trace of the collection: chain-of-custody form, message, work order, or log entry.
- Whether Princeton's CEC recorded data for the period was retained, destroyed on a routine cycle, or removed.

Affiliations recorded: US Navy, USS Princeton (CG-59), Carrier Strike Group 11 (2004).

### Why it holds its tier

Re-graded C on a global pass (rank 604 of 1057, score 26). 4 connections, 3 of them custody/witness/funding/programmatic. Axes C1/A1/D1.

### The claims on the record

- **Tier 1 documented.** The 14 Nov 2004 incident is formally documented in Navy range fouler reporting released via FOIA in January 2023, which records that reporting terminated at 3rd Fleet with no process to share it. Source: Navy range fouler reports, Navy FOIA reading room, 13 January 2023, reported by The War Zone
- **Tier 3 stated inference.** Data recording media covering the Princeton's tracking of the objects were collected or ordered wiped by unidentified personnel in the days after 14 Nov 2004. Reasoning: the account is independently given by two Princeton crewmen in different billets, and the Navy has never produced Princeton sensor data for the date despite the incident being formally documented in its own range fouler reporting - an absence consistent with, though not proof of, removal. Source: Gary Voorhis, public documentary and broadcast interviews from 2018; no primary record located as of Aug 2026

### Its strongest connections

- Gary Voorhis runs out to **USS Princeton (CG-59)** (institution, board tier B), a institutional link at Tier 2 named person on the record. The link reads: CEC/Aegis systems technician aboard USS Princeton, Nov 2004. Voorhis's technical billet placed him at the node where the encounter's recorded machine data physically lived. Sources: The War Zone - Tic Tac incident in newly released range fouler reports, https://www.twz.com/tic-tac-uap-incident-included-in-72-newly-released-range-incursion-reports, dated 2023-01-14.

## George S. Trimble

George S. Trimble is a person on the board; board tier C, contextual, real and relevant, no direct programme linkage on the record yet; dates 1955-06-10; axes C1 A1 D0; 3 connections.

In one line: Glenn L. Martin Company executive; proposed RIAS as an industrial institute for advanced study.

### The longer account

RIAS was Trimble's idea and gravity was explicitly its subject, and both facts come off his own dated letter. Writing to Bryce DeWitt on 10 June 1955 he opens: 'During a recent conversation with Mr. George Rideout, president of Roger Babson's Gravity Research Foundation, we were commiserating on the unfortunate state of the affairs that knowledgable folks do not wish to get mixed up in the field of gravity research.' He continues: 'It occurred to us some time ago that our industry was vitally concerned with gravity ... At least one category of the things one must study, when he desires to bring space flight to reality, is the laws of nature surrounding the force of gravity.' He described the proposed body as an 'industrial version of the Institute for Advanced Study'.

That is an aerospace prime's executive, in 1955, in writing, saying his industry needed gravity research and that respectable people would not touch it -- which is a documented statement of exactly the reputational mechanism this board maps elsewhere. Martin money also reached the university side: Trimble and the Glenn L. Martin Company bought a Founder's Membership in the Institute of Field Physics for $5,000 and offered to help solicit more.

The industrial and academic halves were deliberately kept apart, and the source says why. The Martin company's institute was 'industry-sponsored, and not ensconced in a university', while the Institute of Field Physics was placed inside UNC 'so as to avoid the conflict that physicists felt working in an industrial setting.' Note what is not carried here: no document read states that Trimble recruited Louis Witten, and this board draws no such edge.

### Why it holds its tier

### The claims on the record

- **Tier 1 documented.** In November 1955 he was a vice-president of Martin, not its chief executive, and the atom-bomb comparison he made was about time and not about money. Ansel Talbert's Herald Tribune series identifies him as 'vice-president in charge of advanced design planning of Martin Aircraft Corp.' and as 'organizing a new Research Institute for Advanced Study', and quotes him: 'I think we could do the job in about the time that it actually required to build the first atom bomb if enough trained scientific brain-power simultaneously began thinking about and working towards a solution.' Source: Ansel E. Talbert, New York Herald Tribune, 20 November 1955 caption and continuation p. 36 and 22 November 1955 continuation p. 10, page images at https://archive.org/download/talbert1ny/talbert1ny_djvu.txt and https://archive.org/download/talbert3ny/talbert3ny_djvu.txt
- **Tier 1 documented.** The UNC Physics and Astronomy department history names the Air Force route into the Institute of Field Physics as the Aeronautical Research Laboratory at Wright-Patterson Air Force Base, not the Wright Air Development Center: 'Joshua Goldberg was, at the time of the establishment of the IOFP and the conference, in charge of aspects of the Air Force's funding of general relativity, in the "modern physics research branch" (based at the Aeronautical Research Laboratory at Wright Patterson Air Force Base).' The page uses neither 'Wright Air Development Center' nor 'WADC'. Source: History of the Institute of Field Physics (1955-1959), UNC Physics and Astronomy department history https://physics.unc.edu/home/department-history/section-ii/
- **Tier 2 named person on the record.** In a letter to Bryce DeWitt dated 10 June 1955, Trimble wrote: 'It occurred to us some time ago that our industry was vitally concerned with gravity. As time goes on we become more and more concerned because we feel certain that sooner or later man will invade space and we see it as our job to do everything possible to speed this event. At least one category of the things one must study, when he desires to bring space flight to reality, is the laws of nature surrounding the force of gravity.' Source: Trimble to Bryce DeWitt, 10 June 1955, quoted by Dean Rickles in the Edition Open Sources edition of the 1957 Chapel Hill conference report
- **Tier 2 named person on the record.** The same letter opens by recording a conversation with George Rideout, president of Roger Babson's Gravity Research Foundation, 'commiserating on the unfortunate state of the affairs that knowledgable folks do not wish to get mixed up in the field of gravity research.' Rickles adds that Trimble bemoans that most of those working on gravitation are 'madmen and quacks'. Source: Trimble to Bryce DeWitt, 10 June 1955, quoted by Dean Rickles
- **Tier 2 named person on the record.** Trimble and the Glenn L. Martin Company bought a Founder's Membership in the Institute of Field Physics for $5,000 and offered to help solicit further funding. Source: Dean Rickles, 'The Chapel Hill Conference in Context'
- **Tier 2 named person on the record.** Trimble described the body he proposed as an 'industrial version of the Institute for Advanced Study'. The Glenn L. Martin Company's institute was 'industry-sponsored, and not ensconced in a university', while the Institute of Field Physics was deliberately placed inside the University of North Carolina 'so as to avoid the conflict that physicists felt working in an industrial setting.' Source: Dean Rickles, 'The Chapel Hill Conference in Context'

## Gerald Anderson

Gerald Anderson is a person on the board; board tier C, contextual, real and relevant, no direct programme linkage on the record yet; dates 1990; axes C2 A0 D0; 3 connections.

In one line: Claimed first-hand child witness, Plains of San Agustin, 1947.

### The longer account

Gerald Anderson came forward in 1990, after a television broadcast, claiming that as a five-year-old he had been rockhounding with his family on the Plains of San Agustin in July 1947 and had walked up to a crashed disc with dead and one living occupant. He was the only claimed first-hand witness to the San Agustin site and was accordingly promoted hard by Stanton Friedman and Don Berliner in 'Crash at Corona' (1992). The case against him was made not by the Air Force but by other ufologists: Mark Rodeghier of CUFOS and Fred Whiting of the Fund for UFO Research produced a joint report, 'The Plains of San Agustin Controversy', which established among other things that a telephone bill Anderson submitted as corroboration had been altered. His own quoted words in the Air Force's 1997 report - 'I thought they were plastic dolls' - and his descriptions of the recovery procedures were read by the Air Force as accurate reporting of an anthropomorphic dummy balloon launch and recovery, of which numerous examples were flown and recovered in the region. Anderson matters to this board as the field's most instructive self-correction: two civilian research organisations audited their own star witness and published the result against interest.

### Where the record conflicts

- Friedman continued to defend elements of Anderson's account after the CUFOS/FUFOR report; Randle rejected it.

### Recorded as unverified

- The precise findings of the Rodeghier-Whiting document have not been read in the original here, only as cited by the Air Force report.

### Why it holds its tier

Re-graded C on a global pass (rank 665 of 1057, score 24). 3 connections, 1 of them custody/witness/funding/programmatic. Axes C2/A0/D0.

### The claims on the record

- **Tier 1 documented.** Anderson's interview transcript is reproduced at Appendix C, page 187, of the US Air Force's 1997 report. Source: The Roswell Report: Case Closed (1997), Appendix C
- **Tier 1 documented, allegation shaped wording as stored.** The USAF's 'Case Closed' prints Gerald Anderson's interview in full as Appendix C, beginning at page 187, noting it was 'excerpted in their entirety from unedited interviews used to prepare the video, Recollections of Roswell, Part II (1993)' and appears courtesy of the Fund for UFO Research. The Air Force also quotes him directly on the alleged bodies: 'I thought they were plastic dolls.' Source: Headquarters USAF, 'The Roswell Report: Case Closed' (1997), Appendix C https://archive.org/details/DTIC_ADA326147
- **Tier 2 named person on the record.** Anderson described the bodies as 'I thought they were plastic dolls' and described recovery procedures the Air Force found consistent with high-altitude balloon and anthropomorphic dummy launch and recovery. Source: The Roswell Report: Case Closed (1997), Section 1.4 and Conclusion
- **Tier 2 named person on the record.** Mark Rodeghier (CUFOS) and Fred Whiting (Fund for UFO Research) published a joint investigation, 'The Plains of San Agustin Controversy', concluding against Anderson's reliability. Source: Rodeghier and Whiting, 'The Plains of San Agustin Controversy' (1992), cited at note 123 of the 1997 Air Force report

### Its strongest connections

- Gerald Anderson runs out to **Plains of San Agustin claim, July 1947** (event, board tier B), a witness-chain link at Tier 2 named person on the record. The link reads: Sole claimed first-hand witness to the San Agustin site. Anderson came forward in 1990; the Air Force's 1997 report identifies him as the one first-hand testimony among the three supporting the claim. Sources: The Roswell Report: Case Closed (1997), https://archive.org/details/AFD-101027-030, dated 1997-01-01.
- Gerald Anderson runs out to **J. Allen Hynek Center for UFO Studies (CUFOS)** (institution, board tier C), a investigative link at Tier 2 named person on the record. The link reads: CUFOS and FUFOR investigated and discredited their own witness. ## Glenn T. Seaborg

Glenn T. Seaborg is a person on the board; board tier B, documented channel and capability, programme role indicated but not yet nailed to a primary source; dates 1961-01-16; axes C2 A3 D1; 3 connections.

In one line: Chairman, United States Atomic Energy Commission, named 16 January 1961, confirmed 24 February 1961.

### The longer account

The board's own framing calls the programme's security apparatus one taken from the Manhattan Project and the Atomic Energy Commission, and it has never named a single AEC chairman. Seaborg is the one the corpus reaches, and the records that name him in the office also carry the structural fact the board should want: the CIA's declassified chronology records him named AEC chairman on 16 January 1961 succeeding John A. McCone, and McCone became Director of Central Intelligence, so two years later Seaborg is writing on Commission letterhead to the same man at CIA. That 20 March 1963 letter transmits the Commission's Annual Report to Congress for 1962 and points the DCI at its fallout appendix. The end of the chairmanship is not established by any record opened for this pass and is left off this node deliberately.

### Recorded as unverified

- The end of the chairmanship. The triage row gives 1961 to 1971 and no record opened for this pass gives an end date. An AEC annual report to Congress was not opened either; the 1963 letter names and describes the 1962 report but is not that report.

### Why it holds its tier

Re-graded B on a global pass (rank 322 of 1057, score 36). 3 connections, 0 of them custody/witness/funding/programmatic. Axes C2/A3/D1.

### The claims on the record

- **Tier 1 documented.** A second, earlier instance of the office runs the other way. On 25 October 1961 the Director of Central Intelligence wrote to 'The Honorable Glenn T. Seaborg, Chairman, Atomic Energy Commission', thanking him for his impressions of a conference in Vienna and saying 'I am showing your good report to a few of our interested people.' It is signed 'Allen W. Dulles, Director'. Source: CIA reading room document CIA-RDP80B01676R000700150004-7, 25 October 1961, https://archive.org/details/cia-readingroom-document-cia-rdp80b01676r000700150004-7
- **Tier 1 documented.** He is in the office on Commission letterhead in 1963, writing to the man he succeeded at the AEC, now at CIA: 'It is with pleasure that I forward to you a copy of the Commission's Annual Report to Congress for 1962', signed 'Glenn T. Seaborg, Chairman', addressed to 'Honorable John A. McCone, Director, Central Intelligence Agency'. Source: CIA reading room document CIA-RDP80B01676R002800020014-7, 20 March 1963, https://archive.org/details/cia-readingroom-document-cia-rdp80b01676r002800020014-7
- **Tier 1 documented.** The CIA's own declassified chronology dates the appointment and its confirmation: '16 JAN. Dr. Glenn T. Seaborg named AEC chairman, succeeding John A. McCone (confirmed by Senate 24 Feb).' The entry sits under the volume's period heading for January 1961. Source: CIA Historical Staff Chronology 1946-65, Volume II 1956-65, June 1970, CIA-RDP85B00803R000200050003-5, declassified in part 8 July 2013, https://archive.org/details/cia-readingroom-document-cia-rdp85b00803r000200050003-5
- **Tier 1 documented.** The same letter points the Director of Central Intelligence at the report's fallout material: 'In connection with the Military Applications section, you may find Appendix 15--Environmental Contamination from Nuclear Explosives--of interest, since it concerns fallout data obtained from recent nuclear detonations.' Source: CIA reading room document CIA-RDP80B01676R002800020014-7, 20 March 1963, https://archive.org/details/cia-readingroom-document-cia-rdp80b01676r002800020014-7
- **Tier 2 named person on the record.** On the Danny Jones Podcast #181, 16 April 2023, at 3:24, Jeremy Rys cited Seaborg's island-of-stability prediction for superheavy elements against the claim that Bob Lazar predicted element 115. The publication he names was not opened for this node, and the lead map for that episode gives its date inconsistently as May 1969 in one row and May 1989 in another. Nothing here resolves it. Source: Danny Jones Podcast #181, 'Explosive New Bob Lazar Evidence & Area 51 Secrets Revealed', 16 April 2023, at 3:24 to 4:16

## Gregory M. Guillot

Gregory M. Guillot is a person on the board; board tier C, contextual, real and relevant, no direct programme linkage on the record yet; dates 2024-10-30; axes C0 A3 D2; 3 connections.

In one line: General, USAF; Commander, NORAD and US Northern Command (Feb 2024- ).

### The longer account

Guillot is the four-star who had to say out loud that the United States cannot identify what is flying over its own nuclear-capable bases. He took command of NORAD and US Northern Command in February 2024 and immediately made counter-unmanned-systems the centrepiece of his 90-day assessment, with the December 2023 Langley Air Force Base incursions as the driving case. Speaking to The War Zone on 30 October 2024 he conceded the limits of what he could say - 'The only thing I can tell you about the Langley drones is roughly the number and roughly the altitude' - and, on the question everyone wanted answered, 'I have not seen [and] I do not know of any organized or unorganized foreign nexus.' NORAD's own figure at that point was more than 600 drone reports over US military installations since 2022. By March 2026, after waves of incursions over Barksdale Air Force Base and other strategic installations following the launch of Operation Epic Fury, Guillot told reporters 'We've seen an increase from last year in the number of detections over military installations,' with roughly a quarter of detected drones now defeated versus almost none previously. Read from the board's premise, Guillot is the honest institutional face of a real gap: the command responsible for homeland air defence is describing an unattributed, persistent, sensor-resistant intrusion campaign against the continental United States and cannot name the actor.

### Where the record conflicts

- Guillot's 'no foreign nexus' finding sits against the DHS/FBI/DoD/FAA joint conclusion of 17 December 2024 that the New Jersey sightings were 'commercial drones, hobbyist drones, and law enforcement drones' - an explanation that does not account for objects roughly 20 feet long at 100 mph over a fighter base for seventeen nights.

### Recorded as unverified

- The classified annexes of Guillot's 90-day counter-UAS assessment.
- NORAD's per-installation incursion counts for 2025 and 2026.

Affiliations recorded: US Air Force, Commander, NORAD and USNORTHCOM (Feb 2024- ), Deputy Commander, US Central Command (prior).

## John F. Reed

John F. Reed is a person on the board; board tier B, documented channel and capability, programme role indicated but not yet nailed to a primary source; dates 2023-07-27; axes C3 A2 D3; 3 connections.

In one line: D-RI; SASC Chairman 2021-2025; sponsor of S. 2226.

### The longer account

Reed is the single most continuously entitled person on this board. He has held a 10 U.S.C. 119(e)(2) seat on the Senate Armed Services Committee across every one of the load-bearing moments the board tracks after 2009: he was ranking member in 2020 when the Unidentified Aerial Phenomena Task Force was stood up, chairman in 2023 when David Grusch testified, and ranking member again in August 2026. In each configuration he was entitled to receive waived special access program information and the Secretary's justification for the waiver, jointly with his opposite number. He also sits on the Senate Appropriations Committee and its Defense Subcommittee, though membership on those bodies confers nothing under section 119 - only the chair and ranking seats do. Reed's importance to the board is that he is the continuity: where chairmanships rotated with party control, Reed occupied one of the two Senate armed services seats without interruption. If a waived DoD special access program concerning recovered non-human technology existed and was notified under 119(e)(2) at any point between 2015 and 2026, Reed is the person most likely to have received that notification. He has made no public statement to that effect, and no public statement denying it. That silence is the expected behaviour of someone lawfully constrained by the statute, and it is not evidence either way - but it does mean the board should treat him as a node of known entitlement rather than known knowledge.

Affiliations recorded: U.S. Senate, Rhode Island (1997- ), Chairman, Senate Armed Services Committee (2021-2025); Ranking Member (2025- ).

## James R. Clapper Jr.

James R. Clapper Jr. is a person on the board; board tier B, documented channel and capability, programme role indicated but not yet nailed to a primary source; dates 2009-11-17; axes C1 A3 D0; 3 connections.

In one line: Under Secretary of Defense for Intelligence, 2007-2010; later DNI 2010-2017.

### The longer account

Clapper is the official who killed the Reid SAP request, and the fact that this decision sat with him rather than with DIA alone is the most underused fact in the AAWSAP record. The final packet presented to Deputy Secretary of Defense William Lynn III on 17 November 2009 carries Clapper, as Under Secretary of Defense for Intelligence, recommending against establishing a Special Access Program at that time, on the stated basis that the FY09 deliverables and anticipated FY10 work did not justify it. Seven months later he became Director of National Intelligence, a post he held until January 2017, meaning the man who signed off on refusing SAP protection to the UAP program then ran the entire intelligence community through the period in which, per the whistleblower record, the recovery and reverse-engineering effort was being kept outside congressional reporting. Clapper has spoken publicly about UAP since 2017 in general terms and has never been asked on the record about the memorandum bearing his recommendation. The board records this as a decision node: a USD(I) told a Deputy Secretary that the deliverables from a program the Senate Majority Leader wanted compartmented were not sensitive enough to compartment.

- Whether Clapper was briefed on any material beyond the DIRD deliverables when he made the recommendation.

- **Tier 1 documented.** The supporting DIA memorandum of 13 November 2009 found 'insufficient grounds to classify this open program' and stated no report contained information 'sufficiently sensitive and vulnerable to require extra protection'. Source: DIA memo U-09-2660CE-IM, 13 November 2009

## James Jesus Angleton

James Jesus Angleton is a person on the board; board tier B, documented channel and capability, programme role indicated but not yet nailed to a primary source; dates 1955-12-07; axes C2 A3 D2; 3 connections.

In one line: Chief, CIA Counterintelligence Staff, 1954-1974; ran the covert mail programme 1955-1973.

### The longer account

Angleton was Chief of the CIA's Counterintelligence Staff from 1954 to 1974 and, on the Church Committee's finding, in charge of the Agency's covert mail programme from 1955 until its termination in 1973. In November 1955 he proposed moving the New York mail intercept from photographing envelopes to opening them, indexing and analysing the product, and shifting primary responsibility for the project from the Office of Security to his own staff. The Committee found that the project was never processed through the Agency's approval system, had no separate funds, and in twenty years was reviewed by disinterested components twice, both reviews' recommendations going unimplemented. Angleton's own explanation was that normal processing would have opened the activity to too many people, and that the arrangement short-circuited the project approval process. He is carried here as a documented precedent for records and activities held outside an agency's own approval and review machinery, and for nothing else: nothing in the Church Committee record connects him to any UFO-related holding.

### Recorded as unverified

- Any connection to a UFO-related holding or registry. The word 'registry' appears nowhere in Church Committee Book III, and nothing in that record connects him to this board's subject.

### Why it holds its tier

Re-graded B on a global pass (rank 272 of 1057, score 39). 3 connections, 0 of them custody/witness/funding/programmatic. Axes C2/A3/D2.

### The claims on the record

- **Tier 1 documented.** Angleton's own stated reason for the exemption was the number of people normal processing would have exposed: a typical project would need sign-off by many components and a project review board, giving 'this tremendous opening up of the activity to a great number of people. ... That is the reason why I think it was excepted from [the usual approval system], and that way it shortcircuited the normal project approval process.' Source: S. Rept. 94-755, Book III, quoting Angleton testimony of 17 September 1975, https://www.intelligence.senate.gov/wp-content/uploads/2024/08/sites-default-files-94755-iii.pdf
- **Tier 1 documented.** Asked in public session why the Agency continued the mail programme after the President revoked the authority, Angleton answered: 'I have no satisfactory answer or that.' Source: S. Rept. 94-755, Book III, public hearing exchange with Senator Church, https://www.intelligence.senate.gov/wp-content/uploads/2024/08/sites-default-files-94755-iii.pdf
- **Tier 1 documented.** He proposed the change from photographing mail to opening it and the transfer of the project to his own staff. 'In November 1955, James Angleton, the Chief of the Counterintelligence (CI) Staff, submitted a proposal to Richard Helms for the further expansion of the New York mail intercept project', recommending access to 'all mail traffic to and from the U.S.S.R. which enters, departs, or transits the United States through the Port of New York' and that 'primary responsibility for the project be transferred within the Plans Directorate from 0/S to the CI Staff.' Helms approved it on 7 December 1955. Source: S. Rept. 94-755, Book III, https://www.intelligence.senate.gov/wp-content/uploads/2024/08/sites-default-files-94755-iii.pdf
- **Tier 1 documented.** The Committee states his office and its dates: 'James Angleton, who served as Chief of the CIA Counterintelligence Staff from 1954 to 1974 and was in charge of the CIA covert mail program from 1955 to its termination in 1973.' Source: S. Rept. 94-755, Book III, Supplementary Detailed Staff Reports on Intelligence Activities and the Rights of Americans, Senate Select Committee to Study Governmental Operations with Respect to Intelligence Activities, 1976, section on the Huston Plan, https://www.intelligence.senate.gov/wp-content/uploads/2024/08/sites-default-files-94755-iii.pdf
- **Tier 1 documented.** The project sat outside the Agency's own approval and review machinery: it 'was never processed through the approval system and has no separate funds ... the normal programming procedures have not been followed for the project as a whole', and the Committee concluded that 'The security of the operation was enhanced by this exemption but the opportunity for critical evaluation by disinterested division heads was lost.' Source: S. Rept. 94-755, Book III, https://www.intelligence.senate.gov/wp-content/uploads/2024/08/sites-default-files-94755-iii.pdf

## James V. Forrestal

James V. Forrestal is a person on the board; board tier B, documented channel and capability, programme role indicated but not yet nailed to a primary source; dates 1949; axes C3 A2 D1; 3 connections.

In one line: Secretary of the Navy until September 1947; first Secretary of Defense, 17 September 1947 to 28 March 1949.

### The longer account

James V. Forrestal was Secretary of the Navy until September 1947 and then the first Secretary of Defense, from 17 September 1947 to 28 March 1949, under the National Security Act of 1947 that created the office. He reaches this board as the addressee of the memorandum the whole MJ-12 corpus is built on and as the subject of a recurring claim that his death was concealed. Two things about him are documented and one is not. Documented: the office and its dates, and the statutory requirement that its holder be a civilian, which contradicts the 1987 document's repeated description of him as 'General James V. Forestall'. Not documented from here: the United States Navy board of investigation into his death of 1949, the Willcutts Report, which could not be opened at the Internet Archive, in the National Archives catalogue, or through the university finding aid that a search named; the claim that his medical records are sealed is therefore neither confirmed nor refuted on this board.

### Recorded as unverified

- The Willcutts Report, the United States Navy board of investigation into his death in 1949, and with it the claim that his medical records are sealed. It is not in the Internet Archive catalogue and not findable in the National Archives catalogue under 'Willcutts'.
- That Forrestal was hearing an account of recovered bodies from the head of the Air Force shortly before his hospitalisation. Michels prefaced it with 'I don't know if this is right' and named no document.

### The claims on the record

- **Tier 1 documented.** His tenure is dated to the day in the Department of State's own editorial apparatus: 'Forrestal, James V., Secretary of the Navy until September 1947; Secretary of Defense, September 17, 1947-March 28, 1949.' Source: Persons, FRUS, Emergence of the Intelligence Establishment, 1945-1950, https://history.state.gov/historicaldocuments/frus1945-50Intel/persons
- **Tier 1 documented.** The office he held first was created by statute, and its holder must be a civilian: 'There shall be a Secretary of Defense, who shall be appointed from civilian life by the President, by and with the advice and consent of the Senate: Provided, That a person who has within ten years been on active duty as a commissioned officer in a Regular component of the armed services shall not be eligible for appointment as Secretary of Defense.' - **Tier 1 documented.** The same section makes the office the President's principal national-security assistant, with duties to 'Establish general policies and programs for the National Military Establishment and for all of the departments and agencies therein' and to 'Exercise general direction, authority, and control over such departments and agencies'. Forestall who jumped to his death from a 16th story hospital window. General Forestall's medical records are sealed to this day.' Source: AREA52 - DEBRIEFED with Chris Ramsay, ep. 20, 3 January 2025, 0:12:27; National Security Act of 1947, 61 Stat. 495, sec. 202(a)

## Jeffrey Nuccetelli

Jeffrey Nuccetelli is a person on the board; board tier A, named in sworn testimony or primary documents as directly inside the programme; dates 2025-09-09; axes C1 A1 D2; 3 connections.

In one line: USAF veteran, security forces / military police, Vandenberg AFB 2003-2005.

### The longer account

Nuccetelli brought the base-security side of the observational record into the congressional record. In written testimony to the House Oversight Task Force on 9 September 2025 he described serving as military police at Vandenberg Air Force Base, home of the National Missile Defense project, and receiving reports on 14 October 2003 from Boeing contractors of 'a massive, glowing red square silently hovering over two missile defense sites.' The same night, security guards reported a bright fast-moving object and witnesses described 'a massive rectangular craft, larger than a football field.' A week later a patrol reported an object over the ocean that descended and 'vanished instantly into the night sky'; in 2005 a patrol reported 'a massive triangular craft, larger than a C-130, hovering low over the main base.' Two things make this testimony operationally significant rather than anecdotal. First, he states that 'Official Air Force records documenting it are now held by AARO and the FBI' - which is a specific, checkable custody claim that FOIA can be aimed at. Second, he states one witness 'was threatened and told to keep quiet about the incident,' which is the same suppression pattern the Navy witnesses describe, applied to a strategic missile installation.

### Recorded as unverified

- The Air Force security forces blotter entries and incident reports for Vandenberg AFB, 14 Oct 2003 and 2005, have not been released.
- The identity of the person who threatened the witness.
- AARO has not confirmed holding the Vandenberg records Nuccetelli says it holds.
- Whether AARO ever interviewed Nuccetelli or logged his account in its case holdings.
- Whether the Vandenberg security forces incident reports he describes still exist and whether the Air Force identified them as covered UAP records under the FY2024 NDAA.

Affiliations recorded: US Air Force security forces / military police, Vandenberg AFB (c. 2003-2005), U.S. Air Force (16 years, incl. Vandenberg AFB security forces), US Air Force (approx. 16 years), Vandenberg AFB security forces, US Air Force, Vandenberg Air Force Base, House Oversight Task Force on the Declassification of Federal Secrets.

## Dr. Joseph Cannon

Dr. Joseph Cannon is a person on the board; board tier B, documented channel and capability, programme role indicated but not yet nailed to a primary source; dates 2019-10-17; axes C2 A2 D0; 3 connections.

In one line: Government principal investigator, TTSA CRADA; U.S. Army Ground Vehicle Survivability and Protection.

### The longer account

Cannon is the named U.S. Government principal investigator on the To The Stars Academy CRADA, the point at which a private company claiming custody of material from a craft of unknown origin was formally connected to an Army laboratory. He is quoted in TTSA's 17 October 2019 announcement: 'Our partnership with TTSA serves as an exciting, non-traditional source for novel materials and transformational technologies.' Army public affairs subsequently distanced the service from the provenance question, with GVSP spokesman Doug Halleaux saying that because 'the Army's interest is in the potential for novel materials and exploring the edges of materials science, any speculation as to their origin is (pardon the pun) immaterial.' When John Greenewald sought Cannon's correspondence with TTSA, the Army located twenty-nine emails totalling one hundred pages and withheld all of them in full under FOIA exemptions (b)(4) and (b)(5).

### What is left unexplained

The Army withheld 100 pages of correspondence about a materials collaboration it publicly described as routine. Deliberative-process and trade-secret exemptions over an unclassified novel-materials review is an unusually heavy posture for a partnership characterised as immaterial to origin.

### Recorded as unverified

- What, if anything, the Army actually tested, and what the results were. No technical output from the CRADA has been released.

Affiliations recorded: U.S. Army Combat Capabilities Development Command, Ground Vehicle Systems Center / Ground Vehicle Survivability and Protection.

### Why it holds its tier

- **Tier 1 documented.** On 21 February 2020 CCDC FOIA Officer Joy L. Source: CCDC FOIA response to The Black Vault, 21 February 2020

## Kathleen H. Hicks

Kathleen H. Hicks is a person on the board; board tier C, contextual, real and relevant, no direct programme linkage on the record yet; dates 2022-07-15; axes C0 A3 D2; 3 connections.

In one line: Deputy Secretary of Defense (2021-2025); signed AOIMSG and AARO establishment memoranda.

### The longer account

Every structural decision about the official UAP apparatus between 2021 and 2024 carries Hicks's signature. As Deputy Secretary of Defense she directed the establishment of the Airborne Object Identification and Management Synchronization Group on 23 November 2021, an entity built to answer Congress's Section 1683 mandate without disturbing anything below it. When AOIMSG proved inadequate she signed the memorandum of 15 July 2022 - announced 20 July - establishing the All-domain Anomaly Resolution Office, disestablishing AOIMSG, ordering the Navy to shut down the UAP Task Force and hand its data and materials to AARO, and placing AARO under the Under Secretary of Defense for Intelligence and Security. In late July and August 2023, with Grusch's testimony fresh, she pulled AARO out of the USD(I&S) chain and made its director report directly to her, saying the Department takes UAP seriously because they are a potential national security threat and a safety risk. That re-wiring is the most consequential organisational fact in the domain: it put the office that adjudicates whistleblower claims about concealed special access programmes directly under the deputy head of the department those programmes would be concealed within. Notably, Hicks did not publicly endorse the central findings of the Historical Record Report Volume I when it was released in March 2024, a silence Christopher Mellon flagged at the time - the report's assertions were made at the AARO level, never ratified at cabinet level.

### Recorded as unverified

- Whether the July/August 2023 reporting-chain change was prompted specifically by the Grusch testimony of 26 July 2023.

Affiliations recorded: Deputy Secretary of Defense (2021-2025), Center for Strategic and International Studies, OSD Policy (Obama administration).

### Why it holds its tier

Re-graded C on a global pass (rank 658 of 1057, score 24). 3 connections, 0 of them custody/witness/funding/programmatic. Axes C0/A3/D2.

### The claims on the record

- **Tier 1 documented.** Hicks, as Deputy Secretary of Defense, signed the 15 July 2022 memorandum establishing AARO, superseding the 23 November 2021 AOIMSG direction and ordering the Navy to disestablish the UAPTF. Source: DoD memorandum, Establishment of the All-domain Anomaly Resolution Office, 15 July 2022
- **Tier 1 documented.** In summer 2023 Hicks took direct oversight of AARO, repositioning its director to report to her rather than through USD(I&S). Source: DefenseScoop, 30 August 2023
- **Tier 2 named person on the record.** Hicks declined to publicly endorse the key findings of Historical Record Report Volume I. Source: Analysis by Douglas Dean Johnson; contemporaneous comment by Christopher Mellon, March 2024
